# Signal MD mentor-page drafts: topics 1251-1500

Status of every entry: `draft_pending_clinician_review` unless marked `needs_evidence`. Nothing has been written to the database.
Every teaching point cites PMIDs whose abstracts were read; "Gaps" lists what the abstracts do not state and must be added or confirmed by the reviewer.

## Cluster `osteoporosis` (topics 1251, 1252, 1253) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Osteoporosis guidelines base treatment on fracture risk assessment (FRAX and DXA) and prior fragility fracture, with national guidelines now naming imminent or very high risk and supporting early anabolic therapy. Under-treatment after fracture remains a major gap.

**Teaching points**
- The 2024 Australian guideline recommends FRAX-based risk stratification, clarifies imminent and very high fracture risk, addresses rebound vertebral fractures after denosumab cessation, and supports early osteoanabolic therapy. [PMID 40134107]
- The 2024 UK guideline (NOGG), endorsed by IOF and ESCEO, covers prevention and treatment of osteoporosis, and a 2026 paper discusses its application in China. [PMID 40921943, 42642185]
- The German guideline notes 71% of people with osteoporosis in Germany remain untreated and stresses accurate diagnosis and interdisciplinary care. [PMID 39803994]
- The Dutch multidisciplinary guideline provides flowcharts to support diagnosis and treatment for fracture prevention in daily practice. [PMID 38564062]
- The ECTS 2024 recommendations give pragmatic advice on preventing and treating glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis. [PMID 39556468]
- In older adults, optimising osteoporosis management reduces fracture incidence, morbidity and loss of quality of life. [PMID 41146827]

**Uncertainties**
- Intervention thresholds differ between national guidelines.

**MCQ angles**
- Treat after a fragility fracture; consider anabolic first for very high risk.
- Risk of vertebral fractures after stopping denosumab.

**Case hooks**
- 72-year-old woman with a vertebral fracture and T-score -2.4.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** DXA T-score definitions, FRAX thresholds and bisphosphonate duration and drug holidays are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38564062: The Dutch multidisciplinary guideline osteoporosis and fracture prevention, taking a local guideline to the international arena (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39556468: Prevention and treatment of glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis in adults: recommendations from the European Calcified Tissue Society (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39803994: Clinical Practice Guideline: The Diagnosis and Treatment of Osteoporosis (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40134107: 2024 Royal Australian College of General Practitioners and Healthy Bones Australia guideline for osteoporosis management and fracture prevention in postmenopausal women and men over 50 years of age (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40921943: The 2024 UK clinical guideline for the prevention and treatment of osteoporosis (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41146827: Bone Health and Osteoporosis Management in the Elderly: A Review of Guidelines and Orthopedic Implications (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42642185: [Interpretation of 2024 United Kingdom National Osteoporosis Guideline Group (NOGG) Clinical Guidelines: Prevention and Treatment of Osteoporosis] (guideline, 2026)

Existing links for #1251 screened by title: papers kept 0, dropped 3; guideline sources kept 2, dropped 0.

## Cluster `otitis` (topics 1254) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Acute otitis media in children is a common antibiotic prescribing problem; consensus guidance seeks targeted antibiotic use, pneumococcal vaccination reduces recurrence, and chronic suppurative disease is managed topically.

**Teaching points**
- An Italian intersociety consensus addresses antibiotic treatment of acute and recurrent otitis media in children, and stewardship work shows room to improve antibiotic use. [PMID 39980042, 42699456]
- Pneumococcal conjugate vaccine reduced tympanostomy tube insertion and recurrent acute otitis media, with a non-significant effect on otitis media with effusion and unclear effect on mastoiditis. [PMID 36924215]
- In chronic suppurative otitis media, adding systemic antibiotics to ciprofloxacin ear drops made little difference to ear discharge resolution at one to two weeks (low-certainty evidence). [PMID 40484405]
- A German S2k guideline reserves fluoroquinolones, cephalosporins (other than group 1), clindamycin and carbapenems for well-founded exceptional cases in ENT infections. [PMID 42230387]
- The ACR Appropriateness Criteria address imaging in inflammatory ear disease, and an expert consensus standardises terminology for acute ear discharge in children. [PMID 40409884, 40769763]

**Uncertainties**
- Which children benefit from immediate antibiotics versus observation.

**MCQ angles**
- Pneumococcal vaccination and recurrent otitis media.
- Topical therapy is first line in chronic suppurative otitis media.

**Case hooks**
- Child with fever, otalgia and bulging tympanic membrane.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, watchful waiting rules, otitis externa treatment and mastoiditis management are not in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36924215: Efficacy of Pneumococcal Vaccine on Otitis Media: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39980042: Antibiotic treatment of acute and recurrent otitis media in children: an Italian intersociety Consensus (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40409884: ACR Appropriateness Criteria® Inflammatory Ear Disease (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40484405: Systemic antibiotics for chronic suppurative otitis media (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40769763: Defining the Terminology for Acute Ear Discharge in Children: Systematic Review and Expert Consensus Using the Nominal Group Technique (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42230387: [German S2k guideline on antibiotic therapy of ENT infections : The most important information in brief] (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42699456: Improving Guideline-concordant Antibiotic Use for Acute Otitis Media in a Pediatric Primary Care Network (guideline, 2026)

Existing links for #1254 screened by title: papers kept 3, dropped 0; guideline sources kept 0, dropped 0.

## Cluster `outpatient_diabetes` (topics 1255) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Outpatient diabetes control now sits within a cardiovascular-kidney-metabolic framework, with guideline-recommended drugs still under-used in practice.

**Teaching points**
- The 2026 AHA/ACC/ADA/ASN guideline addresses prevention, detection, evaluation and management of cardiovascular-kidney-metabolic syndrome for cardiology, endocrinology, nephrology and primary care. [PMID 42265997]
- In Belgian primary care, use of guideline-recommended type 2 diabetes medicines increased but a substantial gap in optimal use remains. [PMID 39487060]
- An Indian guideline for hypertension in type 2 diabetes prefers ARB and dihydropyridine CCB combinations over beta-blockers and thiazides. [PMID 39163066]
- A 2025 guideline update treats obesity pharmacotherapy as an integral part of treatment for people with obesity, and a 2026 guideline recommends pharmacologic therapy alongside lifestyle modification in outpatient adults with overweight or obesity. [PMID 40789597, 42296496]
- The Endocrine Society/ESE guideline on pre-existing diabetes in pregnancy links preconception care with better first-trimester HbA1c and lower rates of congenital malformations. [PMID 40652453]

**Uncertainties**
- Individual HbA1c targets are not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Choosing second-line therapy by comorbidity (CVD, CKD, heart failure).
- Pre-pregnancy counselling in diabetes.

**Case hooks**
- Type 2 diabetic with HbA1c 8.4% on metformin and eGFR 50.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** HbA1c targets, monitoring schedule, screening for complications and specific drug sequencing are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39163066: Management of Hypertension in Patients with Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus: Indian Guideline 2024 by Association of Physicians of India and Indian College of Physicians (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39487060: Trends in type 2 diabetes medication use and guideline adherence in Belgian primary care (2019-2023) (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40652453: Preexisting Diabetes and Pregnancy: An Endocrine Society and European Society of Endocrinology Joint Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40789597: Pharmacotherapy for obesity management in adults: 2025 clinical practice guideline update (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42265997: 2026 AHA/ACC/ADA/ASN Guideline for the Prevention, Detection, Evaluation, and Management of Cardiovascular-Kidney-Metabolic Syndrome: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42296496: Pharmacologic Treatments With Lifestyle Modifications in Nonpregnant Adults With Overweight or Obesity in Outpatient Settings: A Living Clinical Guideline From the American College of Physicians (April 2026) (guideline, 2026)

Existing links for #1255 screened by title: papers kept 6, dropped 2; guideline sources kept 3, dropped 1.

## Cluster `opat` (topics 1256, 1257) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy is a safe, effective and cost-effective alternative to inpatient care when delivered by services with infection specialist oversight, monitoring and stewardship.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials suggests OPAT is a safe and effective alternative to inpatient treatment, though more RCTs are needed. [PMID 38960209]
- The 2018 IDSA OPAT guideline covers service organisation, monitoring and stewardship but not treatment of specific infections. [PMID 30423035]
- UK good practice recommendations (2019, updated 2026) act as quality indicators for OPAT services in adults and children regardless of setting or service model. [PMID 34222901, 42028542]
- German practice guidelines conclude OPAT is a safe, cost-effective alternative to inpatient care requiring infectious diseases expertise and antimicrobial stewardship. [PMID 40844698]
- A systematic review proposes a tool for optimised antibiotic prescribing with elastomeric infusion devices in the community. [PMID 33576336]

**Uncertainties**
- Patient selection criteria vary by service.

**MCQ angles**
- Who is suitable for OPAT: stable, reliable access and monitoring.
- Line-related complications of home IV therapy.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with osteomyelitis needing 6 weeks of IV antibiotics.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Eligibility checklists, monitoring bloods schedules and access device choice are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30423035: 2018 Infectious Diseases Society of America Clinical Practice Guideline for the Management of Outpatient Parenteral Antimicrobial Therapy (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 33576336: Home intravenous antibiotherapy and the proper use of elastomeric pumps: Systematic review of the literature and proposals for improved use (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34222901: Updated good practice recommendations for outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy (OPAT) in adults and children in the UK (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 38960209: Safety and efficacy of outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized clinical trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40844698: Practice guidelines for outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy (OPAT) in Germany (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42028542: 2026 Updated good practice recommendations for outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy (OPAT) in adults and children in the UK (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `ovarian_cancer` (topics 1258, 1259) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Advanced ovarian cancer care combines cytoreduction with platinum chemotherapy and biomarker-driven maintenance. PARP inhibitor choice follows mutation status and balances benefit against toxicity.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of PARP inhibitor maintenance after first-line chemotherapy found risk ratios for high-grade adverse events ranging from 1.15 for veliparib to 4.73 for niraparib. [PMID 41191357]
- Tumour mutational status is a key biomarker and clinician perception of early progression risk drives choice of maintenance treatment in advanced ovarian cancer. [PMID 39448084]
- A British consensus updates recommendations for genetic testing in epithelial ovarian cancer with audit standards. [PMID 39222974]
- The ASCO guideline update addresses neoadjuvant chemotherapy for newly diagnosed advanced ovarian cancer, and an ASCO living guideline addresses systemic treatment of recurrence. [PMID 39841949, 42441925]
- SEOM-GEICO and Pan-Asia ESMO guidelines summarise management strategies for epithelial ovarian cancer. [PMID 39008159, 40494042]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal maintenance for BRCA wild-type tumours.

**MCQ angles**
- BRCA and HRD status and PARP inhibitor benefit.
- Toxicities: anaemia and thrombocytopenia with niraparib.

**Case hooks**
- Stage IIIC high-grade serous carcinoma after interval debulking: maintenance choice.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Primary versus interval debulking trial data, CA125 follow-up and PARP inhibitor survival results are not in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39008159: SEOM-GEICO clinical guideline on epithelial ovarian cancer (2023) (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39222974: British Gynaecological Cancer Society/British Association of Gynaecological Pathology consensus for genetic testing in epithelial ovarian cancer in the United Kingdom (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39448084: Consensus on drivers of maintenance treatment choice and patterns of care in advanced ovarian cancer (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 39841949: Neoadjuvant Chemotherapy for Newly Diagnosed, Advanced Ovarian Cancer: ASCO Guideline Update (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40494042: Pan-Asia adapted ESMO Clinical Practice Guideline for the management of patients with newly diagnosed and relapsed epithelial ovarian cancer (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41191357: PARP Inhibitor Maintenance After First-Line Chemotherapy in Advanced-Stage Epithelial Ovarian Cancer: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 42441925: Systemic Treatment of Ovarian Cancer Recurrence: ASCO Living Guideline, Version 2026.1.0 (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `oxalate_nephropathy` (topics 1260) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Oxalate nephropathy results from primary hyperoxaluria or enteric hyperoxaluria. RNA interference therapy is changing management of primary disease, with transplant decisions made case by case.

**Teaching points**
- The ERKNet/OxalEurope consensus gives recommendations on diagnosis and management of primary hyperoxaluria, including conventional therapy, dialysis, transplantation and new therapies. [PMID 36604599]
- RNA interference therapy for primary hyperoxaluria appears effective with acceptable safety, with better effect at higher dose and longer duration. [PMID 40207102]
- Current approaches favour isolated kidney transplantation with adjunctive siRNA therapy, considered case by case. [PMID 39917790]
- For enteric hyperoxaluria, evidence on factors driving adverse outcomes and long-term efficacy of treatments is lacking. [PMID 32900691]
- Evidence on Oxalobacter formigenes in primary hyperoxaluria is inconclusive. [PMID 39886625]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of liver-kidney transplant in the siRNA era.

**MCQ angles**
- Fat malabsorption leads to enteric hyperoxaluria and oxalate nephropathy.
- Hydration and low-oxalate diet.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with Crohn disease and ileal resection presenting with AKI and calcium oxalate crystals.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic urine oxalate thresholds, biopsy findings and conservative management are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32900691: Pathophysiology and Treatment of Enteric Hyperoxaluria (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 36604599: Clinical practice recommendations for primary hyperoxaluria: an expert consensus statement from ERKNet and OxalEurope (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 39886625: Efficacy and safety of <i>Oxalobacter formigenes</i> in patients with primary hyperoxaluria: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39917790: Management of patients with kidney stones (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40207102: The efficacy and safety of RNA interference for the treatment of primary hyperoxaluria: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

Existing links for #1260 screened by title: papers kept 4, dropped 1; guideline sources kept 3, dropped 0.

## Cluster `oxygen_targets` (topics 1261) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Oxygen is a drug to be prescribed with a target saturation. Guidelines for adults and children in acute care recommend setting targets on admission, avoiding hyperoxia, and using high-flow oxygen selectively.

**Teaching points**
- The AARC adult guideline addresses oxygen therapy in the acute care setting, including that high-flow or conventional oxygen may be used in immunocompromised patients and humidification should be considered when flows exceed 4 L/min. [PMID 34728574]
- The German S3 guideline is the first national guideline on oxygen use in acute out-of-hospital and in-hospital care of adults. [PMID 34933311]
- For children, no specific oxygen target can be recommended outside bronchiolitis, and establishing a patient- or disease-specific target on admission is best practice. [PMID 33790048]
- High-flow nasal cannula in infants with bronchiolitis may shorten the duration of oxygen therapy (low-certainty evidence). [PMID 38506440]
- The TSANZ home oxygen guideline stresses delivery devices, patient expectations and hazards of therapy. [PMID 39009413]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal target ranges are not given in the retrieved abstracts.

**MCQ angles**
- Different target saturations in COPD with hypercapnia versus other patients.
- Fire and burn hazards of oxygen.

**Case hooks**
- COPD patient with SpO2 84% on arrival: initial oxygen prescription.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Specific target ranges (94-98% general, 88-92% at risk of hypercapnia) are standard but not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33790048: AARC Clinical Practice Guideline: Management of Pediatric Patients With Oxygen in the Acute Care Setting (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34728574: AARC Clinical Practice Guideline: Management of Adult Patients With Oxygen in the Acute Care Setting (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 34933311: German S3 Guideline: Oxygen Therapy in the Acute Care of Adult Patients (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 38506440: High-flow nasal cannula therapy for infants with bronchiolitis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39009413: Thoracic Society of Australia and New Zealand clinical practice guideline on adult home oxygen therapy (guideline, 2024)

Existing links for #1261 screened by title: papers kept 2, dropped 1; guideline sources kept 0, dropped 0.

## Cluster `pacemaker` (topics 1262) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pacemaker indications are set out in the 2021 ESC pacing and CRT guideline, with newer guidance on physiologic (conduction system) pacing, paediatric devices, device infection and cardioneuroablation.

**Teaching points**
- The 2023 HRS/APHRS/LAHRS guideline covers cardiac physiologic pacing for avoidance and mitigation of heart failure, including patient selection, implant, follow-up and use in children. [PMID 37283271]
- The 2021 ESC guidelines on cardiac pacing and cardiac resynchronisation therapy are endorsed for professionals caring for patients with an indication for pacing. [PMID 39836347]
- The new paediatric device guidelines downgraded some indications, including ventricular tachycardia and arrhythmic syncope, to class II. [PMID 38667717]
- An EHRA statement reviews cardioneuroablation for reflex syncope and functional bradyarrhythmias as an alternative to pacing. [PMID 39082698]
- Guidance on prevention of cardiac implantable device infection addresses gaps in existing guidelines. [PMID 41149277]

**Uncertainties**
- Selection between conventional, physiologic and leadless pacing.

**MCQ angles**
- Pacing in symptomatic sinus node dysfunction and high-grade AV block.
- Pacemaker-induced cardiomyopathy and conduction system pacing.

**Case hooks**
- Elderly patient with syncope and complete heart block.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Class I indications for sinus node dysfunction and AV block, NICE criteria and pacing mode selection are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37283271: 2023 HRS/APHRS/LAHRS guideline on cardiac physiologic pacing for the avoidance and mitigation of heart failure (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38667717: New Guidelines of Pediatric Cardiac Implantable Electronic Devices: What Is Changing in Clinical Practice? (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39082698: Cardioneuroablation for the treatment of reflex syncope and functional bradyarrhythmias: A Scientific Statement of the European Heart Rhythm Association (EHRA) of the ESC, the Heart Rhythm Society (HRS), the Asia Pacific Heart Rhythm Society (APHRS) and the Latin American Heart Rhythm Society (LAHRS) (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39836347: 2021 European Society of Cardiology guidelines on cardiac pacing and cardiac resynchronisation therapy : Statement of endorsement by the NVVC (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41149277: Addressing Complications in Cardiac Implantable Electronic Devices: A Guideline to Prevention of CIED Infection (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `paeds_anaphylaxis` (topics 1263) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Anaphylaxis in children is treated with prompt intramuscular adrenaline, supported by written emergency action plans and education of children and carers. Local evidence-based pathways can safely reduce admissions.

**Teaching points**
- First-aid management of anaphylaxis includes epinephrine, a written personalised emergency action plan, and education of children and supervising adults on recognition and first aid. [PMID 28193791]
- Updated Australasian guidance keeps the recommendation to use a 0.3 mg adrenaline injector device for children over 20 kg and restates weight-based dosing. [PMID 34211247]
- A meta-analysis of community epinephrine use supports public education on recognising anaphylaxis and giving prompt epinephrine to avoid the rare risk of fatality. [PMID 33549844]
- A local evidence-based guideline with outcome monitoring safely reduced hospitalisations for paediatric anaphylaxis. [PMID 35059724]
- The 2023 anaphylaxis practice parameter update addresses management including anaesthesia-related situations through shared decision-making. [PMID 38108678]

**Uncertainties**
- Observation period after adrenaline is not covered in the abstracts.

**MCQ angles**
- Intramuscular adrenaline is first-line; antihistamines are adjuncts.
- Auto-injector dose by weight.

**Case hooks**
- Child with urticaria, wheeze and hypotension minutes after eating peanut.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Recognition criteria, dose per kg, repeat dosing and biphasic reaction observation are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28193791: Epinephrine for First-aid Management of Anaphylaxis (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 33549844: Community Use of Epinephrine for the Treatment of Anaphylaxis: A Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34211247: Updated anaphylaxis guidelines: management in infants and children (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 35059724: Safely Reducing Hospitalizations for Anaphylaxis in Children Through an Evidence-Based Guideline (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 38108678: Anaphylaxis: A 2023 practice parameter update (guideline, 2024)

Existing links for #1263 screened by title: papers kept 3, dropped 0; guideline sources kept 0, dropped 0.

## Cluster `paeds_asthma` (topics 1264) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Childhood asthma diagnosis relies on objective testing: the ERS recommends spirometry, bronchodilator reversibility and FeNO first line and advises against diagnosing from history alone. Guidance for severe exacerbations is limited and inconsistent.

**Teaching points**
- The ERS guidelines for children aged 5 to 16 recommend spirometry, bronchodilator reversibility testing and exhaled nitric oxide fraction as first-line diagnostic tests, and recommend against diagnosing asthma on history alone or after a single abnormal objective test. [PMID 33863747]
- A review of guidelines found acute paediatric asthma exacerbation guidance has substantial deficits in quality and provides limited, inconsistent advice for severe exacerbations; IV magnesium was the most recommended parenteral treatment. [PMID 38325912]
- The AARC/PALISI guideline suggests a dedicated protocol or pathway for managing children treated for critical asthma (conditional recommendation, low certainty). [PMID 40323974]
- The Emilia-Romagna group considers preschool wheezing an umbrella term for distinct phenotypes and gives management guidance for it. [PMID 36013002]
- Implementing a paediatric asthma guideline improved hospital-based care in one centre. [PMID 29367203]

**Uncertainties**
- Stepwise controller therapy is not covered in the retrieved abstracts.

**MCQ angles**
- Objective tests to confirm asthma in children.
- Preschool wheeze phenotypes.

**Case hooks**
- 7-year-old with recurrent night cough and wheeze after exercise.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Stepwise management (ICS, LTRA, LABA, biologics), inhaler technique and acute treatment doses are not stated in the retrieved abstracts; add from GINA or BTS/SIGN.

**Sources**
- PMID 29367203: Implementation and Improvement of Pediatric Asthma Guideline Improves Hospital-Based Care (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 33863747: European Respiratory Society clinical practice guidelines for the diagnosis of asthma in children aged 5-16 years (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 36013002: Management of Preschool Wheezing: Guideline from the Emilia-Romagna Asthma (ERA) Study Group (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 38325912: Analysis of guideline recommendations for treatment of asthma exacerbations in children: a Pediatric Emergency Research Networks (PERN) study (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40323974: AARC and PALISI Clinical Practice Guideline: Pediatric Critical Asthma (guideline, 2025)

Existing links for #1264 screened by title: papers kept 3, dropped 0; guideline sources kept 0, dropped 0.

## Cluster `paeds_dehydration` (topics 1265) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Paediatric dehydration is usually due to gastroenteritis and is managed with oral rehydration, avoiding unnecessary hospital admission and antibiotics. Retrieved evidence is limited.

**Teaching points**
- The 2024 German S2k guideline on acute gastroenteritis in children aims to avoid unnecessary hospitalisation and includes measures to prevent spread of infection. [PMID 39250962]
- A US outpatient guideline covers children older than six months with symptoms for less than seven days managed in the outpatient setting. [PMID 27268427]
- Chinese guidelines do not recommend antibiotics for acute infectious diarrhoea in children, except in specified circumstances such as chronic underlying disease. [PMID 30269306]
- German perioperative guidance for children notes crystalloids alone are insufficient in some situations and fluid responsiveness should be assessed. [PMID 27747968]

**Uncertainties**
- Rehydration route and volumes are not stated in the abstracts.

**MCQ angles**
- Oral rehydration solution is first line for mild to moderate dehydration.
- Antibiotics are rarely needed.

**Case hooks**
- Toddler with two days of diarrhoea, dry mucous membranes and reduced urine output.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Dehydration scoring, oral rehydration solution composition, nasogastric or IV fluid indications and ondansetron are not in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27268427: Clinical Practice Guideline for the Treatment of Pediatric Acute Gastroenteritis in the Outpatient Setting (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 27747968: Perioperative intravenous fluid therapy in children: guidelines from the Association of the Scientific Medical Societies in Germany (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 30269306: Chinese clinical practice guidelines for acute infectious diarrhea in children (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 39250962: S2k Leitlinie akute infektiöse Gastroenteritis im Säuglings-, Kindes- und Jugendalter – Update 2024 (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `paget` (topics 1266, 1267, 1268) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Paget disease of bone is monitored with alkaline phosphatase and treated with potent intravenous bisphosphonates such as zoledronate, which give prolonged biochemical remission; some experts now suggest treating most patients at diagnosis.

**Teaching points**
- An Italian position paper notes the safety and long-term efficacy of zoledronate and suggests treating most, if not all, cases at diagnosis. [PMID 38488978]
- A clinical practice guideline says biochemical markers rather than symptoms indicate relapse, with assessment every 1 to 2 years after zoledronate and every 6 to 12 months with less potent drugs. [PMID 27600563]
- The 2019 JBMR guideline identified a lack of research on patient-focused clinical outcomes in Paget disease and several areas for future research. [PMID 30803025]
- In a Cochrane review of bisphosphonates in adults, mild gastrointestinal adverse events occurred in 64% of treated and 48% of control participants (RR 1.32), with uncertain results for treatment discontinuation. [PMID 29192423]
- Epigenetic factors contribute to Paget disease pathogenesis and may provide diagnostic markers. [PMID 33929316]

**Uncertainties**
- Whether treating asymptomatic disease prevents complications.

**MCQ angles**
- Raised ALP with normal calcium and a lytic then sclerotic bone lesion.
- Zoledronate single infusion.

**Case hooks**
- Older man with bone pain, raised ALP and a thickened femur on X-ray.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Indications for treatment, zoledronate dose, imaging features and complications (deafness, sarcoma) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27600563: Diagnosis and treatment of Paget's disease of bone : A clinical practice guideline (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 29192423: Bisphosphonates for Paget's disease of bone in adults (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 30803025: Diagnosis and Management of Paget's Disease of Bone in Adults: A Clinical Guideline (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 33929316: Epigenetic analysis of Paget's disease of bone identifies differentially methylated loci that predict disease status (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 38488978: Diagnosis and treatment of Paget's disease of bone: position paper from the Italian Society of Osteoporosis, Mineral Metabolism and Skeletal Diseases (SIOMMMS) (guideline, 2024)

Existing links for #1266 screened by title: papers kept 3, dropped 0; guideline sources kept 2, dropped 0.

## Cluster `palindromic` (topics 1269) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Palindromic rheumatism causes recurrent self-limited attacks of joint inflammation and is best viewed as part of the rheumatoid arthritis continuum. Most patients evolve to RA, and there is no evidence-based optimum therapy.

**Teaching points**
- Systematic reviews find the optimum treatment strategy for palindromic rheumatism largely undefined, needing randomised trials in well-defined cohorts. [PMID 33401055]
- Randomised trials are needed to prove DMARDs prevent progression of palindromic rheumatism to autoimmune connective tissue disease and to determine which DMARD is preferred. [PMID 34525234]
- Palindromic rheumatism is conceptualised as part of the RA continuum, and since most patients evolve to RA it may offer a therapeutic opportunity to avoid that evolution. [PMID 31595059, 33666522]
- Ultrasound is expected to be central in risk modelling of the preclinical phases of inflammatory arthritis, including palindromic rheumatism. [PMID 33195348]

**Uncertainties**
- Predictors of progression to RA.

**MCQ angles**
- Recurrent short attacks with complete resolution between episodes.
- Anti-CCP and rheumatoid factor as risk markers (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Patient with hours-to-days of swollen finger joints, resolving, recurring monthly.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, antibody associations and hydroxychloroquine evidence are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 31595059: Palindromic rheumatism as part of the rheumatoid arthritis continuum (review, 2019)
- PMID 33195348: The Role of Ultrasound Across the Inflammatory Arthritis Continuum: Focus on "At-Risk" Individuals (review, 2020)
- PMID 33401055: How should we treat palindromic rheumatism? A systematic literature review (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 33666522: Palindromic rheumatism: a unique and enigmatic entity with a complex relationship with rheumatoid arthritis (review, 2021)
- PMID 34525234: Treatment of palindromic rheumatism: A systematic review (systematic review, 2021)

Existing links for #1269 screened by title: papers kept 1, dropped 0; guideline sources kept 0, dropped 0.

## Cluster `palliative_icu` (topics 1270) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Palliative care in the ICU is integrated early, with symptom assessment, family communication and structured support, as set out in the 2024 ESICM guideline and German consensus recommendations.

**Teaching points**
- The ESICM 2024 guideline suggests early integration of palliative care with standardised symptom assessment for patients at high risk of dying, and recommends shared decision-making and communication training for staff. [PMID 39361081]
- German recommendations state timely integration of palliative care in intensive care aims to improve quality of life and symptom control and to support highly stressed relatives. [PMID 37723595, 37285027]
- A narrative review gives practical recommendations for dexmedetomidine in palliative care. [PMID 35400162]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal drug regimens for terminal ICU symptoms are not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Comfort-focused care after withdrawal of life-sustaining treatment.
- Family conferences.

**Case hooks**
- Ventilated patient with end-stage lung disease and a goals-of-care decision to withdraw ventilation.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Drug dosing for dyspnoea, pain and agitation and extubation practice are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35400162: Dexmedetomidine: a magic bullet on its way into palliative care-a narrative review and practice recommendations (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37285027: [Palliative aspects in clinical acute and emergency medicine as well as intensive care medicine : Consensus paper of the DGIIN, DGK, DGP, DGHO, DGfN, DGNI, DGG, DGAI, DGINA and DG Palliativmedizin] (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37723595: Recommendations on palliative care aspects in intensive care medicine (guideline, 2023)

## Cluster `pancreatic_cancer` (topics 1271, 1272, 1273, 1275) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pancreatic cancer care centres on neoadjuvant therapy for borderline resectable disease, chemotherapy-based adjuvant treatment, and triplet or doublet chemotherapy for advanced disease according to fitness.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of borderline resectable and resectable PDAC concluded neoadjuvant therapy should be standard of care for borderline resectable disease, with the best regimen and role in resectable disease still to be defined. [PMID 40126570]
- A guideline review found adjuvant chemoradiotherapy gives no survival advantage over chemotherapy alone and is not recommended; SBRT in PDAC should be used only within a trial or registry. [PMID 37504342]
- A network meta-analysis of first-line regimens for unresectable or metastatic disease found gemcitabine plus nab-paclitaxel remains a viable alternative, particularly for patients unfit for triplet therapy. [PMID 39542008]
- A review aligns neoadjuvant guideline recommendations with real-world evidence to refine patient selection for resectable and borderline resectable disease. [PMID 41008927]
- Stereotactic radiotherapy raises tumour dose while sparing nearby organs, provided breathing motion is accounted for. [PMID 40839896]
- A study found non-receipt of guideline-concordant treatment associated with socioeconomic context, access to care and vulnerability at presentation. [PMID 42584751]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal neoadjuvant regimen.

**MCQ angles**
- FOLFIRINOX versus gemcitabine plus nab-paclitaxel by performance status.
- Neoadjuvant therapy in borderline resectable disease.

**Case hooks**
- 58-year-old with a borderline resectable pancreatic head adenocarcinoma, CA19-9 elevated.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Trial names and survival figures (PRODIGE, NAPOLI-3, PREOPANC), BRCA-targeted therapy and biliary drainage are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37504342: Adjuvant Chemotherapy and Radiotherapy in Resected Pancreatic Ductal Adenocarcinoma: A Systematic Review and Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 39542008: Comparison of first-line chemotherapy regimens in unresectable locally advanced or metastatic pancreatic cancer: a systematic review and Bayesian network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40126570: Neoadjuvant treatment versus upfront surgery in borderline resectable and resectable pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma: meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40839896: Radiotherapy of pancreatic cancers: 2025 update (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41008927: Neoadjuvant Therapy in Pancreatic Ductal Adenocarcinoma: Aligning Guideline Recommendations with Real-World Evidence (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42584751: Determinants of Non-Receipt of Guideline-Concordant Treatment in Pancreatic Cancer: A Classification Tree Analysis (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `pancreatic_cysts` (topics 1274) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pancreatic cysts, most often IPMNs, are managed by risk stratification using worrisome features and high-risk stigmata. Many resections are for benign lesions, and small stable cysts may need less intensive surveillance.

**Teaching points**
- High-risk stigmata strongly predict high-grade dysplasia or invasive carcinoma in IPMN, and surgical decisions should be individualised for preferences, comorbidities and life expectancy. [PMID 40522384]
- Small cyst size and lack of growth are negative predictors of malignant transformation in low-risk IPMN, so surveillance of small stable cysts might be de-intensified, pending prospective studies. [PMID 39370669]
- Roughly 44% of pancreatic resections for IPMN are for benign cysts or IPMN with low-grade dysplasia, because of pre-operative misclassification. [PMID 41284225]
- Among resected main-duct and mixed-type IPMNs a significant proportion are benign, so better diagnostics are needed. [PMID 40117982]
- A Danish consensus advocates a risk-adapted, multidisciplinary approach that avoids unnecessary investigations and surgical overtreatment. [PMID 42678719]
- Fukuoka consensus and European evidence-based guidelines have been compared for IPMN management. [PMID 38893274]

**Uncertainties**
- Surveillance intervals and when to stop.

**MCQ angles**
- Worrisome features versus high-risk stigmata.
- Main-duct dilation and mural nodule.

**Case hooks**
- Incidental 14 mm branch-duct pancreatic cyst on CT for another reason.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** The specific size thresholds and surveillance schedules in Fukuoka and ACG guidance are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38893274: Navigating Intraductal Papillary Mucinous Neoplasm Management through Fukuoka Consensus vs. European Evidence-Based Guidelines on Pancreatic Cystic Neoplasms-A Study on Two European Centers (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39370669: Small cyst size and lack of growth as negative predictors of malignant transformation in low-risk intraductal papillary mucinous neoplasms of the pancreas: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40117982: Risk of pancreatic cancer and high-grade dysplasia in resected main-duct and mixed-type intraductal papillary mucinous neoplasms: A prevalence meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40522384: Understanding intraductal papillary mucinous neoplasm from pathogenesis to risk assessment: a pictorial review based on the kyoto guidelines (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41284225: High Burden of Pancreatic Surgery for Intraductal Papillary Mucinous Neoplasia With Low-Grade Dysplasia and Benign Cysts: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 42678719: Danish Pancreatic Cancer Group consensus guidelines on the management of pancreatic IPMN (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `pnet` (topics 1276) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pancreatic neuroendocrine tumours are managed by grade, function and extent: surgery (increasingly minimally invasive) for localised nonfunctioning tumours, and somatostatin analogues, targeted agents, temozolomide-based chemotherapy or ablation for selected advanced or small lesions.

**Teaching points**
- Minimally invasive surgery for nonfunctioning pancreatic NETs is safe and associated with a considerably shorter postoperative hospital stay than open surgery, although further studies are needed. [PMID 39806757]
- EUS-guided radiofrequency ablation is feasible for pancreatic neuroendocrine neoplasms with excellent technical success, high radiological and clinical response and acceptable adverse events. [PMID 37702096]
- Temozolomide-based regimens in advanced locally unresectable and metastatic pancreatic NETs appear effective, though additional clinical trials are required. [PMID 38347461]
- The ASCO symptom management guideline covers liver-directed and systemic options and palliative care for well-differentiated GEP-NETs, with limited guidance on sequencing. [PMID 40344544]
- Treatments for MEN1-associated endocrine tumours are supported by low or very low-certainty evidence. [PMID 40523371]

**Uncertainties**
- Sequencing of systemic therapies.

**MCQ angles**
- Insulinoma, gastrinoma and glucagonoma as functioning tumours (not in abstracts).
- MEN1 association.

**Case hooks**
- Incidental 1.5 cm nonfunctioning pancreatic NET on CT.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Size thresholds for observation, grading by Ki-67 and PRRT are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37702096: Safety and efficacy of endoscopic ultrasound-guided radiofrequency ablation for pancreatic neuroendocrine neoplasms: Systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38347461: Efficacy and safety of temozolomide-based regimens in advanced pancreatic neuroendocrine tumors: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39806757: Minimally invasive versus open surgery for nonfunctioning pancreatic neuroendocrine tumors: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40344544: Symptom Management for Well-Differentiated Gastroenteropancreatic Neuroendocrine Tumors: ASCO Guideline (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 40523371: Treatments for MEN1-associated endocrine tumours: three systematic reviews and a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `pancreatitis_complications` (topics 1277) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Acute pancreatitis management is guided by recent ACG and IAP guidelines, emphasising early refeeding, fluid strategy and endoscopic or minimally invasive management of collections. Targeted anti-inflammatory therapy remains a research gap.

**Teaching points**
- The 2024 ACG guideline addresses the safety of early refeeding and its importance in preventing complications of acute pancreatitis. [PMID 38857482]
- The revised 2025 IAP guidelines identify targeted therapies for controlling systemic inflammation and mitigating organ dysfunction as research priorities. [PMID 40651900]
- A meta-analysis of lactated Ringer's versus saline suggests lactated Ringer's may curb pancreatic necrosis and other indices, though this needs further corroboration. [PMID 40085761]
- Comorbid diabetes and pancreatic necrosis are predictors of enteral nutrition intolerance in acute pancreatitis. [PMID 40077780]
- A Spanish endoscopy consensus provides guidance on endoscopic management of pancreatic collections. [PMID 38305682]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal fluid type and rate.

**MCQ angles**
- Early oral or enteral feeding is preferred to prolonged fasting.
- Infected necrosis managed with step-up approach (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Patient with severe gallstone pancreatitis on day 10 with a walled-off collection.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Atlanta classification of complications, timing of intervention for necrosis and antibiotics are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38305682: Endoscopic management of pancreatic collections. Endoscopic Ultrasound Group, Spanish Society of Digestive Endoscopy (GSEED-USE) Clinical Guidelines (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38857482: American College of Gastroenterology Guidelines: Management of Acute Pancreatitis (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40077780: Clinical Predictors and Prevalence of Enteral Nutrition Intolerance in Acute Pancreatitis: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `type3c_diabetes` (topics 1278) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pancreatogenic (type 3c) diabetes is accompanied by exocrine pancreatic insufficiency, which needs enzyme replacement titrated to nutritional status. Diagnostic testing for exocrine insufficiency remains limited in availability.

**Teaching points**
- The AGA update notes cross-sectional imaging cannot identify exocrine pancreatic insufficiency, and that breath tests and direct pancreatic function tests are promising but not widely available. [PMID 37737818]
- Pancreatic enzyme replacement dosing guidelines vary widely across disease types; most are initial doses, not upper limits, and doses should be titrated to nutritional status and response with regular follow-up. [PMID 38117426]
- Exocrine pancreatic insufficiency is common in coeliac disease, and is more frequent in patients with persistent symptoms on a gluten-free diet (28.4%) than in asymptomatic patients (3%). [PMID 37294459]
- Brazilian recommendations cover diagnosis and treatment of exocrine insufficiency including in pancreatic cancer patients, where enzyme replacement can improve symptoms, nutrition and quality of life. [PMID 41337569, 40415734]
- Sarcopenia is common in chronic pancreatitis, and early detection and comprehensive care are advised. [PMID 40077740]

**Uncertainties**
- Glucose management specific to type 3c diabetes is not covered in the retrieved abstracts.

**MCQ angles**
- Faecal elastase for exocrine insufficiency (not in abstracts).
- Enzyme replacement with meals.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with chronic pancreatitis, steatorrhoea and brittle diabetes.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Insulin therapy, hypoglycaemia risk and glucagon deficiency in type 3c diabetes are not covered by the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37294459: Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency Is Common in Celiac Disease: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37737818: AGA Clinical Practice Update on the Epidemiology, Evaluation, and Management of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency: Expert Review (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38117426: Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency Dosing Guidelines for Pancreatic Enzyme Replacement Therapy Vary Widely Across Disease Types (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40077740: Prevalence and Risk of Sarcopenia in Patients with Chronic Pancreatitis: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40415734: The Brazilian Society of Surgical Oncology (SBCO) Guidelines for the Treatment of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency in Cancer Patients (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41337569: Brazilian consensus- and evidence-based recommendations in the diagnosis and treatment of pancreatic exocrine insufficiency in patients after digestive surgeries. Position paper of six brazilian medical societies of surgery (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `pancytopenia` (topics 1279) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pancytopenia work-up should consider marrow failure; for severe acquired aplastic anaemia current guidance adds eltrombopag to immunosuppression, requires expert-centre management and gives antimicrobial prophylaxis for neutropenia.

**Teaching points**
- The 2026 ASH guideline for severe and very severe aplastic anaemia recommends adding eltrombopag to immunosuppressive regimens and antibiotic and antifungal prophylaxis for patients with neutropenia, and describes a second-line option after failure of immunosuppression. [PMID 41920091]
- The British Society for Haematology guideline strongly recommends multidisciplinary discussion and expert-centre involvement in adult aplastic anaemia. [PMID 38247114]
- In children with severe aplastic anaemia, a systematic review supports adding eltrombopag to first-line immunosuppressive therapy; AIEOP guidelines cover acquired aplastic anaemia in childhood. [PMID 37168802, 38889660]

**Uncertainties**
- Causes of pancytopenia other than marrow failure are not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Marrow aspirate and trephine with cytogenetics to distinguish aplastic anaemia from MDS.
- Immunosuppression versus transplant by age.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with fatigue, bruising and pancytopenia with hypocellular marrow.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** The differential (B12/folate deficiency, MDS, leukaemia, hypersplenism, drugs) and initial investigations are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `pandemic_prep` (topics 1280) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Lessons from COVID-19 for preparedness are mainly about health system capacity, hospital infection control, surveillance and data. Retrieved sources are regional and indirect, so this page is limited.

**Teaching points**
- South American lessons from COVID-19 stress strengthening health infrastructure, institutionalising telehealth, combating misinformation and promoting research and innovation. [PMID 42531590]
- A Latin American Delphi consensus sets regional priorities for pandemic preparedness and response, stressing collaboration and capacity. [PMID 40288949]
- Hospital-based bacterial and fungal outbreaks occurred during the pandemic, and findings support environmental hygiene and antimicrobial stewardship within preparedness frameworks. [PMID 41344614]
- A living review of vaccine effectiveness observational studies highlights standardised protocols and robust data linkage between vaccination, testing and outcomes for future pandemics. [PMID 42594644]
- Simulation-based airway training and lessons from blood donation services provide further preparedness practices. [PMID 41446849, 42624820]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence is regional and observational.

**MCQ angles**
- Surveillance, testing and data linkage in early outbreak response.

**Case hooks**
- Hospital preparing surge capacity for a novel respiratory virus.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** WHO or national preparedness frameworks and vaccine platform lessons are not in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `panic_disorder` (topics 1281) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Panic disorder is treated with CBT and/or medication. Evidence for drug treatments is of moderate to very low certainty, and CBT is effective in several delivery formats including guided self-help.

**Teaching points**
- A network meta-analysis of drug treatment for panic disorder rated the evidence as moderate to very low certainty because of bias, inconsistency and imprecision. [PMID 35045991]
- A network meta-analysis found no difference between guided self-help, face-to-face individual and group CBT for panic disorder, although confidence in the evidence was not high for any format. [PMID 37132646]
- A network meta-analysis of psychotherapies for panic disorder with or without agoraphobia identified reasonable first-line choices and found high-risk-of-bias studies inflate efficacy. [PMID 35049483]
- Virtual reality applications improve anxiety disorder outcomes over waiting-list or psychoeducation controls and can complement therapy when conventional treatment is unavailable. [PMID 37453405]

**Uncertainties**
- Comparative efficacy of drug classes.

**MCQ angles**
- CBT with interoceptive exposure.
- SSRIs as first-line drug (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with recurrent attacks of palpitations, dyspnoea and fear of dying.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Which drugs (SSRI, SNRI, benzodiazepine) work best, diagnostic criteria and differential diagnosis are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35045991: Drug treatment for panic disorder with or without agoraphobia: systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35049483: Comparative efficacy and acceptability of psychotherapies for panic disorder with or without agoraphobia: systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2022)

## Cluster `papilloedema` (topics 1282, 1283) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Papilloedema from raised intracranial pressure, commonly idiopathic intracranial hypertension, threatens vision. Treatment combines weight loss, acetazolamide, and surgical options for refractory or severe visual loss.

**Teaching points**
- Venous sinus stenting is a safe and effective option for IIH patients unresponsive to medical therapy or with significant visual symptoms, but long-term outcomes need study. [PMID 38457956]
- Transorbital ultrasonography shows increased optic nerve sheath diameter and optic disc elevation associated with IIH, allowing non-invasive detection of raised ICP. [PMID 37599314]
- A meta-analysis of topiramate versus acetazolamide in IIH noted topiramate's benefit on weight reduction and acetazolamide's ability to lower CSF pressure and improve vision, recommending combination treatment. [PMID 40142261]
- Optic nerve sheath fenestration to preserve vision in IIH is supported by updated meta-analysis but is limited by publication bias. [PMID 39585430]
- GLP-1 and dual GIP/GLP-1 receptor agonists are under study in IIH, with randomised trials needed. [PMID 40937960]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal surgical option for vision-threatening disease.

**MCQ angles**
- IIH: young obese woman with headache, pulsatile tinnitus and papilloedema, normal imaging.
- Visual field monitoring.

**Case hooks**
- Woman with headache, transient visual obscurations and bilateral disc swelling.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Frisén grading, pseudopapilloedema (optic disc drusen) distinction and lumbar puncture opening pressure are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37599314: B-mode transorbital ultrasonography for the diagnosis of idiopathic intracranial hypertension: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38457956: Venous sinus stenting for idiopathic intracranial hypertension: An updated Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39585430: Effectiveness of optic nerve sheath fenestration in preserving vision in idiopathic intracranial hypertension: an updated meta-analysis and systematic review (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40142261: Effectiveness of Topiramate Versus Acetazolamide in the Management of Idiopathic Intracranial Hypertension: ASystematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40937960: Efficacy and Safety of GLP-1 and Dual GIP/GLP-1 Receptor Agonists in Idiopathic Intracranial Hypertension: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `paracetamol` (topics 1284) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Paracetamol poisoning is treated with prompt acetylcysteine, which prevents liver injury when started early. Two-bag regimens are effective with fewer anaphylactoid reactions, and consensus statements standardise assessment and triage.

**Teaching points**
- A US and Canada consensus statement gives evidence-based recommendations for management of acetaminophen poisoning, and a separate consensus guides out-of-hospital triage of paracetamol exposure. [PMID 37552484, 40047505]
- Early N-acetylcysteine, with treatment from 8 to 24 hours after overdose, is important to prevent or minimise liver damage regardless of regimen or route. [PMID 36034775]
- A two-bag acetylcysteine regimen was safe and effective and significantly reduced non-allergic anaphylactoid reactions compared with a three-bag regimen (OR 0.24). [PMID 40013897]
- The paracetamol-aminotransferase multiplication product can be used more than 24 hours after overdose and in staggered ingestions, addressing limitations of the Rumack-Matthew nomogram. [PMID 36444937]
- There is insufficient evidence to recommend fomepizole or cimetidine as primary or adjunct therapy in severe paracetamol poisoning. [PMID 32762579]

**Uncertainties**
- Treatment of late presentations and staggered overdoses.

**MCQ angles**
- Rumack-Matthew nomogram from 4 hours.
- Acetylcysteine is most effective within 8 hours.

**Case hooks**
- Patient presenting 6 hours after an intentional paracetamol overdose.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** King's College criteria for transplant, nomogram thresholds and acetylcysteine doses are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32762579: Metabolic and mitochondrial treatments for severe paracetamol poisoning: a systematic review (systematic review, 2020)
- PMID 36034775: N-Acetylcysteine for Preventing Acetaminophen-Induced Liver Injury: A Comprehensive Review (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 36444937: Performance of the paracetamol-aminotransferase multiplication product in risk stratification after paracetamol (acetaminophen) poisoning: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37552484: Management of Acetaminophen Poisoning in the US and Canada: A Consensus Statement (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 40013897: Comparison of two-bag and three-bag acetylcysteine regimens in the treatment of paracetamol poisoning: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40047505: Out-of-hospital assessment and triage of paracetamol (acetaminophen) exposure in the United States and Canada: a consensus guideline (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `paraganglioma` (topics 1285) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Paragangliomas, especially extra-adrenal tumours with germline SDHB variants, carry a high metastatic risk. Genetic testing guides surveillance and management, and treatment of metastases combines local and systemic options.

**Teaching points**
- The international SDHB consensus states that management of metastases in SDHB-related PPGL is challenging and relies on local or systemic therapies, or both. [PMID 38097671]
- Patients with a pathogenic germline variant have a higher rate of metastasis than patients with no identified variant. [PMID 41183483]
- NANETS consensus guidelines address surveillance after resection and management of metastatic or unresectable pheochromocytoma and paraganglioma. [PMID 33939658]
- The Japan Endocrine Society guideline notes genetic testing guides management of different variants and molecularly targeted drugs are advancing. [PMID 41083371]
- European guidance exists for adrenal and extra-adrenal paraganglioma in children and adolescents. [PMID 41261973]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal systemic therapy for metastatic disease.

**MCQ angles**
- SDHB mutations and malignant potential.
- Alpha-blockade before surgery for functioning tumours (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Young patient with a carotid body paraganglioma and a family history.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Biochemical screening (plasma metanephrines), imaging choice and perioperative blockade are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33939658: The North American Neuroendocrine Tumor Society Consensus Guidelines for Surveillance and Management of Metastatic and/or Unresectable Pheochromocytoma and Paraganglioma (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 38097671: Management of phaeochromocytoma and paraganglioma in patients with germline SDHB pathogenic variants: an international expert Consensus statement (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `paraneoplastic` (topics 1286) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Paraneoplastic and autoimmune neurological syndromes are diagnosed by antibody testing, CSF, MRI and FDG-PET, with a search for an underlying tumour. Management is multidisciplinary immunotherapy plus tumour treatment.

**Teaching points**
- UK national guidelines on suspected infectious and autoimmune encephalitis in adults stress multidisciplinary input to reduce mortality and long-term neurological morbidity. [PMID 42863638]
- A meta-analysis of FDG PET in autoimmune encephalitis found antibody-related patterns of regional metabolic abnormality, supporting its role in diagnosis and prognosis. [PMID 39601059]
- Neurofilament light chain was higher in serum and CSF in neuronal surface antibody-associated encephalitis than in controls but was not significantly related to clinical or paraclinical features. [PMID 39856041]
- Autoimmune encephalitis after herpes simplex encephalitis has age-specific features and improved outcomes with immunotherapy, which was confirmed as safe. [PMID 40780589]
- Seronegative autoimmune encephalitis is diagnostically challenging and needs biomarker discovery and prospective cohorts. [PMID 41499723]
- NCCN guidelines for thymoma and thymic carcinoma highlight multidisciplinary management, relevant as thymoma is a paraneoplastic neurological association. [PMID 40499586]

**Uncertainties**
- Predictors of response to immunotherapy in seronegative disease.

**MCQ angles**
- Anti-Hu with small cell lung cancer; anti-Yo with ovarian or breast cancer (not in abstracts).
- Search for tumour when onconeural antibodies are positive.

**Case hooks**
- Smoker with subacute sensory neuronopathy and anti-Hu antibodies.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Antibody-specific tumour associations (Hu, Yo, Ri, NMDAR) and screening imaging strategy are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39601059: Regional Metabolic Abnormalities in Autoimmune Encephalitis: A Meta-analysis of 498 Cases With Brain FDG PET (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39856041: Neurofilament light chain levels in neuronal surface antibody-associated autoimmune encephalitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40499586: Thymomas and Thymic Carcinomas, Version 2.2025, NCCN Clinical Practice Guidelines In Oncology (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40780589: Clinical phenotype and outcomes in autoimmune encephalitis after herpes simplex virus encephalitis: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41499723: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of the Clinical Features Associated With Seronegative Autoimmune Encephalitis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 42863638: Management of suspected infectious and autoimmune encephalitis in adults - National Guidelines from the United Kingdom Joint Societies (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `empyema` (topics 1287) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Parapneumonic effusion and empyema are treated with antibiotics and drainage, with intrapleural fibrinolytics or surgery for loculated disease. Paediatric data show chest tube with fibrinolytics is lower in cost with similar outcomes to surgery.

**Teaching points**
- A Cochrane review found intrapleural fibrinolytic therapy in adult parapneumonic effusion reduced overall treatment failure (OR 0.16, low-certainty evidence). [PMID 31684683]
- A living systematic review in children found a chest tube plus fibrinolytics costs less, with similar length of stay and clinical outcomes to other management options. [PMID 37463660]
- Implementing a clinical practice guideline for paediatric empyema improved adherence to national guidelines without changing length of stay or treatment failure rates. [PMID 39423731]
- Japanese guidelines classify empyema into acute, chronic and postoperative types and give surgical recommendations for each. [PMID 39969668]
- A 2026 IDSA/PIDS guideline addresses paediatric pneumonia and pleural infection with GRADE methodology. [PMID 41839546]

**Uncertainties**
- Best first-line approach in adults (fibrinolysis versus early surgery).

**MCQ angles**
- Pleural fluid pH below 7.2 or pus indicates drainage.
- Intrapleural tPA with DNase (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Pneumonia not responding to antibiotics with a loculated effusion on ultrasound.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Light criteria, pleural fluid pH and glucose thresholds, MIST data and antibiotic duration are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 31684683: Intra-pleural fibrinolytic therapy versus placebo, or a different fibrinolytic agent, in the treatment of adult parapneumonic effusions and empyema (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 37463660: Conservative and Surgical Modalities in the Management of Pediatric Parapneumonic Effusion and Empyema: A Living Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39423731: Implications of Using a Clinical Practice Guideline on Outcomes in Pediatric Empyema (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39969668: Guidelines for the treatment of empyema (The Japanese Association for Chest Surgery) (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41839546: Clinical Practice Guideline by the Infectious Diseases Society of America and the Pediatric Infectious Diseases Society: 2026 Guideline Update on The Management of Community-Acquired Pneumonia in Infants and Children Older than 3 Months of Age (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `parenteral_nutrition` (topics 1288) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Parenteral nutrition is reserved for patients who cannot be fed enterally, with ESPEN practical guidance for home PN, micronutrients and lipid emulsions. In critical illness early enteral feeding is preferred where the gut works.

**Teaching points**
- The ESPEN practical guideline on home parenteral nutrition provides recommendations using SIGN methodology. [PMID 36796121]
- The ESPEN short micronutrient guideline offers practical advice on provision and monitoring of micronutrients during nutritional support in at-risk diseases. [PMID 38350290]
- Expert consensus statements from the Lipid Summit address intravenous lipid emulsions in long-term and home parenteral nutrition. [PMID 41438196]
- In critically ill patients with a functioning gut, early enteral nutrition is favoured over early parenteral nutrition for infection control and recovery. [PMID 39796444]
- A GI diseases nutrition guideline recommends nutritional support within 72 hours of admission when oral intake covers under 60% of protein-calorie needs. [PMID 40035369]

**Uncertainties**
- Lipid emulsion choice.

**MCQ angles**
- Complications: line sepsis, refeeding, liver disease.
- Enteral nutrition preferred when the gut works.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with short bowel syndrome on home parenteral nutrition with raised liver enzymes.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Indications for PN, access, macronutrient prescription and complications monitoring are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36796121: ESPEN practical guideline: Home parenteral nutrition (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38350290: ESPEN practical short micronutrient guideline (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39796444: Comparison of Early Enteral Nutrition Versus Early Parenteral Nutrition in Critically Ill Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40035369: Clinical nutrition in gastrointestinal diseases: an up-to-date clinical practice guideline (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41438196: Long-term/home parenteral nutrition: expert consensus statements regarding intravenous lipid emulsions (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `parkinson` (topics 1289, 1290, 1291, 1292, 1293, 1294) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Parkinson disease care covers dopaminergic therapy, management of motor complications, and referral for deep brain stimulation or other advanced therapies. Retrieval gave guideline material on non-motor problems and referral, not on levodopa itself.

**Teaching points**
- A consensus on referral for deep brain stimulation provides a framework so that appropriate candidates have timely access to surgery and misconceptions about brain surgery are addressed. [PMID 41484162]
- A Gulf consensus uses the 5-2-1 criteria for advanced therapy referral and proposes a tiered network model for Parkinson disease care. [PMID 42656222]
- The German guideline gives a practice-oriented stepwise approach to diagnosis and treatment of autonomic failure, pain and sleep disturbances in Parkinson disease, and another section covers impulse control disorders, psychosis and delirium. [PMID 39751950, 39046524]
- Best practice guidelines address diagnosis, evaluation and management of cognitive disorders in Parkinson disease to optimise cognitive care. [PMID 41870152]
- A Gulf consensus covers safe Ramadan fasting in Parkinson disease with shared decision-making on motor and non-motor management. [PMID 41458120]

**Uncertainties**
- Levodopa dosing strategy and management of motor fluctuations are not covered by the retrieved abstracts.

**MCQ angles**
- Referral for DBS when motor complications persist despite optimised medication.
- Psychosis in Parkinson disease: avoid typical antipsychotics (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Patient on levodopa 6 years with wearing-off and dyskinesia.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Levodopa and dopamine agonist therapy, COMT/MAO-B inhibitors, motor complication management and DBS outcomes are not stated in the retrieved abstracts; needs a core Parkinson guideline (NICE NG71 or MDS) before approval.

**Sources**

- PMID 39751950: Diagnosis and treatment of autonomic failure, pain and sleep disturbances in Parkinson's disease: guideline "Parkinson's disease" of the German Society of Neurology (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `pnh` (topics 1295) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Paroxysmal nocturnal haemoglobinuria is diagnosed by flow cytometric clone detection and treated with terminal or proximal complement inhibitors. Breakthrough haemolysis and residual anaemia are the main management challenges.

**Teaching points**
- An expert consensus addresses management of pharmacodynamic breakthrough haemolysis in treated PNH, based on clinical experience in the absence of randomised data. [PMID 39665683]
- Expert consensus covers monitoring and treatment of PNH clones in patients with aplastic anaemia in Asia, including when haemolysis is mild or moderate. [PMID 39596227]
- A guideline update notes the principal goal is control of haemolysis and that approaches to residual anaemia and fatigue need refinement as new complement-targeting drugs emerge. [PMID 39505557]
- Chinese and Singapore consensus documents give recommendations for screening, diagnosis and management, and a Chinese consensus covers clonal screening and monitoring of complement inhibitor therapy. [PMID 39307719, 38979993, 38604785]

**Uncertainties**
- Choice between C5 and proximal complement inhibitors.

**MCQ angles**
- Flow cytometry for GPI-anchored proteins (CD55/CD59).
- Thrombosis as the main cause of death (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with dark morning urine, haemolytic anaemia and Budd-Chiari syndrome.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Clinical triad, thrombosis prophylaxis and eculizumab/ravulizumab specifics are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38604785: [Expert consensus on clonal screening and monitoring of complement inhibitor therapy in paroxysmal nocturnal hemoglobinuria (2024)] (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38979993: Consensus recommendations for optimising the diagnosis and treatment of paroxysmal nocturnal haemoglobinuria in Singapore (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `pfo` (topics 1296) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** PFO closure is considered in selected younger patients with cryptogenic stroke after careful work-up; residual shunt after closure raises recurrence risk, and evidence in patients over 60 is limited.

**Teaching points**
- The ESO guidelines give recommendations and consensus statements on diagnostic methods for PFO detection and on medical management after PFO closure. [PMID 38752755]
- A meta-analysis found a residual shunt after PFO closure significantly increased the risk of recurrent cerebrovascular events. [PMID 36882039]
- In patients over 60 with cryptogenic stroke, evidence for PFO closure is limited by confounding and short follow-up, and long-term randomised trials are essential. [PMID 41099439]
- British guidance on agitated saline contrast echocardiography describes when to move to advanced imaging and uses frameworks such as the RoPE score. [PMID 42219525]
- In acute pulmonary embolism, PFO was associated with higher ischaemic stroke incidence (OR 5.36) and increased mortality. [PMID 39622459]

**Uncertainties**
- Selection of older patients for closure.

**MCQ angles**
- RoPE score to estimate attribution of stroke to PFO.
- Antithrombotic therapy after closure.

**Case hooks**
- 38-year-old with an embolic-pattern infarct and a large right-to-left shunt on bubble study.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Trial results (CLOSE, RESPECT, REDUCE), large shunt and atrial septal aneurysm criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36882039: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of the Association between Residual Shunts after Patent Foramen Ovale Closure and Long-Term Cerebrovascular Events (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38752755: European Stroke Organisation (ESO) Guidelines on the diagnosis and management of patent foramen ovale (PFO) after stroke (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39622459: Risk of ischemic stroke in patients with pulmonary embolism and patent foramen ovale: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41099439: Outcomes of patent foramen ovale closure in patients over 60 years with cryptogenic stroke: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 42219525: Agitated Saline Contrast Echocardiography in the diagnosis of right to left shunts: Guidance and recommendations from the British Society of Echocardiography (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `ventilator_dyssynchrony` (topics 1297) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Patient-ventilator asynchrony, particularly ineffective and double triggering, is associated with longer mechanical ventilation and ICU stay. Proportional modes such as NAVA improve synchrony, with some evidence of weaning benefit.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found patient-ventilator asynchrony, specifically ineffective triggering and double triggering, was associated with longer mechanical ventilation and ICU length of stay. [PMID 40793855]
- Neurally adjusted ventilatory assist improves patient-ventilator interaction, but effects on patient-important outcomes remain uncertain. [PMID 30882481]
- Compared with other partial support modes, NAVA may improve weaning success in difficult-to-wean adults, and a later meta-analysis suggested improved synchronisation and outcomes in acute respiratory failure compared with conventional modes. [PMID 34187528, 35273975]
- A review addresses monitoring of asynchrony during non-invasive ventilation, and machine learning detection of asynchrony is developing but needs standardised definitions. [PMID 36743668, 40640842]

**Uncertainties**
- Whether correcting asynchrony improves survival.

**MCQ angles**
- Ineffective triggering and auto-PEEP.
- Double triggering and short inspiratory time.

**Case hooks**
- Ventilated COPD patient with ineffective efforts on the flow-time waveform.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Waveform recognition of each asynchrony type and adjustment of trigger, cycling and sedation are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30882481: Effect of Neurally Adjusted Ventilatory Assist on Patient-Ventilator Interaction in Mechanically Ventilated Adults: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 34187528: Neurally adjusted ventilatory assist as a weaning mode for adults with invasive mechanical ventilation: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35273975: Neurally Adjusted Ventilatory Assist vs. Conventional Mechanical Ventilation in Adults and Children With Acute Respiratory Failure: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36743668: Monitoring the patient-ventilator asynchrony during non-invasive ventilation (systematic review, 2022)

- PMID 40793855: The Association Between Patient-Ventilator Asynchrony and Clinical Outcomes in Mechanically Ventilated Patients: A Systematic Review (meta-analysis, 2025)
