## Cluster `pcsk9` (topics 1298) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Evolocumab lowered LDL cholesterol by about 60% on top of statins and reduced cardiovascular events in FOURIER. Benefit is greatest in high-risk patients, and PCSK9 inhibitors or ezetimibe add little in low-to-moderate risk groups.

**Teaching points**
- FOURIER: evolocumab on a statin background lowered LDL cholesterol (59% reduction versus placebo, median 30 mg/dL) and reduced cardiovascular events, showing patients with atherosclerotic disease benefit from lowering LDL below current targets. [PMID 28304224]
- A BMJ network meta-analysis found that among moderate- and low-risk groups, adding a PCSK9 inhibitor or ezetimibe to statins gave little or no benefit for MI and stroke. [PMID 35508321]
- A meta-analysis of 35 randomised trials found PCSK9 inhibitors improve cardiovascular outcomes without overall benefit on all-cause or cardiovascular mortality, a benefit that may be reachable with higher baseline LDL. [PMID 29223954]
- Lowering LDL cholesterol is as effective at reducing vascular events in older patients as in those under 75, supporting non-statin therapy in older patients. [PMID 33186535]
- PCSK9 inhibition significantly reduced the risk of venous thromboembolism, with lipoprotein(a) reduction a possible mediator. [PMID 32223429]
- The 2018 AHA/ACC systematic review found benefit for ezetimibe and PCSK9 inhibitors added to statins, but not for niacin or CETP inhibitors. [PMID 30586775]

**Uncertainties**
- Mortality benefit in all-cause outcomes.

**MCQ angles**
- Who qualifies for PCSK9 inhibitors: very high risk above LDL threshold on maximal statin and ezetimibe (not in abstracts).
- Injection frequency and cost.

**Case hooks**
- Post-MI patient with LDL-C 2.2 mmol/L on high-intensity statin plus ezetimibe.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Eligibility thresholds and cost-effectiveness rules differ by country and are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28304224: Evolocumab and Clinical Outcomes in Patients with Cardiovascular Disease (other, 2017)
- PMID 29223954: Effect of PCSK9 Inhibitors on Clinical Outcomes in Patients With Hypercholesterolemia: A Meta-Analysis of 35 Randomized Controlled Trials (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 30586775: Systematic Review for the 2018 AHA/ACC/AACVPR/AAPA/ABC/ACPM/ADA/AGS/APhA/ASPC/NLA/PCNA Guideline on the Management of Blood Cholesterol: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Task Force on Clinical Practice Guidelines (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 32223429: The Effect of PCSK9 (Proprotein Convertase Subtilisin/Kexin Type 9) Inhibition on the Risk of Venous Thromboembolism (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 33186535: Efficacy and safety of lowering LDL cholesterol in older patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 35508321: PCSK9 inhibitors and ezetimibe with or without statin therapy for cardiovascular risk reduction: a systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)

## Cluster `cardiac_arrest_rhythms` (topics 1299) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Non-shockable arrest rhythms (PEA and asystole) are managed with high-quality CPR, early adrenaline and a search for reversible causes. The 2025 ERC and AHA guidelines emphasise early effective ALS and distinguishing causative from secondary arrhythmias.

**Teaching points**
- The ERC 2025 adult ALS guidelines emphasise providing early and effective ALS interventions to improve survival from in- and out-of-hospital cardiac arrest. [PMID 41117572]
- The 2025 AHA adult ALS guideline underscores identifying causative versus secondary arrhythmias to inform timing of therapeutic strategies. [PMID 41122884]
- ERC and AHA special circumstances guidelines describe modifications to basic and advanced life support for conditions that cause arrest, with separate graded recommendations for adults and children. [PMID 41117569, 41122889]
- The 2025 AHA post-cardiac arrest care guideline covers care after return of spontaneous circulation, survivorship and organ donation. [PMID 41122894]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for individual drugs and airway strategies is covered in the full guidelines.

**MCQ angles**
- Reversible causes (4 Hs and 4 Ts) in PEA.
- Asystole: check leads, confirm rhythm, adrenaline every 3-5 minutes (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- In-hospital arrest with PEA and a history of massive haemoptysis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** The algorithm (compressions, adrenaline timing, rhythm checks) and the list of reversible causes are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `peanut_oit` (topics 1300) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Oral immunotherapy can desensitise people with peanut and other IgE-mediated food allergies, with omalizumab adjunct therapy improving safety. Long-term outcomes, optimal duration and cost-effectiveness remain open.

**Teaching points**
- The EAACI 2025 guidelines on IgE-mediated food allergy note future research is needed on disease-modifying treatments with standardised patient-focused outcomes. [PMID 39473345]
- Omalizumab with oral immunotherapy in children enhances the safety of OIT, but optimal duration, long-term outcomes and cost-effectiveness need further research. [PMID 40143213]
- Biologics in IgE-mediated food allergy have limited RCT evidence, and more research is needed on treatment duration and long-term outcomes. [PMID 40510735, 37185824]
- Quality of life benefit was observed after completing an OIT protocol, with limited evidence of benefit during active treatment. [PMID 40919791]
- The GA2LEN 2022 guideline addresses approaches to education, prediction of severe reactions, cost-effectiveness of immunotherapy and biologics. [PMID 36119657]

**Uncertainties**
- Sustained unresponsiveness after stopping OIT.

**MCQ angles**
- OIT risks: allergic reactions during build-up dosing.
- Role of omalizumab as adjunct.

**Case hooks**
- Teenager with peanut anaphylaxis asking about oral immunotherapy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Efficacy figures from PALISADE and dose protocols are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36119657: Managing food allergy: GA&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;LEN guideline 2022 (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37185824: Biologicals in IgE-mediated food allergy (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `paeds_all` (topics 1301) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Paediatric B-ALL treatment increasingly integrates blinatumomab, asparaginase optimisation, targeted agents and CAR T-cell therapy for relapsed or refractory disease, with attention to complications such as thrombosis.

**Teaching points**
- The ASH 2026 guideline for frontline management of ALL in adolescents and young adults states use of targeted agents in frontline therapy is increasingly supported, though further research is needed. [PMID 41670627]
- Recommendations address integrating frontline blinatumomab, asparaginase and antimetabolites, and asparaginase hypersensitivity and formulation issues. [PMID 42578884]
- A meta-analysis of CAR T-cell therapy in relapsed or refractory B-ALL found promising results with anti-CD22 CAR T, and new costimulatory domains and targets could enhance efficacy. [PMID 38738799]
- A practice guideline gives GRADE-based recommendations for preparing children and young adults with B-ALL for CAR T-cell therapy, and an international consensus defines B-cell recovery after CD19 CAR T. [PMID 38488312, 41367923]
- Anticoagulant treatment of venous thromboembolism in children with ALL should be tailored to thrombosis site, clinical and radiological evolution and ongoing asparaginase exposure. [PMID 42749602]

**Uncertainties**
- Sequencing of immunotherapy and CAR T.

**MCQ angles**
- Asparaginase toxicities: pancreatitis, thrombosis, hypersensitivity (not in abstracts).
- CRS and neurotoxicity with CAR T.

**Case hooks**
- Child with B-ALL relapsing 14 months after end of treatment.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Risk stratification, MRD-guided therapy and standard backbone regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `peep` (topics 1302) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** In ARDS, PEEP is set individually within protective ventilation, with guidelines advising against routine muscle relaxants or corticosteroids in moderate to severe disease. Electrical impedance tomography is a promising but unproven titration tool.

**Teaching points**
- A 2026 German guideline advises keeping respiratory parameters in the protective range, adjusting PEEP individually, and not routinely using muscle relaxation or corticosteroids in moderate to severe ARDS. [PMID 41495012]
- The ATS 2024 update provides recommendations for adult ARDS and stresses individual patient factors while further trials are awaited. [PMID 38032683]
- Electrical impedance tomography-guided PEEP titration has potential to prevent ventilator-induced lung injury, but larger studies are needed. [PMID 38512400]
- During surgery, high PEEP with recruitment manoeuvres had a high posterior probability of benefit on postoperative pulmonary complications versus low PEEP, particularly in laparoscopy. [PMID 39042027]
- Perioperative guidelines promote protective ventilation strategies with standardised definitions to reduce variability. [PMID 42612906]

**Uncertainties**
- Whether driving-pressure-guided PEEP improves mortality.

**MCQ angles**
- Low tidal volume and plateau pressure limits in ARDS.
- Recruitment manoeuvres harms (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Moderate ARDS with P/F 120 and plateau pressure 31.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Driving pressure targets and PEEP tables, and evidence from ART and ALVEOLI trials, are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38032683: An Update on Management of Adult Patients with Acute Respiratory Distress Syndrome: An Official American Thoracic Society Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38512400: Electrical impedance tomography-guided positive end-expiratory pressure titration in ARDS: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39042027: High Positive End-expiratory Pressure (PEEP) with Recruitment Maneuvers versus Low PEEP during General Anesthesia for Surgery: A Bayesian Individual Patient Data Meta-analysis of Three Randomized Clinical Trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41495012: Clinical Practice Guideline: Mechanical Ventilation and Extracorporeal Membrane Oxygenation in Acute Respiratory Insufficiency (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42612906: Consensus recommendations on perioperative mechanical ventilation strategies: an evidence-based guideline by the Brazilian Society of Anesthesiology (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `pegloticase` (topics 1303) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pegloticase is considered for refractory gout that fails conventional urate-lowering therapy, and lesinurad as an add-on uricosuric. Flare prophylaxis is advised during the first months of urate lowering.

**Teaching points**
- The 2016 EULAR recommendations advise flare prophylaxis with low-dose colchicine or NSAIDs during the first 6 months of urate-lowering therapy and that pegloticase can be considered in refractory gout. [PMID 28430170, 27457514]
- An international position paper defined the place of the uricosuric lesinurad when it was introduced. [PMID 30244431]
- A review of gout treatments highlights emerging targeted and biologic therapies that may help severe or refractory disease. [PMID 28658988]
- Cardiovascular effects of urate-lowering therapies in chronic gout showed no clear differences, but trials were short and had few events. [PMID 28379501]

**Uncertainties**
- Cardiovascular safety comparisons among urate-lowering drugs.

**MCQ angles**
- Pegloticase infusion reactions and anti-drug antibodies (not in abstracts).
- G6PD testing before pegloticase.

**Case hooks**
- Tophaceous gout with serum urate 600 despite maximal allopurinol and febuxostat.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Pegloticase dosing, infusion reaction prevention and methotrexate co-therapy are not stated in the retrieved abstracts; lesinurad has since been withdrawn.

**Sources**
- PMID 27457514: 2016 updated EULAR evidence-based recommendations for the management of gout (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 28379501: Cardiovascular effects of urate-lowering therapies in patients with chronic gout: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 28430170: Current management of gout: practical messages from 2016 EULAR guidelines (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 28658988: Updates on the treatment of gout, including a review of updated treatment guidelines and use of small molecule therapies for difficult-to-treat gout and gout flares (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 30244431: International position paper on the appropriate use of uricosurics with the introduction of lesinurad (guideline, 2018)

## Cluster `pid` (topics 1304, 1305) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pelvic inflammatory disease is treated with broad-spectrum antibiotics covering gonorrhoea, chlamydia, anaerobes and increasingly Mycoplasma genitalium. European and French guidelines differ in first-line regimens.

**Teaching points**
- The 2017 European guideline recommends testing for Mycoplasma genitalium in women with possible PID and male partners of confirmed cases, now recommends moxifloxacin as first-line (notably with M genitalium PID), suggests doxycycline for partners to limit macrolide exposure, and highlights MRI to exclude differential diagnoses. [PMID 29198181]
- French guidelines select ceftriaxone-doxycycline-metronidazole as first-line for uncomplicated PID, with moxifloxacin or levofloxacin/ofloxacin plus metronidazole as alternatives, and moxifloxacin for detected M genitalium. [PMID 30878689]
- A Cochrane review found azithromycin probably improves cure rates in mild-moderate PID compared with doxycycline (RR 1.35), and little or no difference between quinolones and cephalosporins for cure. [PMID 32820536]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal regimen as M genitalium macrolide resistance rises.

**MCQ angles**
- Fitz-Hugh-Curtis syndrome (not in abstracts).
- Treat partners and screen for gonorrhoea, chlamydia and M genitalium.

**Case hooks**
- Young woman with lower abdominal pain, cervical motion tenderness and fever.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic minimum criteria, admission criteria, IV regimens and follow-up are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29198181: 2017 European guideline for the management of pelvic inflammatory disease (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 30878689: [Treatment of uncomplicated pelvic inflammatory disease: CNGOF and SPILF Pelvic Inflammatory Diseases Guidelines] (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 32820536: Antibiotic therapy for pelvic inflammatory disease (meta-analysis, 2020)

## Cluster `nsclc_pembrolizumab` (topics 1306) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Adding pembrolizumab to platinum-pemetrexed chemotherapy improved overall and progression-free survival in untreated metastatic nonsquamous NSCLC without EGFR or ALK alterations (KEYNOTE-189), and combination therapy is favoured when rapid response is needed.

**Teaching points**
- KEYNOTE-189: in untreated metastatic nonsquamous NSCLC without EGFR or ALK mutations, adding pembrolizumab to pemetrexed and platinum gave estimated 12-month overall survival 69.2% versus 49.4% (HR for death 0.49). [PMID 29658856]
- A meta-analysis found pembrolizumab plus chemotherapy offered greater long-term overall survival than pembrolizumab alone in patients with PD-L1 expression of 1% to 49%. [PMID 39072325]
- A network meta-analysis suggests combination therapy can be considered when rapid response or prevention of rapid progression is needed. [PMID 35341985]
- Pembrolizumab plus chemotherapy was reported as possibly the optimal strategy for major advanced NSCLC with reliable safety, especially in nonsquamous disease. [PMID 34907661]
- Nivolumab plus ipilimumab showed trends toward long-term benefit with manageable toxicity compared with other immunotherapy combinations, and the best combination varies with PD-L1 level. [PMID 36669321, 36389713]

**Uncertainties**
- Best option at low PD-L1 expression.

**MCQ angles**
- Check EGFR, ALK and PD-L1 before first-line therapy.
- Immune-related adverse events.

**Case hooks**
- Metastatic lung adenocarcinoma, PD-L1 20%, no driver mutation: first-line choice.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Squamous histology data (KEYNOTE-407), PD-L1 TPS of at least 50% monotherapy and toxicity management are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29658856: Pembrolizumab plus Chemotherapy in Metastatic Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer (other, 2018)
- PMID 34907661: Efficacy and safety of anti-PD-1/PD-L1 in combination with chemotherapy or not as first-line treatment for advanced non-small cell lung cancer: A systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35341985: Pembrolizumab alone or with chemotherapy for metastatic non-small-cell lung cancer: A systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36389713: A systematic review and network meta-analysis of first-line immune checkpoint inhibitor combination therapies in patients with advanced non-squamous non-small cell lung cancer (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36669321: Long-term comparative efficacy and safety of nivolumab plus ipilimumab relative to other first-line therapies for advanced non-small-cell lung cancer: A systematic literature review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39072325: Comparative long-term outcomes of pembrolizumab plus chemotherapy versus pembrolizumab monotherapy as first-line therapy for metastatic non-small-cell lung cancer: a systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `penicillin_allergy` (topics 1307, 1308) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Most patients labelled penicillin-allergic are not allergic. Delabelling by direct oral challenge or structured testing is safe, can be done by non-allergists, and improves antibiotic choice.

**Teaching points**
- Penicillin allergy delabelling by non-allergists is efficacious and safe, with reported harm about 0%; the proportion delabelled rises with complexity of testing, but many can be delabelled without skin testing. [PMID 36450321]
- Reactions to direct penicillin challenges are infrequent and comparable to indirect challenges after allergy testing, so direct challenge is safe across age groups and settings. [PMID 39283610]
- BSACI guidance supports delabelling services run by non-allergists for low-risk patients, with a framework for drug provocation testing and separate adult and paediatric sections. [PMID 36128691]
- Hong Kong consensus supports testing by non-allergists, with low-risk cases managed at spoke clinics supported by an allergist-led hub, and removal of inaccurate labels after a negative provocation test with counselling. [PMID 36133403]
- Surgical patients with a penicillin allergy label can be assessed and tested by trained professionals meeting minimum safe-testing standards. [PMID 30916014]
- The reported global prevalence of penicillin allergy comes mainly from high-income countries and secondary care. [PMID 39874990]

**Uncertainties**
- Data from low-income settings.

**MCQ angles**
- Direct oral amoxicillin challenge for low-risk histories.
- Why delabelling improves antimicrobial stewardship.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with childhood rash on amoxicillin needing perioperative cefazolin.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Risk-stratification tools (PEN-FAST), management of severe delayed reactions and cephalosporin cross-reactivity are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30916014: Management of a surgical patient with a label of penicillin allergy: narrative review and consensus recommendations (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 36128691: BSACI guideline for the set-up of penicillin allergy de-labelling services by non-allergists working in a hospital setting (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36133403: Hong Kong Drug Allergy Delabelling Initiative (HK-DADI) consensus statements for penicillin allergy testing by nonallergists (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36450321: The effectiveness of interventions that support penicillin allergy assessment and delabeling of adult and pediatric patients by nonallergy specialists: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39283610: Reaction Risk to Direct Penicillin Challenges: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39874990: The global prevalence of reported penicillin allergy: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `peptic_ulcer` (topics 1309, 1310) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Peptic ulcer disease management centres on H. pylori eradication guided by local resistance, acid suppression, endoscopic haemostasis of bleeding ulcers and PPI protection in patients needing antiplatelets.

**Teaching points**
- A scoping review of H. pylori guidelines stresses that health authorities must update guidance in step with surveillance of local resistance patterns. [PMID 40346683]
- The ACG 2024 clinical practice guideline addresses treatment of H. pylori infection including salvage therapy and the role of susceptibility testing. [PMID 39626064]
- Vonoprazan-based triple therapy achieved high eradication rates in dual-resistant H. pylori and is a viable salvage option; an Egyptian consensus validates high-dose dual therapy. [PMID 41138484, 42412729]
- Guidelines for endoscopic management of non-variceal upper GI bleeding (second edition) pool new evidence, though levels of evidence and recommendations remain mostly low. [PMID 40114631]
- For patients with prior ulcer bleeding who need antiplatelet or anticoagulant therapy for cardiovascular prophylaxis, an international consensus suggests PPI therapy. [PMID 31634917]
- Japanese guidelines recommend PPIs or vonoprazan to prevent ulcer recurrence in patients with a history of ulcer on low-dose aspirin. [PMID 33620586]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal third-line regimens.

**MCQ angles**
- Test and treat H pylori; confirm eradication.
- Endoscopic therapy for high-risk stigmata then high-dose PPI (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Patient on aspirin with melaena and a Forrest Ib duodenal ulcer.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** First-line regimen composition, Forrest classification and rebleeding prevention are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 31634917: Management of Nonvariceal Upper Gastrointestinal Bleeding: Guideline Recommendations From the International Consensus Group (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 33620586: Evidence-based clinical practice guidelines for peptic ulcer disease 2020 (guideline, 2021)

## Cluster `perinatal_depression` (topics 1311) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Perinatal depression screening is under-implemented compared with guideline recommendations, and treatment options now include neurosteroids (brexanolone, zuranolone), updated pharmacotherapy guidance, exercise and psychological interventions.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review found a significant gap between recommendations for perinatal depression screening and their implementation, highlighting the need to integrate routine screening and referral into maternal care. [PMID 38733723]
- ACOG clinical guidance covers zuranolone and brexanolone for postpartum depression and replaces earlier practice advisory on zuranolone. [PMID 41066774]
- Updated guidance on pharmacologic treatment of perinatal depression summarises recommendations for nonpsychiatric clinicians. [PMID 41922074]
- A network meta-analysis supports stage-specific, volume-adjusted exercise prescriptions, with mind-body exercise more efficacious postpartum. [PMID 41531421]
- Antenatal depression increases the risk of postpartum depression, with about 12.8% of postpartum depression cases attributable to it, informing screening frequency and follow-up. [PMID 37742599]
- Non-pharmacological interventions delivered by trained health professionals may treat perinatal depression among adolescent mothers. [PMID 40081580]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal screening interval.

**MCQ angles**
- Screen with EPDS or PHQ-9 in pregnancy and postpartum.
- Antidepressant choice in breastfeeding (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Woman 3 weeks postpartum with low mood and intrusive thoughts.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Screening tools and thresholds, SSRI safety data and suicide risk assessment are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37742599: Antenatal depression across trimesters as a risk for postpartum depression and estimation of the fraction of postpartum depression attributable to antenatal depression: A systematic review and meta-analysis of cohort studies (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38733723: Gaps between current practice in perinatal depression screening and guideline recommendations: a systematic review (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40081580: Non-pharmacological interventions for perinatal depression and anxiety among adolescent mothers: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41066774: Zuranolone and Brexanolone for the Treatment of Postpartum Depression (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `periodic_paralysis` (topics 1312) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Periodic paralysis includes hypokalaemic and hyperkalaemic channelopathies and thyrotoxic forms. Treatment is trigger avoidance, potassium modification, diuretics and carbonic anhydrase inhibitors, with dichlorphenamide the best-supported drug.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis supports dichlorphenamide in periodic paralysis with high-certainty evidence for primary outcomes and consistent efficacy across aetiologies and ages, although side effects need monitoring and counselling. [PMID 41371188]
- Treatment of periodic paralysis includes behavioural avoidance of triggers, modification of potassium levels, diuretics and carbonic anhydrase inhibitors. [PMID 29125635]
- A systematic review of hypokalaemic periodic paralysis case reports found phenotypic differences linked to different mutations and a suboptimal response to common preventive treatment. [PMID 33085076]
- A genome-wide meta-analysis found susceptibility loci for thyrotoxic periodic paralysis, including variants near genes implicated in monogenic muscle paralysis (KCNJ2, TRIM2). [PMID 33105104]
- Mutation-specific alterations in NaV1.4 function explain the diverse phenotypes of skeletal muscle sodium channelopathies. [PMID 28939973]

**Uncertainties**
- Best preventive drug by genotype.

**MCQ angles**
- Low potassium during attacks in hypokalaemic periodic paralysis; careful potassium replacement.
- Thyrotoxic periodic paralysis in Asian men.

**Case hooks**
- Young man with morning flaccid weakness after a carbohydrate-rich meal and low potassium.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Attack treatment, precipitants and the hyperkalaemic variant's treatment details are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28939973: Sodium Channelopathies of Skeletal Muscle (review, 2018)
- PMID 29125635: Review of the Diagnosis and Treatment of Periodic Paralysis (review, 2018)
- PMID 33085076: [Hypokalemic periodic paralysis: a systematic review of published case reports] (systematic review, 2020)
- PMID 33105104: Genome-wide meta-analysis reveals novel susceptibility loci for thyrotoxic periodic paralysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 41371188: Safety and efficacy of dichlorphenamide in patients with periodic paralysis: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `periop_antithrombotic` (topics 1313, 1315, 1330) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Perioperative antithrombotic management balances thrombosis against bleeding. Evidence is limited for most questions; DOACs can be interrupted and resumed without heparin bridging, and procedure-specific guidance exists for interventional pain, emergency and gastrostomy procedures.

**Teaching points**
- The ACCP perioperative antithrombotic guideline notes uncertainty remains about best practice for most perioperative management questions. [PMID 35964704]
- The 2025 DOAC guidance states DOACs can be interrupted and resumed for procedures without bridging with LMWH. [PMID 40448969]
- A 2024 consensus on interventional pain procedures reached 100% acceptance on 16 of 18 final items for managing antiplatelet and anticoagulant therapy, with disagreement on two statements. [PMID 39133736]
- French emergency medicine recommendations provide up-to-date management of patients on anticoagulants in emergency settings with strong expert agreement. [PMID 40581261]
- The ASGE gastrostomy guideline calls for multidisciplinary discussion of bleeding versus cardiovascular risk in patients on antithrombotics. [PMID 39520459]
- Canadian radiology practice guidelines address periprocedural management of bleeding risk, aiming to avoid unnecessary delays and consultations. [PMID 41964243]

**Uncertainties**
- Bridging is rarely needed but data are limited.

**MCQ angles**
- No bridging for most DOAC interruptions.
- Continue aspirin for secondary prevention in most surgery (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Patient on apixaban for AF scheduled for colectomy: interruption plan.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Specific interruption durations by drug and renal function, mechanical valve and recent stent rules are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35964704: Perioperative Management of Antithrombotic Therapy: An American College of Chest Physicians Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 39133736: Perioperative Management of Antiplatelet and Anticoagulant Therapy in Patients Undergoing Interventional Techniques: 2024 Updated Guidelines from the American Society of Interventional Pain Physicians (ASIPP) (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39520459: American Society for Gastrointestinal Endoscopy guideline on gastrostomy feeding tubes: summary and recommendations (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40448969: 2025 Guidelines for direct oral anticoagulants: a practical guidance on the prescription, laboratory testing, peri-operative and bleeding management (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `periop_betablocker` (topics 1314) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Starting beta-blockers just before non-cardiac surgery increases harm: in POISE metoprolol cut myocardial infarction but increased death and stroke. Established beta-blocker therapy is generally continued rather than withdrawn.

**Teaching points**
- POISE: extended-release metoprolol started before non-cardiac surgery reduced a cardiac outcome (HR 0.73) but increased deaths (3.1% vs 2.3%) and strokes (1.0% vs 0.5%). [PMID 18479744]
- Beta-blockers do not improve perioperative outcomes in vascular and endovascular surgery in a meta-analysis. [PMID 28039238]
- After cardiac surgery, perioperative beta-blockers prevent postoperative atrial fibrillation but did not translate into improved clinical outcomes. [PMID 32252112]
- In CABG, nearly all patients without contraindications are candidates for perioperative beta-blocker therapy, with carvedilol the most beneficial in one review. [PMID 30037300]
- Observational data suggest beta-blocker exposure relates to 30-day all-cause mortality but not to 30-day MACE or stroke, and commentary highlights potential harm of perioperative withdrawal. [PMID 32169568, 29935568]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal handling in patients with established beta-blockade.

**MCQ angles**
- Do not start high-dose beta-blocker on the day of surgery.
- Continue chronic beta-blockers perioperatively.

**Case hooks**
- Patient on bisoprolol for 5 years scheduled for hip replacement.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Guideline class recommendations (continue versus initiate in high-risk patients) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 18479744: Effects of extended-release metoprolol succinate in patients undergoing non-cardiac surgery (POISE trial): a randomised controlled trial (other, 2008)
- PMID 28039238: Effect of beta-blockers on perioperative outcomes in vascular and endovascular surgery: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 29935568: Beta-blockers in noncardiac surgery: Did observational studies put us back on safe ground? (review, 2018)
- PMID 30037300: Rationale for administering beta-blocker therapy to patients undergoing coronary artery bypass surgery: a systematic review (systematic review, 2018)
- PMID 32169568: Beta-blocker exposure for short-term outcomes following non-cardiac surgery: A meta-analysis of observational studies (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 32252112: Perioperative Beta-Blocker for Atrial Fibrillation after Cardiac Surgery: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)

## Cluster `periop_risk` (topics 1316, 1317, 1319, 1320, 1321, 1498) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Perioperative risk assessment combines clinical risk indices such as the Revised Cardiac Risk Index, functional capacity, biomarkers and procedure-specific risk, as set out in the 2024 AHA/ACC guideline, to decide who needs further testing or optimisation.

**Teaching points**
- The Revised Cardiac Risk Index was derived and prospectively validated as a simple index that identifies higher-risk patients undergoing nonurgent major noncardiac surgery and outperformed other published indexes. [PMID 10477528]
- The 2024 AHA/ACC multisociety guideline provides evidence-based strategies for perioperative cardiovascular management in noncardiac surgery, including pharmacological therapy, monitoring and devices. [PMID 39316661, 39320289]
- ESAIC focused guidelines advise on perioperative use of cardiac troponin and natriuretic peptides in noncardiac surgery patients for three scopes of application. [PMID 37265332]
- The 2025 ESPEN surgery guideline adds recommendations for frailty assessment, sarcopenia diagnosis and prehabilitation. [PMID 40957230]
- EASL guidance advises individualised multidisciplinary risk assessment for extrahepatic abdominal surgery in cirrhosis. [PMID 40348682]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of routine biomarkers and stress testing.

**MCQ angles**
- RCRI items: ischaemic heart disease, heart failure, cerebrovascular disease, insulin treatment, creatinine above 2 mg/dL, high-risk surgery (not in abstracts).
- Functional capacity of 4 METs.

**Case hooks**
- 68-year-old with diabetes and prior MI before an elective hernia repair.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Numeric RCRI risk by score, stress testing indications and specific optimisation steps are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 10477528: Derivation and prospective validation of a simple index for prediction of cardiac risk of major noncardiac surgery (other, 1999)
- PMID 37265332: ESAIC focused guideline for the use of cardiac biomarkers in perioperative risk evaluation (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 39316661: 2024 AHA/ACC/ACS/ASNC/HRS/SCA/SCCT/SCMR/SVM Guideline for Perioperative Cardiovascular Management for Noncardiac Surgery: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `periop_diabetes` (topics 1318) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Perioperative diabetes management now has specific advice for newer drugs and devices: SGLT2 inhibitors should be omitted the day before and day of surgery, GLP-1 receptor agonists can be continued with aspiration risk mitigation, and pumps and sensors need planning.

**Teaching points**
- A multidisciplinary UK-led consensus advises continuing GLP-1 receptor agonists and dual GIP/GLP-1 agonists before surgery with full risk assessment and techniques to mitigate pulmonary aspiration, and omitting SGLT2 inhibitors the day before and the day of a procedure. [PMID 39781571]
- SPAQI guidance on SGLT2 inhibitors tailors management to diabetes and other comorbidities, fasting considerations and monitoring for euglycaemic ketoacidosis. [PMID 42067493]
- SPAQI consensus guides perioperative clinicians caring for adults wearing insulin pumps and continuous glucose monitors. [PMID 39388833]
- The SAMBA consensus statement updates perioperative blood glucose management in adults with diabetes undergoing ambulatory surgery, including newer therapies. [PMID 38517760]
- Joint British guidance on bariatric surgery stresses careful planning with diabetes teams and post-operative glucose monitoring, hypoglycaemia surveillance and medication adjustment. [PMID 41840846]

**Uncertainties**
- Aspiration risk with GLP-1 agonists is an evolving area.

**MCQ angles**
- Stop SGLT2 inhibitors before surgery to avoid euglycaemic DKA.
- Insulin pump management on the day of surgery.

**Case hooks**
- Patient on empagliflozin and semaglutide for elective cholecystectomy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Glucose targets, insulin infusion protocols and metformin handling are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38517760: Society for Ambulatory Anesthesia Updated Consensus Statement on Perioperative Blood Glucose Management in Adult Patients With Diabetes Mellitus Undergoing Ambulatory Surgery (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39388833: Perioperative Management of Adult Patients with Diabetes Wearing Devices: A Society for Perioperative Assessment and Quality Improvement (SPAQI) Expert Consensus Statement (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39781571: Elective peri-operative management of adults taking glucagon-like peptide-1 receptor agonists, glucose-dependent insulinotropic peptide agonists and sodium-glucose cotransporter-2 inhibitors: a multidisciplinary consensus statement: A consensus statement from the Association of Anaesthetists, Association of British Clinical Diabetologists, British Obesity and Metabolic Surgery Society, Centre for Perioperative Care, Joint British Diabetes Societies for Inpatient Care, Royal College of Anaesthetists, Society for Obesity and Bariatric Anaesthesia and UK Clinical Pharmacy Association (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41840846: Diabetes management in people undergoing metabolic-bariatric surgery: A guideline from the Joint British Diabetes Societies for Inpatient Care (JBDS-IP) Group (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42067493: Perioperative management of patients taking sodium-glucose cotransporter 2 inhibitors: Society for Perioperative Assessment and Quality Improvement (SPAQI) multidisciplinary consensus statement (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `ppcm` (topics 1323) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Peripartum cardiomyopathy is managed with standard heart failure therapy adapted for pregnancy and lactation, with bromocriptine as a possible adjunct that improves LV recovery but lacks proven mortality benefit. Subsequent pregnancy carries risk when LV dysfunction persists.

**Teaching points**
- Prolactin inhibition with bromocriptine added to standard therapy was associated with significant improvement in LVEF and greater odds of LV recovery, without significant reduction in all-cause mortality. [PMID 36261102]
- A separate meta-analysis found bromocriptine associated with higher survival and LVEF improvement but not with fewer composite adverse clinical outcomes, and called for larger randomised trials. [PMID 34725781]
- In a subsequent pregnancy, persistent LV dysfunction beforehand carries higher mortality than recovered function, and subsequent pregnancy was associated with worsening echo parameters. [PMID 38569669]
- Contrast-enhanced cardiac MRI can serve as an adjunct to stratify the risk of poor LV recovery in postpartum PPCM if performed at a suitable time. [PMID 39295149]
- Receipt of guideline-directed medical therapy in PPCM differs by race and cardiology involvement, so additional equity strategies are needed. [PMID 37870730]

**Uncertainties**
- Bromocriptine's role outside trials.

**MCQ angles**
- Counselling about future pregnancy after PPCM according to LVEF recovery.
- Bromocriptine suppresses lactation.

**Case hooks**
- Woman 3 weeks postpartum with dyspnoea, orthopnoea and LVEF 30%.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic definition, delivery timing, anticoagulation and drug safety in breastfeeding are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 34725781: Effects of bromocriptine in peripartum cardiomyopathy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36261102: Prolactin Inhibition in Peripartum Cardiomyopathy: Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37870730: Association Between Race, Cardiology Care, and the Receipt of Guideline-Directed Medical Therapy in Peripartum Cardiomyopathy (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38569669: Outcomes of subsequent pregnancy in women with peripartum cardiomyopathy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39295149: Meta-analysis of cardiac magnetic resonance in prognosticating left ventricular function in peripartum cardiomyopathy (meta-analysis, 2025)

Existing links for #1323 screened by title: papers kept 4, dropped 0; guideline sources kept 3, dropped 0.

## Cluster `pad` (topics 1324, 1325, 1326, 1327) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Lower extremity PAD care is guided by the 2024 ACC/AHA guideline, which adds recommendations for comprehensive care: risk-factor modification, antithrombotic therapy, exercise therapy and revascularisation chosen on risk, benefit and patient preference.

**Teaching points**
- The 2024 ACC/AHA multisociety PAD guideline updates the prior recommendations with new evidence and adds recommendations for comprehensive care of patients with lower extremity PAD. [PMID 38752899, 38743805]
- The SVS focused update on intermittent claudication calls for a thorough assessment of risk, benefits, degree of impairment and durability of treatment within a patient-centred approach. [PMID 40316185]
- An ACC scientific statement on PAD in adults with diabetes addresses earlier diagnosis, screening timing and methods, and standard trial endpoints. [PMID 41405527]
- Multicomponent interventions, particularly those supporting clinicians and patient access, were most effective at improving medication prescription and reducing lipids in PAD secondary prevention. [PMID 41260242]
- A national PAD guideline in England was associated with reduced revascularisation rates, with greater reduction for moderate PAD in more deprived areas. [PMID 39436641]
- A UK guideline covers CYP2C19 genotype testing for clopidogrel. [PMID 41338248]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal antithrombotic regimen is detailed in the full guideline rather than the abstracts.

**MCQ angles**
- Supervised exercise therapy for claudication.
- ABI below 0.9 diagnostic; non-compressible vessels in diabetes.

**Case hooks**
- Smoker with calf claudication at 200 m and ABI 0.65.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** ABI cut-offs, statin, rivaroxaban/aspirin dosing and revascularisation criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38743805: 2024 ACC/AHA/AACVPR/APMA/ABC/SCAI/SVM/SVN/SVS/SIR/VESS Guideline for the Management of Lower Extremity Peripheral Artery Disease: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38752899: 2024 ACC/AHA/AACVPR/APMA/ABC/SCAI/SVM/SVN/SVS/SIR/VESS Guideline for the Management of Lower Extremity Peripheral Artery Disease: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39436641: Impact of a national guideline for the management of peripheral arterial disease on revascularization rates in England: interrupted time series analysis (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `blood_smear` (topics 1328) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Peripheral blood film review remains a core diagnostic skill. Schistocyte counting by ICSH criteria supports thrombotic microangiopathy diagnosis, and a morphological finding can trigger urgent therapy.

**Teaching points**
- The 2021 ICSH recommendations update identification and quantitation of schistocytes and discuss the validity of microscope schistocyte counts and automated fragmented red cell counts in diagnosing thrombotic microangiopathies. [PMID 34431220]
- Australian and New Zealand TMA consensus notes that other TMA-associated conditions such as infection, pregnancy and malignant hypertension do not exclude TTP or aHUS as the underlying cause. [PMID 29582550]
- A diagnostic guideline for leukopenia states that agranulocytosis with fever requires admission and immediate broad-spectrum antibiotics to reduce mortality. [PMID 29145679]
- There is no evidence of benefit from therapeutic plasma exchange in snakebite-associated TMA, so it cannot be recommended. [PMID 33290400]
- A consensus defines curricular standards for peripheral blood smear review in haematology training. [PMID 36930800]

**Uncertainties**
- Standardisation of schistocyte counting.

**MCQ angles**
- Schistocytes with thrombocytopenia suggest TMA; urgent ADAMTS13 testing.
- Blood film findings in iron deficiency, megaloblastic anaemia and haemolysis (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Patient with fever, confusion, thrombocytopenia, anaemia and schistocytes on film.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** A systematic morphology atlas (red cell, white cell and platelet abnormalities) is not covered by the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29145679: [Leukopenia - A Diagnostic Guideline for the Clinical Routine] (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 29582550: Consensus opinion on diagnosis and management of thrombotic microangiopathy in Australia and New Zealand (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 33290400: Snakebite associated thrombotic microangiopathy: a systematic review of clinical features, outcomes, and evidence for interventions including plasmapheresis (systematic review, 2020)
- PMID 34431220: 2021 update of the 2012 ICSH Recommendations for identification, diagnostic value, and quantitation of schistocytes: Impact and revisions (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 36930800: Consensus recommendations on peripheral blood smear review: defining curricular standards and fellow competency (guideline, 2023)

## Cluster `pd_peritonitis` (topics 1331) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** PD-associated peritonitis is treated per the ISPD 2022 guideline, with prevention measures, culture-guided therapy, and early recognition of unusual pathogens such as mycobacteria and fungi, for which catheter removal may be needed.

**Teaching points**
- The 2022 ISPD peritonitis update includes a new recommendation on adjunctive oral N-acetylcysteine to mitigate aminoglycoside ototoxicity and suggests research areas in prevention and treatment. [PMID 35264029]
- Paediatric ISPD guidance (2024) updates prevention and management of PD-associated infections with new antibiotic susceptibility and stewardship data. [PMID 39313225]
- For nontuberculous mycobacterial PD infection, acid-fast stain and culture are advisable, and early PD catheter removal may be an essential management step. [PMID 39609738]
- Aspergillus peritonitis in PD is hard to diagnose, early antifungal treatment determines prognosis, and mortality remains high despite treatment. [PMID 32893119]
- A review of rare pathogens in PD peritonitis discusses recurrent peritonitis and treatment strategies to reduce morbidity. [PMID 38831580]

**Uncertainties**
- Empirical regimen choice by local resistance.

**MCQ angles**
- Cloudy effluent with white cell count above 100/mm3 and over 50% neutrophils (not in abstracts).
- Intraperitoneal antibiotics.

**Case hooks**
- PD patient with abdominal pain and cloudy dialysate.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria and empirical antibiotic regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32893119: Aspergillus peritonitis in peritoneal dialysis patients: A systematic review (systematic review, 2020)
- PMID 35264029: ISPD peritonitis guideline recommendations: 2022 update on prevention and treatment (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 38831580: Rare Pathogens in Peritoneal Dialysis-Associated Peritonitis: A Comprehensive Case Study and Guideline Review (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39313225: Clinical practice guideline for the prevention and management of peritoneal dialysis associated infections in children: 2024 update (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39609738: Systematic literature review of the diagnosis, prognosis, and treatment of peritoneal dialysis-related infection caused by nontuberculous mycobacteria (systematic review, 2024)

## Cluster `pd_adequacy` (topics 1332) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** PD prescription is individualised using adequacy assessment, with attention to catheter placement technique, training and complications. Evidence comparing automated and continuous ambulatory PD is limited.

**Teaching points**
- A GRADE-based clinical guideline covers adequacy and prescription of peritoneal dialysis. [PMID 39341764]
- The SAGES PD access guideline favours advanced laparoscopic catheter placement rather than basic or open insertion, and concomitant clean surgery with catheter placement rather than staged procedures. [PMID 37989887]
- Studies comparing automated with continuous ambulatory PD are limited, and future research should address residual kidney function, volume status and patient-reported outcomes. [PMID 39258519]
- Catheter insertion technique influences catheter function and clinical outcomes in PD. [PMID 36810986]
- An ISPD position paper addresses teaching PD to patients, highlighting educational practices and nurse competencies. [PMID 40966019]

**Uncertainties**
- Targets for small-solute clearance and their clinical relevance.

**MCQ angles**
- Residual kidney function preservation in PD.
- Peritoneal equilibration test types (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Patient starting PD: modality and prescription.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Adequacy targets (Kt/V), dialysate options and ultrafiltration failure are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36810986: Catheter insertion techniques for improving catheter function and clinical outcomes in peritoneal dialysis patients (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37989887: SAGES peritoneal dialysis access guideline update 2023 (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39258519: Automated peritoneal dialysis versus continuous ambulatory peritoneal dialysis for people with kidney failure (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39341764: Clinical guideline on adequacy and prescription of peritoneal dialysis (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40966019: Teaching peritoneal dialysis: A position paper for the International Society for Peritoneal Dialysis (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `peritoneal_disorders` (topics 1333) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The retrieved evidence concerns peritoneal surface malignancy and carcinomatosis rather than benign peritoneal disorders. Cytoreductive surgery with HIPEC and multidisciplinary consensus pathways are the main themes.

**Teaching points**
- HIPEC may improve survival and reduce recurrence in advanced gastric cancer without significantly increasing complications, per a meta-analysis. [PMID 37158149]
- Consensus guidelines address malignant gastrointestinal obstruction in peritoneal surface malignancy, gastric cancer with synchronous peritoneal metastases, and appendiceal tumours with peritoneal involvement. [PMID 40560502, 40560499, 40558065]

- A consensus addresses peritoneal sarcomatosis. [PMID 39123374]

- Topic scope is unclear.

**MCQ angles**

**Case hooks**

## Cluster `pertussis` (topics 1334) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pertussis control relies on vaccination, including in pregnancy to protect unvaccinated newborns, and on timely diagnosis and antibiotic treatment of cases. Maternal immunisation may modestly blunt infant vaccine responses but remains effective.

**Teaching points**
- Maternal pertussis immunisation may blunt routine infant vaccine effectiveness, but is predicted to remain effective in protecting unvaccinated newborns, supporting current recommendations. [PMID 38297003]
- A consensus statement reviews global pertussis vaccination in pregnancy and strategies for obstetric providers. [PMID 38251722]
- Global Pertussis Initiative recommendations for Eastern and Northern Europe call for aligned national policies, scaled-up immunisation in pregnancy and a life-long immunisation approach. [PMID 41922871]
- An expert consensus on paediatric pertussis gives new concepts in diagnosis and treatment, including intravenous options for severe cases. [PMID 39537933]
- Current pertussis vaccines provide scarce protection against Bordetella parapertussis infection in children. [PMID 38543887]

**Uncertainties**
- Duration of protection from acellular vaccines.

**MCQ angles**
- Tdap in each pregnancy.
- Macrolide treatment and post-exposure prophylaxis (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- 6-week-old infant with paroxysmal cough, apnoea and lymphocytosis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Clinical stages, macrolide regimens, infectious period and contact prophylaxis are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38251722: The current state of pertussis vaccination in pregnancy around the world, with recommendations for improved care: Consensus statements from the Global Pertussis Initiative (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38297003: Maternal pertussis immunization and the blunting of routine vaccine effectiveness: a meta-analysis and modeling study (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38543887: Pertussis Vaccines Scarcely Provide Protection against <i>Bordetella parapertussis</i> Infection in Children-A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39537933: Expert consensus for pertussis in children: new concepts in diagnosis and treatment (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 41922871: The Ongoing Challenge of Pertussis in Eastern and Northern Europe: Recommendations from the Global Pertussis Initiative (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `phage_therapy` (topics 1335) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Bacteriophage therapy is an emerging option for refractory or resistant infections. Clinical evidence is promising but limited, and a new consensus guideline sets out standardised use of personalised phage therapy.

**Teaching points**
- A consensus-based guideline provides clear, practice-oriented recommendations for the safe and standardised use of personalised bacteriophage therapy. [PMID 42768217]
- A meta-analysis of customised and non-customised phage therapy in refractory or resistant infections reports promising outcomes but significant gaps in clinical evidence, needing well-designed trials. [PMID 40633848]
- In hip and knee periprosthetic joint infection, evidence for phage therapy is limited and methodologically weak. [PMID 41562899]
- A systematic review of phage therapy for urinary tract infections reports uncertainty about dosing and administration and calls for randomised trials. [PMID 40435529]
- A review of phage therapy for critical antibiotic-resistant Gram-positive bacteria identifies implementation obstacles and research directions. [PMID 40424685]

**Uncertainties**
- Dosing, resistance to phage and regulatory pathways.

**MCQ angles**
- Phage therapy as compassionate-use adjunct for resistant infections.

**Case hooks**
- Prosthetic joint infection with multidrug-resistant organism, antibiotic options exhausted.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Mechanisms, specific trials and other novel antimicrobials named in the topic are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40424685: Bacteriophage therapy for critical antibiotic-resistant Gram-positive bacteria: A systematic review of clinical researches (systematic review, 2025)
- PMID 40435529: Phage therapy in patients with urinary tract infections: a systematic review (systematic review, 2026)
- PMID 40633848: Clinical application of customized and non-customized bacteriophage therapy in patients with refractory/resistant bacterial infections: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `pk_antibiotics_icu` (topics 1336) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Critical illness alters antibiotic pharmacokinetics, so personalised dosing, extended or continuous beta-lactam infusion and therapeutic drug monitoring are advocated. Aggressive PK/PD target attainment improves clinical efficacy.

**Teaching points**
- The French beta-lactam guideline strongly suggests personalised dosing, continuous or prolonged infusion and therapeutic drug monitoring for beta-lactams in critically ill patients. [PMID 30925922]
- A position paper states antimicrobial TDM should be the standard of care for most antimicrobials in every ICU, though barriers must be addressed. [PMID 32383061]
- A meta-analysis found beta-lactam TDM-guided dosing improved clinical and microbiological cure and treatment response in critically ill patients, with larger randomised trials still needed. [PMID 35731853]
- Attaining aggressive rather than conservative PK/PD targets gave significant clinical benefit for beta-lactams in Gram-negative infections. [PMID 38627763]
- Continuous infusion of vancomycin was associated with 53% lower odds of acute kidney injury and 2.6-fold higher odds of PK target attainment than intermittent infusion, without affecting mortality. [PMID 32317590]
- Continuous or extended-infusion beta-lactams with a loading dose may significantly improve clinical outcomes in sepsis or septic shock. [PMID 33135261]

**Uncertainties**
- Mortality benefit of TDM and continuous infusion.

**MCQ angles**
- Loading dose then extended infusion for time-dependent antibiotics.
- Augmented renal clearance in critical illness.

**Case hooks**
- Septic ICU patient with augmented renal clearance and piperacillin-tazobactam failure.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Specific PK/PD targets (for example 100% fT>MIC), dose adjustments for renal replacement therapy and aminoglycoside strategies are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30925922: Optimization of the treatment with beta-lactam antibiotics in critically ill patients-guidelines from the French Society of Pharmacology and Therapeutics (Société Française de Pharmacologie et Thérapeutique-SFPT) and the French Society of Anaesthesia and Intensive Care Medicine (Société Française d'Anesthésie et Réanimation-SFAR) (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 32317590: Continuous Versus Intermittent Infusion of Vancomycin and the Risk of Acute Kidney Injury in Critically Ill Adults: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 32383061: Antimicrobial therapeutic drug monitoring in critically ill adult patients: a Position Paper<sub>.</sub> (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 33135261: Loading dose and efficacy of continuous or extended infusion of beta-lactams compared with intermittent administration in patients with critical illnesses: A subgroup meta-analysis and meta-regression analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35731853: Beta-Lactam Antibiotic Therapeutic Drug Monitoring in Critically Ill Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 38627763: Impact of attaining aggressive vs. conservative PK/PD target on the clinical efficacy of beta-lactams for the treatment of Gram-negative infections in the critically ill patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `fibromyalgia_pharm` (topics 1337) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Drug treatment of fibromyalgia has modest effects: duloxetine, pregabalin and amitriptyline are the main options, with network meta-analyses showing similar efficacy and differing tolerability, so choice is tailored to symptoms.

**Teaching points**
- A network meta-analysis comparing amitriptyline with FDA-approved treatments suggests clinicians tailor treatment to individual symptoms, weighing benefits and acceptability. [PMID 35587348]
- An overview with network meta-analysis of amitriptyline, duloxetine and pregabalin cautions that tolerability and safety are important to decision-making. [PMID 35347488]
- Duloxetine was more effective than placebo in fibromyalgia, and dose should be customised to the individual. [PMID 37461044]
- A network meta-analysis of antidepressants for chronic pain found moderate efficacy for duloxetine at standard dose, with promising but less certain evidence for milnacipran. [PMID 39367772]
- A consensus statement describes pharmacological and non-pharmacological fibromyalgia treatment, noting CBT and non-invasive brain stimulation may improve disability. [PMID 39982626]

**Uncertainties**
- Comparative long-term benefit and harms.

**MCQ angles**
- Duloxetine, pregabalin and amitriptyline as options; opioids avoided (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Woman with fibromyalgia and insomnia not responding to exercise.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Doses, adverse effects and specific recommendation strengths from EULAR or ACR are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35347488: Comparative efficacy of amitriptyline, duloxetine and pregabalin for treating fibromyalgia in adults: an overview with network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35587348: Comparison of Amitriptyline and US Food and Drug Administration-Approved Treatments for Fibromyalgia: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)

## Cluster `ph_all` (topics 1338) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Philadelphia-positive ALL is now treated with potent third-generation TKIs and blinatumomab, often sparing allogeneic transplantation in MRD-negative patients. MRD is a key prognostic and decision tool.

**Teaching points**
- Introduction of third-generation TKIs and blinatumomab has significantly improved outcomes in newly diagnosed Ph+ ALL while sparing the need for HSCT in some patients. [PMID 36682989]
- Ponatinib was associated with significantly better survival outcomes than first- and second-generation TKIs, although limited safety data call for randomised trials. [PMID 40517974]
- Chemotherapy-free regimens of blinatumomab plus TKIs and steroids achieved promising survival rates, so the need for chemotherapy or transplant requires study. [PMID 38972767]
- Chemotherapy with next-generation TKIs gave comparable survival to allogeneic HSCT in MRD-negative (complete molecular response) patients, informing transplant indication in first remission. [PMID 36882308]
- Minimal residual disease is a potent clinical tool for prognosis in Ph+ ALL, and NGS can be used to test for BCR-ABL1 kinase domain mutations. [PMID 34437635, 32154668]

**Uncertainties**
- Whether transplant is needed for MRD-negative patients.

**MCQ angles**
- T315I mutation and ponatinib.
- MRD-guided decisions about transplantation.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with newly diagnosed Ph+ B-ALL: induction and MRD monitoring.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Induction regimens, TKI dosing and CNS prophylaxis are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32154668: Next-generation sequencing for BCR-ABL1 kinase domain mutations in adult patients with Philadelphia chromosome-positive acute lymphoblastic leukemia: A position paper (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 34437635: Association of minimal residual disease with clinical outcomes in Philadelphia chromosome positive acute lymphoblastic leukemia in the tyrosine kinase inhibitor era: A systemic literature review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 36682989: Outcomes of Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors Maintenance Therapy with or without Allogeneic Hematopoietic Stem Cell Transplantation in Philadelphia Chromosome Positive Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia in First Complete Remission: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 36882308: Chemotherapy with the use of next-generation tyrosine kinase inhibitors based on measurable residual disease has the potential to avoid hematopoietic stem cell transplantation in treatment for adults with Philadelphia chromosome-positive acute lymphoblastic leukemia (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38972767: Efficacy of Chemotherapy-Free Regimens in the Treatment of Philadelphia Chromosome-Positive Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40517974: Comparison of third-generation tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI) ponatinib with first- and second-generation TKIs for treatment of Philadelphia chromosome-positive acute lymphoblastic leukemia: A systematic review and bias-corrected meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `ffr_pci` (topics 1339, 1340) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Physiology-guided PCI uses FFR or non-hyperaemic ratios to target ischaemia-causing lesions. FFR-guided PCI reduced procedural events compared with angiography alone, and coronary CT-derived FFR is entering practice.

**Teaching points**
- An individual patient data meta-analysis found FFR-guided PCI reduced major adverse events in chronic coronary syndrome and NSTE-ACS, mainly through fewer peri-procedural MIs, with no differences in mortality or MACE beyond 30 days. [PMID 40831380]
- A European consensus statement describes applied coronary physiology for procedural PCI planning, disease pattern recognition and post-PCI optimisation. [PMID 37171503]
- Higher post-PCI FFR is associated with better prognosis, but predictive capacity differs by artery, being poor in the LAD and moderate in non-LAD vessels. [PMID 37821185, 36026514]
- Lesions with discordant FFR and non-hyperaemic pressure ratios (FFR positive, NHPR negative) may benefit from revascularisation, requiring further investigation. [PMID 40669964]
- Expert consensus on coronary CT-derived FFR states it can reduce unnecessary invasive angiography and guide targeted revascularisation. [PMID 41686239]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term mortality benefit of FFR guidance.

**MCQ angles**
- FFR below 0.80 indicates a significant stenosis.
- Post-PCI FFR as an optimisation target.

**Case hooks**
- Intermediate 60% LAD stenosis on angiography in stable angina.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** FFR thresholds, FAME trial data and adenosine protocol are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36026514: Predictive value of post-percutaneous coronary intervention fractional flow reserve: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37171503: Applied coronary physiology for planning and guidance of percutaneous coronary interventions. A clinical consensus statement from the European Association of Percutaneous Cardiovascular Interventions (EAPCI) of the European Society of Cardiology (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37821185: Impact of Post-PCI FFR Stratified by Coronary Artery (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 40669964: Outcomes of Lesions With Discordance Between FFR and Nonhyperemic Pressure Ratios (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40831380: Fractional flow reserve vs angiography to guide percutaneous coronary intervention: an individual patient data meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41686239: Clinical use of coronary computed tomography angiography-derived fractional flow reserve: expert consensus by an International Working Group (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `pituitary_adenoma` (topics 1341, 1432) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Prolactinomas are treated first with dopamine agonists, with surgery a reasonable alternative in small, non-invasive tumours. Paediatric and adult consensus guidelines stress specialist-centre management.

**Teaching points**
- The Pituitary Society international consensus covers diagnosis and management of prolactin-secreting adenomas and notes needs for additional therapeutic options and international registries. [PMID 37670148]
- Transsphenoidal surgery for prolactinomas may be particularly effective in small, non-invasive, treatment-naive tumours and may be a viable first-line alternative to dopamine agonists in such patients. [PMID 34325023]
- Italian guidelines for prolactinoma management address endocrinologists, neurosurgeons and gynaecologists and include options such as temozolomide for aggressive tumours. [PMID 37171003]
- Consensus guidelines for paediatric pituitary adenomas recommend surgery in specialist centres and collection of outcome data for new modalities. [PMID 38336897, 38336898]
- Cabergoline prevented tumour progression after surgery in about half of non-functioning pituitary adenoma cases with few adverse events, though tumour shrinkage was uncommon. [PMID 35902444]

**Uncertainties**
- Duration of dopamine agonist therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Cabergoline as first-line for macroprolactinoma.
- Pituitary stalk effect versus prolactinoma by prolactin level.

**Case hooks**
- Woman with amenorrhoea, galactorrhoea and prolactin 3,000 mU/L.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Prolactin thresholds, dose schedules, withdrawal criteria and pregnancy management are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 34325023: Determinants of Surgical Remission in Prolactinomas: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35902444: Treatment of non-functioning pituitary adenoma with cabergoline: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37171003: Italian Guidelines for the Management of Prolactinomas (guideline, 2023)

## Cluster `pituitary_apoplexy` (topics 1342) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pituitary apoplexy needs urgent endocrine replacement and neuro-ophthalmic assessment. Patients without severe visual or neurological deficits can be managed expectantly, whereas visual field defects and ocular palsy favour surgery.

**Teaching points**
- For patients presenting without severe visual or other neurological deficits, expectant management is recommended, individualised to symptom severity at onset. [PMID 39503840]
- A meta-analysis suggests surgical intervention should be advocated for apoplexy with visual field defects and ocular palsy. [PMID 27772771]
- A meta-analysis of 908 patients found surgical and conservative interventions provide comparable outcomes, though more severe cases typically undergo surgery and data are observational. [PMID 39033812]
- A meta-analysis of vision outcomes in early versus late surgery suggests conservative management may be warranted for early stabilisation before surgery. [PMID 30922898]
- Treatment of apoplexy is tailored to the individual case, and visual and endocrine recovery have been reported after both conservative and surgical treatment. [PMID 31470146]

**Uncertainties**
- Timing of surgery.

**MCQ angles**
- Hydrocortisone replacement first.
- Visual field and acuity assessment.

**Case hooks**
- Sudden severe headache, bitemporal hemianopia and hypotension in a patient with a known macroadenoma.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Presentation, MRI findings and emergency steroid dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27772771: Surgical versus non-surgical treatment for pituitary apoplexy: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 30922898: Vision Outcomes in Early versus Late Surgical Intervention of Pituitary Apoplexy: Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 31470146: Visual and Endocrine Recovery Following Conservative and Surgical Treatment of Pituitary Apoplexy: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 39033812: Pituitary Apoplexy and the Current Understanding of Its Management: A Meta-Analysis of 908 Patients (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39503840: The role of intervention timing and treatment modality in visual recovery following pituitary apoplexy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `plantar_fasciitis` (topics 1343) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Plantar fasciitis is a clinical diagnosis managed conservatively with orthoses, taping, stretching and injection options. Platelet-rich plasma gives better medium-term pain control than corticosteroid but not short-term or at one year.

**Teaching points**
- Platelet-rich plasma injections provided superior pain control to corticosteroid at 3 to 6 months but not in the short term or at one year. [PMID 39778212]
- Customised orthoses and taping reduced pain intensity in the short term in plantar fasciitis, though evidence is of low to moderate quality. [PMID 37620126]
- Dry needling was reported to reduce pain and disability in plantar heel pain in the short and long term, but results should be treated with caution because of few trials. [PMID 33760098]
- Photobiomodulation was superior to ESWT for pain relief but not superior to other interventions, and the evidence does not support it as an adjunct to other electrotherapy. [PMID 37464155]
- A Singapore consensus consolidates statements into a treatment pathway for plantar fasciitis, and a review of medical imaging for plantar heel pain summarises imaging findings. [PMID 38920234, 35065676]

**Uncertainties**
- Standardisation of PRP preparation.

**MCQ angles**
- Heel pain worst with first steps in the morning.
- Calcaneal spur is often incidental.

**Case hooks**
- Runner with medial heel pain after rest.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Examination findings, plantar fascia thickness thresholds and stretching programmes are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33760098: Is Dry Needling Effective for the Management of Plantar Heel Pain or Plantar Fasciitis? An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35065676: Medical imaging for plantar heel pain: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37464155: Effects of photobiomodulation therapy (PBMT) on the management of pain intensity and disability in plantar fasciitis: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37620126: Efficacy of pharmacological and non-pharmacological therapies on pain intensity and disability for plantar fasciitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `psoriasis` (topics 1344, 1447, 1448) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Moderate-to-severe psoriasis is treated with biologics, and IL-17 and IL-23 inhibitors show long drug survival. Routine latent TB testing is not required before IL-17 or IL-23 inhibitors, and dose reduction and switching strategies are emerging.

**Teaching points**
- A joint NPF and IPC position statement reached consensus that routine testing for latent TB infection is not required in psoriasis patients treated with IL-17 or IL-23 inhibitors. [PMID 41260526]
- IL-17 and IL-23 inhibitors show drug survival that depends on effect modifiers such as biologic naivety and data source. [PMID 38630365]
- A meta-analysis on switching biologics found adalimumab, ixekizumab and risankizumab preferred for intraclass switching and guselkumab the primary agent for interclass transitions. [PMID 40545752]
- The first international consensus on dose reduction of biologics for psoriasis judged dose reduction feasible, though underlying evidence is still limited. [PMID 42012299]
- European-adapted S3 and French guidelines (2025-2026) give treatment recommendations, monitoring and advice for comorbid situations, pregnancy planning and special types of psoriasis. [PMID 41531416, 41641952, 41469898]
- A network meta-analysis of biologics in paediatric plaque psoriasis found trends towards improved CDLQI and PASI75 responses and called for better-powered trials. [PMID 41090545]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal dose-reduction criteria.

**MCQ angles**
- PASI and DLQI for severity.
- Choosing biologic in psoriatic arthritis versus plaque disease.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with PASI 15 failing methotrexate.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic features, topical therapy, severity thresholds and conventional systemic agents are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38630365: Drug Survival of IL-17 and IL-23 Inhibitors for Psoriasis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40545752: Switching biologic agent in patients with psoriasis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41090545: Biologics for treatment of paediatric plaque psoriasis: A systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41260526: Joint position statement from the National Psoriasis Foundation Medical Board and the International Psoriasis Council on routine testing for latent tuberculosis infection prior to and during treatment of psoriasis patients with interleukin 17 or interleukin 23 inhibitors (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `plasma_cell_leukaemia` (topics 1345) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Plasma cell leukaemia is defined by 5% or more circulating plasma cells on peripheral blood smear in symptomatic myeloma and requires intensive, personalised treatment. Evidence comes from consensus rather than trials.

**Teaching points**
- The IMWG consensus defines plasma cell leukaemia by 5% or more circulating plasma cells on blood smear in patients otherwise diagnosed with symptomatic multiple myeloma. [PMID 34857730]
- European Myeloma Network recommendations advise that transplant-ineligible patients need a personalised intensive therapeutic approach, with allogeneic transplantation in selected patients. [PMID 30038381]
- A 2025 European Myeloma Network review and consensus statement on primary PCL strongly supports clinical trials and biological studies using novel technologies. [PMID 39924085]
- Chinese expert consensus standardises flow cytometric detection of circulating plasma cells and diagnosis and treatment of PCL. [PMID 38951057, 41087176]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal induction and consolidation.

**MCQ angles**
- Aggressive course with high-risk cytogenetics.
- Definition by circulating plasma cell percentage.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with myeloma, hypercalcaemia and 12% plasma cells on the blood film.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Treatment regimens (proteasome inhibitor-based, daratumumab, transplant) and survival data are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30038381: European myeloma network recommendations on diagnosis and management of patients with rare plasma cell dyscrasias (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 34857730: Primary plasma cell leukemia: consensus definition by the International Myeloma Working Group according to peripheral blood plasma cell percentage (guideline, 2021)
