## Cluster `platelet_refractoriness` (topics 1346) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Platelet transfusion refractoriness has immune causes (anti-HLA antibodies) and non-immune causes such as fever; HLA matching and avoiding sensitisation are central. Pathogen-reduced platelets may increase transfusion needs and refractoriness.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis identified fever (OR 2.26) among important risk factors for platelet transfusion refractoriness. [PMID 36856992]
- Anti-HLA antibody formation increases the chance of platelet refractoriness, and the review suggests approaches for HLA matching and for preventing sensitisation. [PMID 40245603]
- Pathogen-reduced platelets may require more transfusions in shorter intervals and may increase refractoriness and alloimmunisation. [PMID 35745493]
- Neonatal restrictive platelet transfusion guidelines address refractoriness and its association with poor outcomes. [PMID 39009717]
- In Glanzmann thrombasthenia anti-alpha-IIb-beta-3 immunisation is a cause of refractoriness, with risk factors discussed. [PMID 29611179]

**Uncertainties**
- Best product for sensitised patients.

**MCQ angles**
- 1-hour and 24-hour corrected count increments (not in abstracts).
- HLA-matched platelets for alloimmune refractoriness.

**Case hooks**
- Leukaemia patient with poor platelet increments after two transfusions.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Definitions of refractoriness and an investigation algorithm are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29611179: Anti-α<sub>IIb</sub> β<sub>3</sub> immunization in Glanzmann thrombasthenia: review of literature and treatment recommendations (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 35745493: Efficacy and Safety of Pathogen-Reduced Platelets Compared with Standard Apheresis Platelets: A Systematic Review of RCTs (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 36856992: A meta-analysis of risk factors associated with platelet transfusion refractoriness (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39009717: Implementing evidence-based restrictive neonatal intensive care unit platelet transfusion guidelines (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40245603: Anti-HLA antibody formation increases the chances of platelet refractoriness in platelet-transfused patients: a systematic review with meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `storage_pool` (topics 1347) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Platelet storage pool disease (delta-SPD) causes mild to moderate mucocutaneous bleeding from deficient dense-granule contents (ADP/ATP, serotonin). Diagnosis uses a stepwise approach for bleeding of unknown cause, and many patients remain undiagnosed.

**Teaching points**
- A review of platelet delta-storage pool disease covers dense-granule contents (ADP/ATP, serotonin) and tools available for diagnosis of different forms. [PMID 32759727]
- Many patients with inherited platelet diseases and normal platelet count remain without a diagnosis, requiring new diagnostic strategies. [PMID 33147934]
- A stepwise diagnostic procedure for mild to moderate bleeding disorders and bleeding disorder of unknown cause is summarised in a 2024 review. [PMID 38454298]
- Management of inherited platelet disorders aims to minimise bleeding risk and achieve haemostasis when needed, with platelet transfusion a crucial component in many. [PMID 33082057]
- Bernard-Soulier syndrome should be considered with persistent macrothrombocytopenia, family history, ITP unresponsiveness and absent ristocetin-induced aggregation. [PMID 39191409]

**Uncertainties**
- Genetic basis of most storage pool disorders.

**MCQ angles**
- Absent second wave aggregation or reduced dense granules on electron microscopy (not in abstracts).
- Desmopressin and antifibrinolytics.

**Case hooks**
- Young woman with easy bruising, heavy periods and normal platelet count and PT/aPTT.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Light transmission aggregometry patterns and treatment of bleeds (desmopressin, tranexamic acid) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32759727: Platelet δ-Storage Pool Disease: An Update (review, 2020)
- PMID 33082057: Inherited Platelet Disorders: Diagnosis and Management (review, 2020)
- PMID 33147934: Inherited platelet diseases with normal platelet count: phenotypes, genotypes and diagnostic strategy (review, 2021)
- PMID 38454298: How to investigate mild to moderate bleeding disorders and bleeding disorder of unknown cause (review, 2024)
- PMID 39191409: Bernard-Soulier Syndrome: A Review of Epidemiology, Molecular Pathology, Clinical Features, Laboratory Diagnosis, and Therapeutic Management (review, 2025)

## Cluster `platelet_thresholds` (topics 1348) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 AABB/ICTMG guideline supports restrictive platelet transfusion strategies, which reduce adverse reactions and conserve platelets, and ASCO provides threshold guidance for cancer patients.

**Teaching points**
- The 2025 AABB and ICTMG guidelines found a consistent pattern of evidence supporting restrictive platelet transfusion strategies, which reduce adverse reactions. [PMID 40440268]
- The ASCO update on platelet transfusion in cancer patients also addresses platelet product production, prevention of Rh alloimmunisation and management of refractoriness. [PMID 29182495]
- The ESICM guideline gives evidence-based recommendations on transfusion strategies in non-bleeding critically ill adults. [PMID 31912207]
- A quality improvement implementation of a neonatal restrictive platelet transfusion guideline reduced non-indicated transfusions without a change in major bleeding. [PMID 33758388]
- The BSH aplastic anaemia guideline recommends multidisciplinary discussion and expert-centre involvement for management. [PMID 38247114]

**Uncertainties**
- Thresholds vary by clinical setting.

## Cluster `pleurodesis` (topics 1351) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Malignant pleural effusion can be controlled with indwelling pleural catheters or pleurodesis; catheters shorten stay and reduce repeat procedures but raise cellulitis risk. Patient preference and cost guide choice.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found indwelling pleural catheters have shorter length of stay and fewer repeat pleural procedures than pleurodesis but a higher risk of cellulitis. [PMID 30272486]
- The ATS/STS/STR guideline gives recommendations for management of malignant pleural effusions, including managing catheter-associated infections with antibiotics without removing the catheter. [PMID 30272503]
- Complications of indwelling pleural catheters occur, with infection the most common. [PMID 36253892]
- In spontaneous pneumothorax, ERS/EACTS/ESTS guidelines make 24 recommendations and highlight evidence gaps for persistent air leak and recurrence prevention. [PMID 38804185, 38806203]
- A Chinese consensus recommends systemic anticancer treatment for patients with good performance status and metastatic malignancy, with specific advice for loculated effusion. [PMID 38044047]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal pleurodesis agent.

**MCQ angles**
- Talc pleurodesis versus IPC for trapped lung.
- IPC for ambulatory management.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with recurrent malignant effusion wishing to avoid admissions.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Pleurodesis agents, talc dosing and technique (slurry versus poudrage) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30272486: Indwelling Pleural Catheter versus Pleurodesis for Malignant Pleural Effusions. A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 30272503: Management of Malignant Pleural Effusions. An Official ATS/STS/STR Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 36253892: Incidence of complications from indwelling pleural catheter for pleural effusion: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `pcp` (topics 1352) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pneumocystis pneumonia diagnosis combines clinical context with beta-D-glucan and PCR testing, where PCR cannot always separate infection from colonisation. TMP-SMX remains the first-line prophylaxis agent in HIV.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of the Fungitell serum beta-D-glucan assay suggests a categorical approach using 400 pg/mL to rule in PCP according to pretest probability, needing external validation. [PMID 39536824]
- In a diagnostic meta-analysis, PCR (qPCR) difficulty in distinguishing infection from colonisation means a standardised-protocol randomised trial is needed to assess its clinical effect. [PMID 39900713]
- In HIV, trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole prophylaxis remains first-line given its mortality benefit; optimal dosing to maximise efficacy and limit toxicity needs study. [PMID 38583518]
- British Society for Medical Mycology recommendations update diagnosis of serious fungal diseases for clinicians and laboratories. [PMID 41232547]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal PCR thresholds.

**MCQ angles**
- Raised LDH and bilateral ground-glass opacities in an HIV patient with CD4 below 200.
- Steroids for PCP with hypoxaemia (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- HIV patient with exertional dyspnoea, dry cough and desaturation on exercise.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Treatment regimen, adjunctive corticosteroids and non-HIV risk groups are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38583518: Comparative efficacy and safety of Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia prophylaxis regimens for people living with HIV: a systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39536824: Diagnostic test accuracy of the Fungitell serum (1→3)-β-D-glucan assay for the diagnosis of Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39900713: Diagnostic tests performance in detecting Pneumocystis jirovecii: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41232547: British Society for Medical Mycology best practice recommendations for the diagnosis of serious fungal diseases: 2025 update (guideline, 2026)

Existing links for #1352 screened by title: papers kept 5, dropped 0; guideline sources kept 4, dropped 1.

## Cluster `pneumonia` (topics 1353) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 ATS community-acquired pneumonia guideline covers diagnostics, antibiotic duration and corticosteroids. In hospitalised non-viral CAP corticosteroids probably reduce short-term mortality and ventilation, and guideline-concordant antibiotics lower 30-day mortality.

**Teaching points**
- The ATS guideline for adult CAP provides recommendations on selected diagnostic and treatment strategies including antibiotic duration and systemic corticosteroids. [PMID 40679934]
- A meta-analysis found corticosteroids probably reduce short-term mortality and the need for invasive mechanical ventilation in hospitalised patients with non-viral CAP (moderate certainty). [PMID 40323455]
- Guideline-concordant antibiotic treatment was associated with reduced 30-day mortality in hospitalised CAP. [PMID 40868039]
- The French SPILF update reassesses the role of chest X-ray, pleuropulmonary ultrasound and CT in diagnosing and following CAP. [PMID 40037948]
- The JRS guideline emphasises advance care planning and interdisciplinary collaboration in adult pneumonia, and a European guideline addresses stroke-associated pneumonia with local protocols targeting aspiration pathogens. [PMID 40614556, 42095755]

**Uncertainties**
- Which patients benefit most from steroids.

**MCQ angles**
- Severity scores CURB-65 and PSI (not in abstracts).
- Duration of antibiotics.

**Case hooks**
- Hospitalised patient with severe CAP and respiratory failure.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Severity assessment, empirical regimens and microbiological testing details are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40037948: Update of guidelines for management of Community Acquired pneumonia in adults by the French Infectious Disease Society (SPILF) and the French-Speaking Society of Respiratory Diseases (SPLF): Endorsed by the French intensive care society (SRLF), the French microbiology society (SFM), the French radiology society (SFR) and the French emergency society (SFMU) (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40323455: Corticosteroids for adult patients hospitalised with non-viral community-acquired pneumonia: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40614556: The JRS guideline for the management of pneumonia in adults 2024 (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40679934: Diagnosis and Management of Community-acquired Pneumonia: An Official American Thoracic Society Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 40868039: Guideline-Concordant Antibiotic Treatment for Hospitalised Patients with Community-Acquired Pneumonia and Clinical Outcomes at a Tertiary Hospital in Australia (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42095755: European stroke organisation guideline on stroke-associated pneumonia (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `pocus_icu` (topics 1354) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Point-of-care ultrasound in the ICU supports diagnosis and resuscitation across shock, sepsis and pulmonary embolism, but evidence for outcome benefit and operator competency thresholds is limited.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of POCUS-guided resuscitation in shock could not explore how operator competency, scan frequency and timing affect outcomes because evidence was insufficient. [PMID 39298556]
- Multi-organ POCUS has high diagnostic accuracy for pulmonary embolism in critically ill patients, though validation in more populations is needed. [PMID 40269937]
- An Italian consensus gives a practical guide to bedside ultrasound in ICU sepsis and septic shock, focusing on key views and findings. [PMID 42615024]
- A consensus paper summarises conditions, indications and performance of focused transoesophageal echocardiography in acute and emergency medicine. [PMID 40643606]

**Uncertainties**
- Effect of POCUS on mortality.

**MCQ angles**
- RUSH protocol for undifferentiated shock (not in abstracts).
- B-lines and A-lines on lung ultrasound.

**Case hooks**
- Hypotensive ICU patient: focused cardiac and IVC assessment.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** FAST, lung ultrasound signs and vascular access applications named in the topic are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39298556: The Impact of Point-of-Care Ultrasound-Guided Resuscitation on Clinical Outcomes in Patients With Shock: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40269937: Diagnostic accuracy of multi-organ point-of-care ultrasound for pulmonary embolism in critically ill patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40643606: [Consensus paper on focused transesophageal echocardiography (fTEE) in clinical acute and emergency medicine : From the Commission for Clinical Cardiovascular Medicine of the DGK in cooperation with the DGINA, DGIIN and DGIM] (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42615024: Usefulness of Bedside Point-of-care Ultrasounds in Intensive Care Unit for Septic Patients: A Consensus Statement of the Italian Society of Echocardiography and Cardiovascular Imaging (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `poisoning_icu` (topics 1355) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Life-threatening poisoning is managed with supportive care and specific antidotes; the 2023 AHA focused update summarises evidence for antidotes and supportive drugs in specific poisonings.

**Teaching points**
- The 2023 AHA focused update on cardiac arrest or life-threatening toxicity reviews intravenous lipid emulsion, lidocaine, methylene blue, naloxone, pralidoxime, sodium bicarbonate, sodium nitrite, sodium thiosulfate, vasodilators and vasopressors for specific critical poisonings. [PMID 37721023]
- The 2025 AHA special circumstances guideline gives graded recommendations for adults and children where conditions require modification of BLS and ALS. [PMID 41122889]
- For life-threatening DOAC bleeding, a guideline could not recommend recombinant factor VIIa as a non-specific haemostatic agent because of scant data. [PMID 38567679]
- Consensus definitions and out-of-hospital triage guidance standardise assessment of paracetamol poisoning. [PMID 40162912, 40047505]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for many antidotes comes from case series.

**MCQ angles**
- Naloxone for opioids; sodium bicarbonate for sodium-channel blocker toxicity (not in abstracts).
- Hydroxocobalamin for cyanide.

**Case hooks**
- Unresponsive patient with miosis and respiratory depression.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Toxidrome recognition tables, extracorporeal removal indications and individual antidote doses are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37721023: 2023 American Heart Association Focused Update on the Management of Patients With Cardiac Arrest or Life-Threatening Toxicity Due to Poisoning: An Update to the American Heart Association Guidelines for Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Emergency Cardiovascular Care (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38567679: Clinical guideline on reversal of direct oral anticoagulants in patients with life threatening bleeding (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40047505: Out-of-hospital assessment and triage of paracetamol (acetaminophen) exposure in the United States and Canada: a consensus guideline (guideline, 2025)

- PMID 41122889: Part 10: Adult and Pediatric Special Circumstances of Resuscitation: 2025 American Heart Association Guidelines for Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Emergency Cardiovascular Care (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `pan` (topics 1356) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Polyarteritis nodosa is managed under the 2021 ACR/Vasculitis Foundation guideline, with distinct approaches for cutaneous PAN, childhood PAN and DADA2, where anti-TNF therapy is indicated.

**Teaching points**
- The 2021 ACR/Vasculitis Foundation guideline gives recommendations on diagnostic strategies, pharmacologic agents and imaging for polyarteritis nodosa. [PMID 34235883]
- In childhood-onset PAN and DADA2, anti-TNF treatment is indicated for vasculitic DADA2, and cyclophosphamide could be switched to mycophenolate once remission is confirmed in severe PAN. [PMID 33901990]
- Evidence for managing cutaneous polyarteritis nodosa is limited, and further studies are needed on remission, relapse and mortality. [PMID 33944902]
- DADA2 has a wide phenotypic spectrum, and early diagnosis and treatment are mandatory because of morbidity and mortality. [PMID 37179309]
- Inflammatory muscle involvement in systemic vasculitis responds well overall to conventional immunosuppressants and glucocorticoids. [PMID 34971804]

**Uncertainties**
- Best remission-maintenance drug.

**MCQ angles**
- Hepatitis B association and spared lungs (not in abstracts).
- Mesenteric angiography microaneurysms.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with fever, mononeuritis multiplex, livedo and hypertension.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Classification criteria, biopsy and angiography features, and induction doses are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33901990: Systematic review of childhood-onset polyarteritis nodosa and DADA2 (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 33944902: Therapeutic options for cutaneous polyarteritis nodosa: a systematic review (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 34235883: 2021 American College of Rheumatology/Vasculitis Foundation Guideline for the Management of Polyarteritis Nodosa (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34971804: Inflammatory muscle involvement in systemic vasculitis: A systematic review (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 37179309: A wide spectrum of phenotype of deficiency of deaminase 2 (DADA2): a systematic literature review (systematic review, 2023)

## Cluster `pkd` (topics 1357, 1358) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 KDIGO guideline is the central reference for ADPKD evaluation and treatment, with adaptations for paediatrics, liver and pancreatic cysts, and intracranial aneurysm screening in at-risk patients. ARPKD evidence was not retrieved.

**Teaching points**
- The KDIGO 2025 guideline addresses evaluation, management and treatment of ADPKD for providers and patients. [PMID 39848746]
- A KDIGO executive summary sets out recommendations for paediatric ADPKD and the transition to adult care. [PMID 41569965]
- Risk factors for unruptured intracranial aneurysm in asymptomatic ADPKD identify patients who may benefit from early screening. [PMID 39951701]
- An ERA commentary covers KDIGO recommendations on hepatopancreatic cysts, and KDOQI and CARI commentaries adapt the guideline to US and Australasian practice. [PMID 40810467, 41864657, 42011799]
- A narrative review integrates guideline recommendations with patient perspectives and real-world effectiveness to ease implementation. [PMID 41926217]

- Evidence base for many recommendations is limited.

## Cluster `pcos` (topics 1359, 1360) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** PCOS management is metabolic and reproductive: lifestyle and metformin, with GLP-1 receptor agonists such as liraglutide adding benefit in overweight patients, and Italian guidance for infertility.

**Teaching points**
- Combined liraglutide and metformin in overweight or obese women with PCOS offered more comprehensive metabolic and hormonal benefits (including LH, FSH, testosterone and cholesterol) than metformin alone. [PMID 40855964]
- Liraglutide improved metabolic and reproductive outcomes in PCOS, though larger and longer trials are warranted to validate its reproductive efficacy and safety. [PMID 41508932]
- Continuing metformin after a positive pregnancy test seems to have greater clinical benefit than discontinuing it, but high-quality research is needed. [PMID 40473092]
- Italian guidelines adapt recommendations for diagnosis and management of infertility in PCOS, and the Society for Endocrinology guideline covers evaluation of androgen excess. [PMID 40055752, 40364581]
- A global systematic review of PCOS prevalence informs international guideline diagnostic criteria and emphasises early, accurate diagnosis. [PMID 41528735]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term reproductive effects of GLP-1 treatment.

**MCQ angles**
- Rotterdam criteria: oligo-anovulation, hyperandrogenism, polycystic ovaries (not in abstracts).
- Letrozole for ovulation induction.

**Case hooks**
- Woman with oligomenorrhoea, hirsutism and BMI 32.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, endometrial protection and contraception options are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40055752: Diagnosis and management of infertility in patients with polycystic ovary syndrome (PCOS): guidelines from the Italian Society of Human Reproduction (SIRU) and the Italian Centers for the Study and Conservation of Eggs and Sperm (CECOS Italy) (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40364581: Society for Endocrinology Clinical Practice Guideline for the Evaluation of Androgen Excess in Women (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40473092: Preconception and first-trimester metformin on pregnancy outcomes in women with polycystic ovary syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40855964: Combined liraglutide and metformin therapy in overweight or obese women with polycystic ovary syndrome: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41508932: Efficacy of liraglutide on metabolic and reproductive outcomes in women with polycystic ovary syndrome: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41528735: Prevalence of polycystic ovary syndrome: a global and regional systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `polycythaemia_vera` (topics 1361, 1362, 1363) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Polycythaemia vera management aims to prevent thrombosis through haematocrit control, aspirin and cytoreduction, with interferon increasingly considered including in patients under 60. Retrieved evidence is consensus-level and does not show the haematocrit target or ruxolitinib data.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of cytoreductive therapy in PV patients under 60 suggests early cytoreduction, particularly with disease-modifying recombinant interferon alpha, may benefit them, and randomised trials are needed. [PMID 38507746]
- An Italian Delphi consensus did not reach consensus on whether bone marrow biopsy is necessary for diagnosis, and 93.4% agreed collaboration between transfusionists and haematologists is important. [PMID 40600747]
- A Japanese Delphi consensus guides selection of patients for ropeginterferon alfa-2b, and a US Delphi consensus defines disease progression and disease modification in PV. [PMID 41779123, 41091181]
- JAK2V617F allele burden has been related to haematological parameters, symptoms and complications, though variable methods prevented high-quality meta-analysis. [PMID 38652240]
- NCCN 2026 myeloproliferative neoplasm insights focus on recent updates for myelofibrosis. [PMID 42425157]

**Uncertainties**
- Place of ruxolitinib after hydroxyurea resistance.

**MCQ angles**
- Haematocrit target below 45% (not in abstracts).
- Thrombosis prevention with aspirin.

**Case hooks**
- 50-year-old with haematocrit 0.58, itch after hot baths and JAK2 V617F.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Haematocrit target, aspirin evidence, venesection, hydroxyurea and ruxolitinib (RESPONSE) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38507746: Cytoreductive therapy in younger adults with polycythemia vera: a meta-analysis of safety and outcomes (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38652240: Association of JAK2V617F allele burden and clinical correlates in polycythemia vera: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40600747: Recommendations for best management of polycythemia vera in Italy: a Delphi consensus statement (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41091181: Results from a US modified Delphi consensus to define disease progression and disease modification in polycythemia Vera (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41779123: Consensus on the use of ropeginterferon alfa-2b in Japanese patients with polycythemia vera: a modified Delphi survey (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42425157: NCCN Guidelines® Insights: Myeloproliferative Neoplasms, Version 2.2026 (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `pmr` (topics 1364, 1365, 1366) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Polymyalgia rheumatica is treated with glucocorticoids tapered to minimise exposure in a frail population, with exercise added and early referral recommended for suspected cases. Treat-to-target recommendations now exist for PMR and GCA.

**Teaching points**
- Treat-to-target recommendations for GCA and PMR define treatment targets and strategies to assess, achieve and maintain them, and set a research agenda. [PMID 36828585]
- French recommendations aim to reduce corticosteroid exposure and side effects in a fragile population in day-to-day PMR care. [PMID 38583691]
- German, Austrian and Swiss recommendations advise that frail patients be offered an individualised exercise programme in addition to drug therapy. [PMID 41346249, 40560440]
- International recommendations support early referral of suspected PMR, complementing EULAR/ACR guidance for established disease. [PMID 38050004]
- An executive summary calls for multidisciplinary integrated protocols for PMR and GCA and increased clinical suspicion. [PMID 38142973]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal steroid taper and role of steroid-sparing drugs.

**MCQ angles**
- Rapid response to low-dose prednisolone and raised ESR/CRP.
- Look for giant cell arteritis symptoms.

**Case hooks**
- 70-year-old with bilateral shoulder and hip girdle stiffness for 4 weeks and ESR 80.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Classification criteria, starting dose and taper schedule, and relapse prevention drugs are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36828585: Treat-to-target recommendations in giant cell arteritis and polymyalgia rheumatica (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38050004: Recommendations for early referral of individuals with suspected polymyalgia rheumatica: an initiative from the international giant cell arteritis and polymyalgia rheumatica study group (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `myositis` (topics 1367, 1368, 1369) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Idiopathic inflammatory myopathies need antibody-based classification, cancer screening per IMACS risk groups and tailored immunosuppression; IVIg and rituximab have supportive but limited evidence. Statin myopathy must be distinguished from immune-mediated necrotising myopathy.

**Teaching points**
- The IMACS cancer screening guideline performed satisfactorily in a myositis cohort, with most patients in high- or moderate-risk groups for whom enhanced screening is recommended, and a refinement score improved risk stratification (AUC 0.762 vs 0.699). [PMID 41978693, 42585765]
- IVIg probably improves disability and muscle strength in refractory idiopathic inflammatory myopathy compared with placebo (SMD 0.86 and 0.78). [PMID 40787733]
- Rituximab in adult inflammatory myositis shows benefit but infusion reactions and infections need careful monitoring, and controlled trials are needed. [PMID 39527803]
- Evidence for targeted immunosuppressive therapies in inflammatory myopathy is very uncertain. [PMID 40747756]
- In anti-MDA5 dermatomyositis with interstitial lung disease, age, smoking, fever, inflammatory markers and rapidly progressive ILD predict mortality. [PMID 40746563]

**Uncertainties**
- Best second-line therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Anti-HMGCR myopathy persists after statin cessation (not in abstracts).
- Cancer screening in dermatomyositis.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with proximal weakness, raised CK and statin use for 2 years.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Distinguishing statin myopathy (CK, EMG, biopsy, anti-HMGCR) is not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39527803: Rituximab Treatment in Adult Patients With Idiopathic Inflammatory Myositis: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40746563: Risk factors for mortality in anti-MDA5 antibody-positive dermatomyositis with interstitial lung disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40747756: Targeted immunosuppressive and immunomodulatory therapies for idiopathic inflammatory myopathies (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40787733: Non-targeted immunosuppressive and immunomodulatory therapies for idiopathic inflammatory myopathies (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41978693: Performance and potential impact of the IMACS cancer screening guideline in myositis: analysis in a myositis referral centre (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42585765: Cancer-associated myositis in Chinese patients with idiopathic inflammatory myopathies: Malignancy spectrum, risk factors, and validation of the IMACS guideline (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `polypharmacy` (topics 1370) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Deprescribing reviews regular medicines to minimise harm in older people; deprescribing preventive medicines in frail or palliative patients was not associated with worse outcomes, though certainty is very low. Pharmacist-led and guideline-supported approaches are available.

**Teaching points**
- A 2026 deprescribing guideline summary supports health professionals in reviewing regular medicines, minimising harm and planning ongoing treatment or monitoring. [PMID 41943558]
- Deprescribing preventive medications in older adults with advanced frailty, dementia or limited life expectancy was not associated with worse outcomes, but evidence certainty was very low. [PMID 41840386]
- In community-dwelling older adults the individual-level association of deprescribing was very small, but at population level the outcomes may be large given the prevalence of polypharmacy and potentially inappropriate medication. [PMID 40338546]
- A guideline addresses deprescribing statins in adults aged 65 and older, to be discussed with patients and carers. [PMID 42728062]
- Consolidated, implementation-ready deprescribing guidance for diuretics is needed, and guidelines for multimorbidity and polypharmacy need better methodology and patient input. [PMID 41825469, 41679655]

**Uncertainties**
- Effect of deprescribing interventions on hard outcomes.

**MCQ angles**
- STOPP/START criteria to identify inappropriate and omitted medicines (not in abstracts).
- Anticholinergic burden.

**Case hooks**
- 85-year-old on 12 medicines with falls and cognitive decline.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** The STOPP/START criteria content and medication review process steps are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40338546: Deprescribing in Community-Dwelling Older Adults: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41679655: Quality and recommendations of guidelines for multimorbidity and polypharmacy in older adults: A systematic review (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41825469: Guidelines and tools to assess appropriateness of diuretic prescribing and aid deprescribing: a systematic review (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41840386: Deprescribing preventive medications in older adults with advanced frailty, dementia, or limited life expectancy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41943558: Deprescribing in Older People: A Clinical Practice Guideline Summary (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42728062: Deprescribing statins for adults 65 years of age and older: Evidence-based clinical practice guideline (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `portal_hypertension` (topics 1371, 1372, 1373) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Portal hypertension care covers primary and secondary prophylaxis of varices, treatment of acute variceal bleeding with endoscopic therapy, and non-cirrhotic causes. Updated APASL guidance covers both cirrhosis and non-cirrhotic portal hypertension.

**Teaching points**
- The updated APASL guidelines on acute variceal bleeding give a contemporary template for management in both cirrhosis and non-cirrhotic portal hypertension. [PMID 40886248]
- APASL recommendations address diagnosis and management of non-cirrhotic portal fibrosis and idiopathic portal hypertension, combining evidence with expert opinion. [PMID 39546143]
- For gastric variceal bleeding, injection of cyanoacrylate glue is the commonest endoscopic treatment, and EUS-guided angiotherapy with glue with or without coils has expanded options. [PMID 39480369]
- International Liver Transplantation Society guidance updates management of portopulmonary hypertension. [PMID 40094355]
- The ACG perioperative guideline advises evidence-based risk assessment for patients with cirrhosis undergoing surgery. [PMID 40899690]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal therapy for gastric varices.

**MCQ angles**
- Non-selective beta-blocker plus band ligation for secondary prophylaxis (not in abstracts).
- Terlipressin and antibiotics in acute variceal bleeding.

**Case hooks**
- Cirrhotic patient with haematemesis and haemodynamic instability.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Primary prophylaxis, vasoactive drugs, TIPS indications and Baveno criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `post_arrest_care` (topics 1374, 1375) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Post-cardiac arrest care now centres on temperature control to normothermia, avoiding hyperoxia and hypotension, structured prognostication and survivorship. The 2025 AHA and ERC-ESICM guidelines are current.

**Teaching points**
- The 2025 AHA post-cardiac arrest care guideline covers care after ROSC, survivorship for patients, caregivers and rescuers, and the potential role of organ donation. [PMID 41122894]
- ERC and ESICM 2025 post-resuscitation guidelines address temperature control, seizure control, general intensive care management, prognostication, long-term outcome, rehabilitation and organ donation. [PMID 41117575]
- ERC-ESICM 2022 advice is to achieve normothermia in patients with mild hypothermia after ROSC and not to use prehospital cooling with large volumes of cold intravenous fluid. [PMID 35131119]
- Canadian best-practice guidance aims to harmonise out-of-hospital arrest care nationally to improve survival and functional outcomes. [PMID 38604702]
- A consensus guideline defines determination of brain death/death by neurologic criteria, including examination, apnoea and ancillary testing. [PMID 37821233]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal target temperature and duration.

**MCQ angles**
- Prognostication should be delayed and multimodal.
- Targets for oxygen and blood pressure after ROSC (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Comatose patient 24 hours after out-of-hospital VF arrest.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Specific temperature targets and durations, neuroprognostication algorithm timings and haemodynamic targets are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35131119: ERC-ESICM guidelines on temperature control after cardiac arrest in adults (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37821233: Pediatric and Adult Brain Death/Death by Neurologic Criteria Consensus Guideline (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38604702: Canadian Cardiovascular Society/Canadian Cardiovascular Critical Care Society/Canadian Association of Interventional Cardiology Clinical Practice Update on Optimal Post Cardiac Arrest and Refractory Cardiac Arrest Patient Care (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 41117575: European Resuscitation Council and European Society of Intensive Care Medicine Guidelines 2025 Post-Resuscitation Care (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41122894: Part 11: Post-Cardiac Arrest Care: 2025 American Heart Association Guidelines for Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Emergency Cardiovascular Care (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `post_ercp_pancreatitis` (topics 1376) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Rectal indomethacin or diclofenac should be given to patients undergoing ERCP unless renal failure; pancreatic stenting and hydration may add benefit, and early needle-knife or transpancreatic sphincterotomy lower PEP in difficult cannulation.

**Teaching points**
- Indomethacin or diclofenac given before or soon after ERCP is inexpensive and safe and is recommended in every patient without renal failure. [PMID 28167118]
- A network meta-analysis of NSAIDs, intravenous fluids, pancreatic stents and their combinations found these effective for preventing PEP, though evidence for some combinations is thin. [PMID 34214449]
- Early needle-knife and transpancreatic sphincterotomy were superior to other techniques in reducing PEP in difficult biliary cannulation. [PMID 34543649]
- ESGE advises that pancreatic sphincterotomy be reserved for patients with coexisting bile duct obstruction or biliary sphincter of Oddi dysfunction. [PMID 27299638]
- Japanese and ESGE guidelines cover risk factors, diagnosis and prevention of PEP and other ERCP-related adverse events. [PMID 40132896, 31863440]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal hydration regimen.

**MCQ angles**
- Rectal NSAID prophylaxis in all ERCP.
- Pancreatic stent in high-risk patients.

**Case hooks**
- Young woman with suspected SOD undergoing ERCP with pancreatic duct cannulation.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Indomethacin dose, fluid type and rate, and stent selection are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27299638: Papillary cannulation and sphincterotomy techniques at ERCP: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Clinical Guideline (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 28167118: Indomethacin and diclofenac in the prevention of post-ERCP pancreatitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis of prospective controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31863440: ERCP-related adverse events: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Guideline (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 34214449: Non-steroidal anti-inflammatory drugs, intravenous fluids, pancreatic stents, or their combinations for the prevention of post-endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography pancreatitis: a systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34543649: Comparative efficacy of different methods for difficult biliary cannulation in ERCP: systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)

## Cluster `pep_hiv` (topics 1377) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** HIV post-exposure prophylaxis is guided by 2025 CDC (non-occupational) and USPHS (occupational) recommendations, with transition to PrEP for those at ongoing risk. Doxycycline PEP is a separate bacterial-STI strategy.

**Teaching points**
- CDC 2025 nPEP guidance states clinicians should offer PrEP to people with ongoing indications and create an nPEP-to-PrEP transition plan. [PMID 40331832]
- The 2025 USPHS guidelines on occupational HIV exposure stress prompt reporting, adherence to recommended HIV PEP regimens when indicated, expert consultation and follow-up of exposed personnel. [PMID 41569270]
- The 2025 Canadian guideline updates HIV pre- and post-exposure prophylaxis to expand access to biomedical prevention. [PMID 41326046]
- Occupational HBV exposure post-exposure prophylaxis uptake among health workers in highly endemic regions is suboptimal, with barriers at individual and facility levels. [PMID 38559369]
- CDC and BASHH guidance on doxycycline post-exposure prophylaxis advises STI testing at baseline and every 3-6 months with HIV screening. [PMID 38833414, 40505019]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal antiretroviral regimen details are not in the abstracts.

**MCQ angles**
- Start PEP as soon as possible within 72 hours (not in abstracts).
- Follow-up testing after exposure.

**Case hooks**
- Needlestick from a patient with unknown HIV status.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Risk assessment, preferred regimens and the 28-day course, and testing schedule are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38559369: Occupational exposure to Hepatitis B virus, disease burden and pathways for postexposure prophylaxis management: recommendations for healthcare workers in highly endemic settings (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38833414: CDC Clinical Guidelines on the Use of Doxycycline Postexposure Prophylaxis for Bacterial Sexually Transmitted Infection Prevention, United States, 2024 (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40331832: Antiretroviral Postexposure Prophylaxis After Sexual, Injection Drug Use, or Other Nonoccupational Exposure to HIV - CDC Recommendations, United States, 2025 (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `pign` (topics 1378) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Post-infectious (post-streptococcal) glomerulonephritis is usually self-limited in children, with severe hypertension and its complications the main acute dangers. Early antibiotic treatment of infection may help prevent it.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review suggests early treatment of bacterial infections supports preventing postinfectious glomerulonephritis, relevant also as antimicrobial resistance grows. [PMID 35314652]
- Posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome can complicate acute postinfectious glomerulonephritis, where severe hypertension is central and prompt evaluation and management are crucial. [PMID 34546419]
- Acute post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis can rarely be associated with rheumatic fever, thrombocytopenia, haemolytic anaemia, Henoch-Schonlein purpura, reactive arthritis or uveitis. [PMID 31392657]
- A systematic review of paediatric post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis reports clinical manifestations and proportions of severe outcomes, with a heterogeneous RPGN/crescentic composite. [PMID 42856850]
- A clinical review describes the presentation, pathology and therapeutic approach of the spectrum of acute glomerulonephritis. [PMID 35461559]

**Uncertainties**
- Frequency of crescentic disease.

**MCQ angles**
- Low C3 that normalises by 8 weeks (not in abstracts).
- Hypertension management is the priority.

**Case hooks**
- Child with cola-coloured urine and periorbital oedema two weeks after a sore throat.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Complement pattern, ASO titre, fluid management and indications for biopsy are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 31392657: Extrarenal Immune-Mediated Disorders Linked with Acute Poststreptococcal Glomerulonephritis: a Systematic Review (systematic review, 2019)
- PMID 34546419: Posterior reversible leukoencephalopathy syndrome associated with acute postinfectious glomerulonephritis: systematic review (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 35314652: Examining the Efficacy of Antimicrobial Therapy in Preventing the Development of Postinfectious Glomerulonephritis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35461559: Acute glomerulonephritis (review, 2022)
- PMID 42856850: Clinical manifestations and proportions of severe outcomes in pediatric acute post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis: a systematic review and meta-analysis of predominantly hospital-based cohorts (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `pics` (topics 1379, 1380) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Post-intensive care syndrome affects physical, cognitive and psychological health, and also families. Multimodal, multidisciplinary rehabilitation is recommended, and risk rises with longer ICU stay.

**Teaching points**
- A German multimodal rehabilitation guideline states PICS requires an individual, multidisciplinary approach that assesses and treats motor, cognitive and psychological impairments. [PMID 37525219]
- A meta-analysis found an association between ICU length of stay and development of PICS, underscoring the need for targeted interventions for prolonged stays. [PMID 40338918]
- PICS and family PICS strongly affect quality of life, rehabilitation, financial stress and potentially preventable readmission. [PMID 38622971]
- No universally accepted standard exists for assessing PICS; consensus-based recommendations are limited and need practical implementation across settings and languages. [PMID 40429591]
- Position statements address optimised nutrition therapy after critical illness, and energy and protein adequacy in general wards among ICU survivors is limited by barriers such as inappropriate tube removal. [PMID 41163099, 39503062]

**Uncertainties**
- Best PICS prevention bundle.

**MCQ angles**
- Domains of PICS: physical, cognitive, mental health.
- ICU diaries and follow-up clinics (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Survivor of prolonged ventilation with weakness, memory problems and nightmares.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Risk factors, screening tools and the evidence for ICU follow-up clinics are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37525219: Guideline on multimodal rehabilitation for patients with post-intensive care syndrome (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38622971: Quality of life in ICU survivors and their relatives with post-intensive care syndrome: A systematic review (systematic review, 2024)

## Cluster `post_mi_bb` (topics 1381) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** In patients with LVEF of at least 50% after myocardial infarction and no other beta-blocker indication, an individual-patient-data meta-analysis found no reduction in death, myocardial infarction or heart failure, supporting stopping routine long-term beta-blockade in this group.

**Teaching points**
- An individual-patient-data meta-analysis of five randomised trials found beta-blockers did not reduce death from any cause (HR 1.04), myocardial infarction (HR 0.89) or heart failure (HR 0.87) in patients with LVEF of at least 50% after MI without other indications. [PMID 41211954]
- Earlier meta-analyses showed beta-blocker use for a year or more does not reduce mortality in MI patients without heart failure in the reperfusion era. [PMID 35058412]
- A Cochrane review found beta-blockers in suspected or diagnosed acute MI probably reduce the risk of reinfarction during follow-up (RR 0.82). [PMID 31845756]
- For long-term oral beta-blockers after PCI for STEMI, the only high-quality RCT data suggested a neutral effect, so interpretation should be cautious. [PMID 33136764]

**Uncertainties**
- Patients with mildly reduced LVEF (40-49%) are not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Beta-blockers are indicated after MI with reduced LVEF or other indications.
- Evidence changed after modern reperfusion era.

**Case hooks**
- Patient 1 year after STEMI with LVEF 58%, on bisoprolol, asking whether to continue.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Individual trial names (REDUCE-AMI, ABYSS) and current guideline classes are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 31845756: Beta-blockers for suspected or diagnosed acute myocardial infarction (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 33136764: Efficacy of Long-Term Oral Beta-Blocker Therapy in Patients Who Underwent Percutaneous Coronary Intervention for ST-Segment Elevation Myocardial Infarction With Preserved Left Ventricular Ejection Fraction: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35058412: Long-Term Beta-Blocker Therapy After Myocardial Infarction Without Heart Failure in the Reperfusion Era-Systemic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 41211954: Beta-Blockers after Myocardial Infarction with Normal Ejection Fraction (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `postop_ileus` (topics 1382) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Postoperative ileus prolongs stay and increases cost. ERAS protocols, early feeding, gum chewing, perioperative dexmedetomidine and electroacupuncture have supportive but variable evidence.

**Teaching points**
- Postoperative ileus carries a substantial global cost, and reducing its incidence and impact is a priority. [PMID 37352872]
- ERAS protocols in gastrectomy and colorectal resection reduce lung infections, shorten hospital stay and speed recovery without serious complications. [PMID 37130316]
- Perioperative intravenous dexmedetomidine may enhance postoperative gastrointestinal function recovery and reduce length of stay. [PMID 38085848]
- Electroacupuncture improved gastrointestinal functional recovery and reduced pain after colorectal surgery. [PMID 37916930]
- Gum chewing reduced ileus symptoms and hospitalisation time, and motility agents may be considered within ERAS, though standardised trials are needed. [PMID 40689540, 40439889]

**Uncertainties**
- Best drug for prevention.

**MCQ angles**
- Opioid-sparing analgesia and early mobilisation reduce ileus.
- Rule out mechanical obstruction.

**Case hooks**
- Day 3 after colectomy with distension and no flatus.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, alvimopan, nasogastric decompression and nutrition rules are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37130316: The Application of Enhanced Recovery After Surgery for Gastrectomy and Colorectal Resection: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37352872: Global cost of postoperative ileus following abdominal surgery: meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37916930: Efficacy of electroacupuncture in improving postoperative ileus in patients receiving colorectal surgery: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38085848: Impact of intravenous dexmedetomidine on postoperative gastrointestinal function recovery: an updated meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40439889: Safety and efficacy of gastrointestinal motility agents following elective colorectal surgery: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40689540: The Effects of Gum Chewing in the Postoperative-Period: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `ponv` (topics 1383) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** PONV prevention uses risk-stratified multimodal antiemetics. Olanzapine and esketamine are emerging adjuncts, and CPIC guidance links CYP2D6 genotype to 5-HT3 antagonist efficacy.

**Teaching points**
- Olanzapine prevented postoperative nausea and vomiting with a pivotal role relative to traditional antiemetics, though dosing and efficacy across surgical populations need further study. [PMID 39952307]
- Esketamine reduced nausea and vomiting after anaesthesia in a meta-analysis, though its risk-benefit profile needs clarification. [PMID 41454547]
- CPIC provides therapeutic recommendations for 5-HT3 receptor antagonists based on CYP2D6 genotype, where variation is linked to reduced drug efficacy. [PMID 41979467]
- China-specific 2025 PONV guidelines provide standardised protocols supporting ERAS. [PMID 41929930]
- In paediatric tonsillectomy, some analgesic regimens lowered PONV but tended to increase postoperative bleeding. [PMID 41420409]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal combinations by risk score.

**MCQ angles**
- Apfel risk factors (not in abstracts).
- Dexamethasone and ondansetron combination.

**Case hooks**
- Non-smoking woman with history of motion sickness for laparoscopic surgery.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Apfel score, standard prophylactic combinations and rescue therapy are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39952307: Olanzapine for the Prevention of Postoperative Nausea and Vomiting: An Updated Meta-Analysis With Trial Sequential Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41420409: The Effects of Pain Controlling Agents on Paediatric Tonsillectomy: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `postop_resp_failure` (topics 1384) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Postoperative pulmonary complications are reduced by preoperative physiotherapy and individualised ventilation; sugammadex may reduce them versus neostigmine. Routine postoperative CPAP, NIV or high-flow oxygen to prevent pneumonia is not supported.

**Teaching points**
- Preoperative physiotherapy was associated with 47% lower odds of postoperative pulmonary complications after elective abdominal surgery (adjusted OR 0.53), with effects on mortality uncertain. [PMID 38472053]
- Routine postoperative CPAP, NIV or high-flow nasal oxygen is not supported to prevent pneumonia after elective surgery in adults. [PMID 34916050]
- Sugammadex reversal was associated with fewer pulmonary complications (pneumonia, atelectasis, NIV, reintubation) than neostigmine, though this may not change length of stay, recovery quality or mortality. [PMID 37081384, 38036323]
- Individualised PEEP during one-lung ventilation was associated with fewer postoperative pulmonary complications and better oxygenation. [PMID 34260559]
- High-flow nasal oxygen therapy in postoperative adults was associated with shorter hospital stay in randomised trials. [PMID 30587060]
- A broad meta-analysis of perioperative interventions for pulmonary complications calls for new low-risk-of-bias trials. [PMID 32161042]

**Uncertainties**
- Which prevention bundle is best.

**MCQ angles**
- Risk factors for postoperative pulmonary complications.
- Incentive spirometry has limited evidence (not in abstracts).

**Case hooks**
- Smoker with COPD for upper abdominal surgery: optimisation steps.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Definitions of postoperative respiratory failure and treatment (NIV versus reintubation) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30587060: The Effect of High-Flow Nasal Oxygen Therapy on Postoperative Pulmonary Complications and Hospital Length of Stay in Postoperative Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 32161042: Perioperative interventions for prevention of postoperative pulmonary complications: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 34260559: Individualized positive end-expiratory pressure (PEEP) during one-lung ventilation for prevention of postoperative pulmonary complications in patients undergoing thoracic surgery: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34916050: Routine postoperative noninvasive respiratory support and pneumonia after elective surgery: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomised trials (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37081384: Postoperative pulmonary complications after sugammadex reversal of neuromuscular blockade: a systematic review and meta-analysis with trial sequential analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38036323: The effect of sugammadex on patient morbidity and quality of recovery after general anaesthesia: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38472053: Preoperative physiotherapy prevents postoperative pulmonary complications after major abdominal surgery: a meta-analysis of individual patient data (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `pph_txa` (topics 1385) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Tranexamic acid reduces the risk of life-threatening postpartum bleeding without increasing thrombosis. It is not recommended for all births, but should be considered before a PPH diagnosis in women at high risk of death.

**Teaching points**
- An individual-patient-data meta-analysis found tranexamic acid reduces life-threatening postpartum bleeding, with no increase in thromboembolic events (0.2% in both groups). [PMID 39461793]
- The authors do not recommend tranexamic acid in all women giving birth, but advise considering it before diagnosis of PPH in women at high risk of death. [PMID 39461793]
- Prophylactic tranexamic acid at caesarean delivery lowered postpartum blood loss and haemoglobin drop in updated meta-analyses, with further research needed in high-risk populations. [PMID 34582795, 37311484]
- SOGC Guideline 431 provides recommendations for postpartum haemorrhage and haemorrhagic shock for the whole care team. [PMID 36567097]
- Strategies are suggested for early detection and first response to PPH at caesarean birth, with implementation the next step. [PMID 38719306]

**Uncertainties**
- Timing and dose for prophylaxis.

**MCQ angles**
- TXA within 3 hours of birth for PPH (WOMAN trial; not in abstracts).
- Uterotonics first.

**Case hooks**
- Atonic uterus with 1.5 L blood loss after vaginal delivery.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** The WOMAN trial itself, dosing and uterotonic ladder are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 34582795: Tranexamic acid for the prevention of postpartum hemorrhage in women undergoing cesarean delivery: an updated meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36567097: Guideline No. 431: Postpartum Hemorrhage and Hemorrhagic Shock (guideline, 2022)

## Cluster `postrenal_aki` (topics 1386) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Post-renal AKI is relieved by urgent decompression; percutaneous nephrostomy and retrograde stenting are both used, with nephrostomy preferable for post-procedure quality of life but a slightly longer stay.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found percutaneous nephrostomy preferable to retrograde ureteral stenting for acute upper obstructive uropathy because of less haematuria and dysuria, though hospital stay is slightly longer. [PMID 33758312]
- In emphysematous pyelonephritis, risk factors predict patients who need aggressive treatment, while minimally invasive modalities carry lower mortality. [PMID 35103928]
- Consensus on fetal lower urinary tract obstruction states vesico-amniotic shunting in selected cases increases perinatal survival, and a Delphi study defines a core outcome set. [PMID 35136187, 38748971]
- EAU/ESPU guidelines summarise paediatric urology including congenital lower urinary tract obstruction. [PMID 38627150, 39909817]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal drainage in infected obstruction.

**MCQ angles**
- Obstructed infected system needs urgent decompression.
- Renal ultrasound as first investigation.

**Case hooks**
- Older man with anuria, bladder outlet obstruction and creatinine rising.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Causes, imaging findings, post-obstructive diuresis and timing of relief are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33758312: Percutaneous nephrostomy versus retrograde ureteral stenting for acute upper obstructive uropathy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35103928: A systematic review and meta-analysis of risk factors and treatment choices in emphysematous pyelonephritis (meta-analysis, 2022)

## Cluster `post_transplant` (topics 1387) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Post-transplant care centres on infection prevention and metabolic complications. Post-transplant diabetes worsens kidney allograft outcomes, and vaccination and CMV monitoring are core parts of follow-up.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found post-transplant diabetes mellitus carries prognostic impact in kidney allograft recipients, underscoring comprehensive management strategies. [PMID 39134508]
- A systematic review supports SGLT2 inhibitors in diabetic solid organ transplant recipients, though larger studies are needed for long-term effects across transplant types. [PMID 41561830]
- Meta-analyses of tirzepatide and of belatacept versus calcineurin inhibitors suggest possible roles for obesity, post-transplant diabetes and reduced immunosuppressive toxicity, pending larger prospective studies. [PMID 41908834, 41766891]
- CMV infection after kidney transplant is common enough that early virological and immunological monitoring, especially in high-risk groups, is advised to reduce morbidity and preserve graft function. [PMID 41483466]
- An updated review integrates evolving vaccination guidelines into the care of adult solid organ transplant recipients. [PMID 41597712]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term safety of newer diabetes drugs in transplant recipients.

**MCQ angles**
- CMV monitoring in high-risk recipients.
- Post-transplant diabetes as a modifiable risk.

**Case hooks**
- Kidney transplant recipient with new hyperglycaemia on tacrolimus and steroids.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Rejection, malignancy surveillance and immunosuppression regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39134508: Prognostic impact of post-transplant diabetes mellitus in kidney allograft recipients: a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41483466: Incidence of Cytomegalovirus (CMV) Infection in After Kidney Transplant Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41561830: Sodium-Glucose Cotransporter 2 Inhibitors in Diabetic Solid Organ Transplant Recipients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Comparative Studies (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41597712: The Role of Vaccination in Adult Solid Organ Transplantation: Updated Reviews with Recent Guidelines (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41766891: Systematic review and meta-analysis of belatacept versus calcineurin inhibitors on risk of post-transplant diabetes mellitus in kidney transplant recipients (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41908834: Safety and efficacy of tirzepatide in transplant recipients: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `pots` (topics 1388, 1389, 1390) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** POTS evidence is thin: oral drug treatment is poorly supported, and mast cell activation disorders are reported with POTS but data are inconsistent. Orthostatic hypotension, a separate entity, has pyridostigmine data in a meta-analysis.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review of oral medications for POTS found that treatments directly targeting the underlying autonomic dysfunction are lacking. [PMID 39882369]
- A systematic review of mast cell activation disorders and hereditary alpha tryptasemia in POTS found that further research with strict validated diagnostic criteria is needed to clarify any true association. [PMID 40185471]
- An AGA update on hypermobile Ehlers-Danlos syndrome advises considering testing for mast cell disorders in patients with hEDS/HSDs and DGBI who have episodic symptoms suggesting generalised mast cell disease. [PMID 40387691]
- Pyridostigmine in orthostatic hypotension raised standing BP in a meta-analysis, with possible supine hypertension, and response may be greater when baroreflex function and sympathetic reserve are preserved. [PMID 40132893]
- The pooled prevalence of orthostatic hypotension in Parkinson's disease is 33.1%, more often with older age, longer duration and more severe motor symptoms. [PMID 40715652]

**Uncertainties**
- Best drug for POTS.

**MCQ angles**
- Orthostatic hypotension in Parkinson's disease.
- Pyridostigmine for orthostatic hypotension.

**Case hooks**
- Young woman with palpitations on standing and hypermobility.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** POTS diagnostic criteria (heart-rate rise on standing/tilt) and non-drug measures are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39882369: Oral medications for the treatment of postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome; a systematic review of studies before and during the COVID-19 pandemic (systematic review, 2024)
- PMID 40132893: Pyridostigmine in the management of orthostatic hypotension: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40185471: Prevalence of mast cell activation disorders and hereditary alpha tryptasemia among patients with postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome and Ehlers-Danlos syndrome: A systematic review (systematic review, 2025)
- PMID 40387691: AGA Clinical Practice Update on GI Manifestations and Autonomic or Immune Dysfunction in Hypermobile Ehlers-Danlos Syndrome: Expert Review (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40715652: Prevalence and impact of orthostatic hypotension in Parkinson's disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `pouchitis` (topics 1391) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pouchitis and inflammatory pouch disorders are covered by AGA and international consensus guidelines. Probiotics show benefit for relapsing pouchitis in a meta-analysis.

**Teaching points**
- The AGA clinical practice guideline provides a patient-centred approach to managing pouchitis and other inflammatory pouch disorders. [PMID 38128971]
- International consensus guidelines address treatment of pouchitis, Crohn's disease of the pouch, cuffitis and other inflammatory pouch disorders, and manage extraintestinal manifestations similarly to patients without pouches. [PMID 34774224]
- An overview of meta-analyses found probiotics associated with remission and relapse prevention in UC and with benefit for relapsing pouchitis, but no significant effect in Crohn's disease. [PMID 39106167]
- A RAND/UCLA expert process standardised items for measuring pouchitis disease activity in clinical trials. [PMID 33735522]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal antibiotic and biologic sequencing.

**MCQ angles**
- Pouchitis after ileal pouch-anal anastomosis for UC.
- Probiotics for relapsing pouchitis.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with restorative proctocolectomy and new frequent loose stools.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Specific antibiotic regimens, endoscopic diagnosis and biologic recommendations are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `precision_oncology` (topics 1392) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Precision oncology depends on quality-assured molecular testing and structured molecular tumour boards. ESMO and national societies issue recommendations to harmonise tissue and liquid biopsy testing.

**Teaching points**
- ESMO Precision Oncology Working Group recommendations set the structure and quality indicators for molecular tumour boards to harmonise standards and speed integration of precision oncology into practice. [PMID 40194904]
- An Austrian, German and Swiss guideline establishes expert consensus on evidence-based standards and implementation of precision oncology. [PMID 40058270]
- Italian best-practice documents on molecular testing in solid tumours cover standards for tissue and liquid biopsies to harmonise diagnostics. [PMID 40963486]
- ESMO guidance on breast cancer germline multigene panel testing states that decades of surveillance and risk-reducing surgery for carriers should be justified by strong evidence of improved cancer mortality or quality of life. [PMID 40523834]

**Uncertainties**
- Which biomarkers justify routine broad panel testing.

**MCQ angles**
- Role of the molecular tumour board.
- Tissue versus liquid biopsy.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with advanced solid tumour and a comprehensive genomic profile result.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** ESCAT actionability tiers and specific tumour-agnostic approvals are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40058270: Precision oncology - Guideline of the Austrian, German and Swiss Societies for hematology and medical oncology (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40194904: ESMO Precision Oncology Working Group recommendations on the structure and quality indicators for molecular tumour boards in clinical practice (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `precocious_puberty` (topics 1393) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** An Endocrine Society guideline now covers central precocious puberty, advising against routine genetic testing but considering MKRN3 sequencing in familial CPP. GnRH analogue therapy may raise BMI short-term with normalisation by adult height.

**Teaching points**
- The Endocrine Society guideline on central precocious puberty advises against routine genetic testing, while suggesting genetic testing such as MKRN3 sequencing be considered for familial CPP through shared decision-making. [PMID 42287186]
- A systematic review supports the Endocrine Society guideline, including use of bone age for GnRH analogue discontinuation. [PMID 42287191]
- Meta-analyses found GnRH analogue treatment may increase BMI in the short term, with BMI likely returning to normal by adult height. [PMID 38185120, 38407229]
- A meta-analysis of pharmacotherapy for CPP or early puberty advises considering the increasing risk of polycystic ovary syndrome before treatment. [PMID 40760548]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal stopping age for GnRH analogue.

**MCQ angles**
- Central versus peripheral precocious puberty.
- GnRH analogue as therapy for CPP.

**Case hooks**
- Girl aged 7 with breast development and advanced bone age.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Age thresholds, diagnostic LH testing and imaging are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38185120: The Effect of GnRHa Treatment on Body Mass Index in Central Precocious Puberty: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `prediabetes` (topics 1394) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Prediabetes progression risk is shaped by identifiable factors. Lifestyle intervention is central, and exercise appears superior to metformin in prediabetes, whereas metformin appears superior in established type 2 diabetes.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis identified risk factors for progression from prediabetes to type 2 diabetes, and called for longer follow-up and clarification of diagnostic criteria. [PMID 40165126]
- A network meta-analysis found exercise appears superior to metformin for glucose metabolism in prediabetes, while metformin appears superior to exercise in type 2 diabetes. [PMID 39242178]
- A clinical guideline provides detailed lifestyle strategies for treatment and remission of type 2 diabetes and prediabetes in adults. [PMID 40546763]
- A Brazilian position statement prefers the 1-hour OGTT over the 2-hour OGTT as more practical and superior, and gives a structured reevaluation interval. [PMID 40038723]
- The 2026 AACE algorithm emphasises a complications- and comorbidities-centric approach beyond glucose levels for choosing pharmacological treatment in type 2 diabetes. [PMID 41842862]

**Uncertainties**
- Best diagnostic test for risk stratification.

**MCQ angles**
- Lifestyle versus metformin in prediabetes.
- Diagnostic thresholds for impaired fasting glucose.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with HbA1c in the prediabetes range and obesity.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diabetes Prevention Program results and diagnostic cut-offs are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39242178: Effects of exercise, metformin and their combination on glucose metabolism in individuals with abnormal glycaemic control: a systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40038723: Brazilian guideline for screening and diagnosis of type 2 diabetes: a position statement from the Brazilian Diabetes Society (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40165126: Risk factors for progression to type 2 diabetes in prediabetes: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `preeclampsia` (topics 1395, 1396, 1397) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pre-eclampsia has lasting consequences: offspring have higher blood pressure and affected women have persistent subclinical atherosclerosis, supporting early follow-up. Evidence for arginine, citrulline and statins in prevention is tentative.

**Teaching points**
- Offspring of pregnancies with preeclampsia are at increased risk of high blood pressure during childhood and young adulthood, so BP monitoring is important. [PMID 38780756]
- Women with a history of pre-eclampsia show subclinical atherosclerosis during pregnancy that persists long term, supporting early follow-up. [PMID 40796266]
- L-arginine and L-citrulline may help prevent and treat pre-eclampsia, but findings need cautious interpretation and more trials on dose and timing. [PMID 39800868]
- Statin use in pregnancy has been associated with reduced preeclampsia risk, but observations need confirmation in high-quality trials. [PMID 40796817]
- Pre-eclampsia and gestational hypertension are linked to altered lipid levels during pregnancy. [PMID 40407883]
- PlGF-based testing for suspected preterm preeclampsia depends on assay selection, with cutoffs either single or gestational-age specific. [PMID 42336060]

**Uncertainties**
- Preventive agents beyond aspirin.

**MCQ angles**
- Long-term cardiovascular risk after pre-eclampsia.
- PlGF testing in suspected preterm pre-eclampsia.

**Case hooks**
- Woman 5 years after pre-eclampsia asking about cardiovascular follow-up.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Aspirin prophylaxis, diagnostic criteria, magnesium sulfate and delivery timing are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38780756: Association Between Preeclampsia and Blood Pressure in Offspring: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39800868: L-Arginine and L-Citrulline for Prevention and Treatment of Pre-Eclampsia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40407883: Pre-eclampsia, gestational hypertension, and lipid levels during pregnancy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40796266: Subclinical atherosclerosis in women after pre-eclampsia: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 40796817: The role of statins during pregnancy on maternal risk of preeclampsia: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 42336060: Laboratory Perspectives on Assay Selection and Implementation of PlGF-Based Testing for Suspected Preterm Preeclampsia (guideline, 2026)

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of pregnant women with chronic kidney disease found adverse pregnancy outcomes, including pre-eclampsia and gestational hypertension, with heterogeneity in how CKD in pregnancy is defined. [PMID 38488268]
- A best-practice position statement advises extensive personalised counselling with a multidisciplinary team and close monitoring during medically assisted reproduction and subsequent pregnancy in CKD. [PMID 37354277]

- Standard definition of CKD in pregnancy.

- RAAS inhibitors and pregnancy.

## Cluster `prerenal_aki` (topics 1403) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Consensus reports address haemodynamic and perfusion-related AKI in specific settings: sepsis, cirrhosis (hepatorenal) and cardiac surgery. Early recognition matters because splanchnic vasoconstrictors can reverse hepatorenal AKI, and euvolaemia and perfusion-guided therapy may mitigate cardiac surgery AKI.

**Teaching points**
- The ADQI consensus on sepsis-associated AKI gives graded recommendations for management, notes that therapeutic options are limited, and highlights care bundles and extracorporeal techniques as under study. [PMID 36823168]
- In cirrhosis, early recognition of HRS-AKI is crucial because splanchnic vasoconstrictors may reverse the AKI and bridge to liver transplantation, the only curative option; a joint ICA-ADQI meeting developed new diagnostic criteria and recommendations. [PMID 38527522]
- For adult cardiac surgery AKI, prevention and early detection are paramount: attention to presurgical kidney health, a cardiopulmonary bypass bundle, strategies to maintain intravascular euvolaemia, and goal-directed therapy prioritising oxygen delivery and end-organ perfusion may mitigate incidence. [PMID 37355415]
- The current lack of specific therapy for AKI prevention or treatment demands refinement of clinical trial design, including predictive and prognostic enrichment. [PMID 39115567]
- A paediatric Delphi consensus recommends adjudicating fluid balance by disease severity and broadening AKI phenotype definitions. [PMID 36178697]

**Uncertainties**
- Therapeutic options for AKI are limited.

**MCQ angles**
- HRS-AKI responds to splanchnic vasoconstrictors.
- Maintain euvolaemia and perfusion in cardiac surgery.

**Case hooks**
- Cirrhotic patient with ascites and rising creatinine after diuretics.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Classic pre-renal versus ATN distinction (urine sodium, FENa, urea ratio), fluid challenge and nephrotoxin avoidance are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page covers consensus reports by setting only.

**Sources**
- PMID 36178697: Consensus-Based Recommendations on Priority Activities to Address Acute Kidney Injury in Children: A Modified Delphi Consensus Statement (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36823168: Sepsis-associated acute kidney injury: consensus report of the 28th Acute Disease Quality Initiative workgroup (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37355415: Adult Cardiac Surgery-Associated Acute Kidney Injury: Joint Consensus Report (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38527522: Acute kidney injury in patients with cirrhosis: Acute Disease Quality Initiative (ADQI) and International Club of Ascites (ICA) joint multidisciplinary consensus meeting (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39115567: Recommendations for clinical trial design in acute kidney injury from the 31st acute disease quality initiative consensus conference. A consensus statement (guideline, 2024)
