## Cluster `pressure_ulcers` (topics 1404) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pressure injury prevention bundles reduce hospital-acquired injuries, though certainty is very low, and nutritional interventions have very uncertain effects.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review found pressure injury prevention care bundles decrease hospital-acquired pressure injuries, but the certainty of this evidence is very low. [PMID 38642429]
- A Cochrane review found the evidence very uncertain for the effect of protein, arginine, zinc and antioxidants and several other nutritional supplements on pressure ulcers. [PMID 38345088]
- The international pressure ulcer/injury guidelines, updated since 2009, are widely cited, indicating worldwide dissemination. [PMID 39254059]
- The 2019 international guideline's nutrition recommendations are translated into policy and practice points for wound healing. [PMID 34462964]
- Implementation of the RNAO prevention guideline in Chile shows coordinated governance, standardised indicators and structured support can scale evidence-based practice. [PMID 42266850]

**Uncertainties**
- Which bundle components matter.

**MCQ angles**
- Prevention bundles reduce hospital-acquired pressure injury.
- Nutrition for wound healing.

**Case hooks**
- Immobile inpatient with non-blanching erythema over the sacrum.

## Cluster `prevent_t1d` (topics 1405) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Screening for islet autoantibodies identifies early-stage type 1 diabetes. Teplizumab is an approved immunotherapy to delay progression, with consensus statements on selection, monitoring and side effects.

**Teaching points**
- ISPAD 2024 guidelines cover screening, staging and strategies to preserve beta-cell function and discuss FDA approval of teplizumab to delay progression. [PMID 39662065]
- The Pediatric Endocrine Society statement gives a protocol for teplizumab administration and calls for long-term surveillance of efficacy and safety. [PMID 38663372]
- UK recommendations for pre-stage 3 type 1 diabetes address islet autoantibody confirmation, monitoring in early-stage disease, starting insulin and single autoantibody positivity. [PMID 40988096]
- A consensus describes the imperatives for implementing general-population islet autoantibody screening for early-stage T1D in Europe. [PMID 41821267]
- A network meta-analysis examined interventions to preserve beta cell function in people newly diagnosed with type 1 diabetes. [PMID 40598585]
- A British Society consensus covers teplizumab in stage 2 T1D, including patient selection, monitoring and common side effects. [PMID 42051156]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term benefit of teplizumab.

**MCQ angles**
- Staging of T1D by autoantibodies and dysglycaemia.
- Teplizumab delays stage 3 onset.

**Case hooks**
- Sibling of a child with T1D found to have two islet autoantibodies.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Trial results for teplizumab and stage definitions are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `preventive_cardiology` (topics 1406) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Primary prevention rests on cardiovascular risk assessment tools guiding lipid-lowering therapy, with targeted screening for asymptomatic atherosclerosis to individualise treatment.

**Teaching points**
- A review links CVD risk assessment tools to lipid-lowering recommendations in primary prevention and highlights targeted screening for asymptomatic atherosclerosis to individualise therapy. [PMID 40217673]
- A living guideline on type 2 diabetes therapeutics assesses cardiovascular, kidney and weight-loss effects, noting cost and availability will affect implementation. [PMID 40813129]
- A systematic review found interventions targeting both clinician support and patient access were most effective at improving secondary cardiovascular risk reduction prescribing and lowering lipids in peripheral artery disease. [PMID 41260242]
- The 2025 AHA/ACC hypertension guideline update emphasises tailored strategies and equitable access to preventive therapy. [PMID 41843050]
- An Italian call to action says cardiovascular prevention must start in childhood. [PMID 41366616]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of imaging for risk reclassification.

- Primary versus secondary prevention.

## Cluster `addison` (topics 1407) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Primary adrenal insufficiency carries a 2.5-fold increase in mortality, mostly from adrenal crisis. Glucocorticoid replacement is linked with more fractures, and pregnancy outcomes are worse.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found mortality in primary adrenal insufficiency increased 2.5-fold, with adrenal crisis the main cause of death in CAH. [PMID 40966723]
- Patients with adrenal insufficiency on glucocorticoid replacement had a higher rate of fractures than controls. [PMID 33846948]
- A systematic review of interventions to prevent adrenal crisis found an emerging field with limited studies and a need for behaviour change intervention research. [PMID 35536876]
- Women with primary adrenal insufficiency had more negative pregnancy and fetal outcomes, including spontaneous abortion, preterm birth and small for gestational age infants. [PMID 39277840]
- Antiphospholipid syndrome-associated adrenal haemorrhage is severe with increased mortality, and most patients develop permanent primary adrenal insufficiency. [PMID 38244823]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal replacement dosing.

**MCQ angles**
- Adrenal crisis as the leading cause of death.
- APS-associated adrenal haemorrhage.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with Addison's disease presenting with vomiting and hypotension.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Autoimmune aetiology, diagnostic ACTH stimulation testing and stress dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33846948: Rate of fracture in patients with glucocorticoid replacement therapy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35536876: Interventions for the prevention of adrenal crisis in adults with primary adrenal insufficiency: a systematic review (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 38244823: Clinical presentation and outcomes in patients with antiphospholipid syndrome-associated adrenal hemorrhage. A multicenter cohort study and systematic literature review (systematic review, 2024)

## Cluster `stemi_pci` (topics 1409) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Primary PCI is the preferred reperfusion strategy for STEMI; where timely access is unavailable, other reperfusion strategies matter. The 2025 ACC/AHA ACS guideline is the current US reference.

**Teaching points**
- A network meta-analysis of reperfusion strategies identifies primary PCI as the preferred treatment for STEMI, with implications for settings where timely primary PCI is not available. [PMID 32500800]
- The 2025 ACC/AHA/ACEP/NAEMSP/SCAI acute coronary syndromes guideline updates many earlier recommendations with new evidence and adds new recommendations where supported by published data. [PMID 40014670]
- A SCAI expert consensus covers STEMI management in the catheterisation laboratory, including options for large thrombus burden and varied anatomical and clinical circumstances. [PMID 39649824]
- A clinical consensus statement addresses reperfusion for STEMI in low- and middle-income countries, including patient education and secondary prevention. [PMID 40922666]
- Stress hyperglycaemia and fasting glucose predict short-term outcomes in STEMI, while HbA1c better predicts longer-term outcomes. [PMID 38529334]
- A meta-analysis of laboratory parameters aims to refine prediction of the no-reflow phenomenon after STEMI to guide prevention. [PMID 38400562]

**Uncertainties**
- Pharmaco-invasive strategy where PCI is delayed.

**MCQ angles**
- Primary PCI versus fibrinolysis.
- No-reflow after primary PCI.

**Case hooks**
- Anterior STEMI presenting to a hospital without a catheterisation laboratory.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Door-to-balloon time targets, antithrombotic regimens and complete revascularisation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32500800: Comparison of Reperfusion Strategies for ST-Segment-Elevation Myocardial Infarction: A Multivariate Network Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 38400562: The predictive value of laboratory parameters for no-reflow phenomenon in patients with ST-elevation myocardial infarction following primary percutaneous coronary intervention: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `pbc` (topics 1410) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Second-line therapy in PBC after UDCA includes PPAR agonists and obeticholic acid; PPAR agonists reduce ALP, GGT and bilirubin when added to UDCA. Fatigue has no recommended pharmacological treatment.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials found adding PPAR agonists to UDCA effectively reduces ALP, GGT and bilirubin compared with UDCA alone. [PMID 40200180]
- A network meta-analysis of second-line treatments in PBC found one drug associated with lower incidence of pruritus, while obeticholic acid was associated with more pruritus and serious adverse events at similar efficacy. [PMID 39720853]
- A European position paper finds that, based on available trials, no pharmacological treatment can be recommended for PBC-related fatigue and proposes an ASK-MEASURE-TREAT algorithm. [PMID 41082897]
- An EASL-AASLD Delphi consensus provides a framework on surrogate endpoints and real-world evidence to support regulatory pathways for new PBC therapies. [PMID 42191456]
- The global prevalence of PBC is rising, representing a growing healthcare burden, and an association with latitude requires further study. [PMID 40398833]

**Uncertainties**
- Hard-outcome benefit of second-line agents.

**MCQ angles**
- Pruritus and fatigue are major symptoms.
- UDCA first-line, add second-line for inadequate response.

**Case hooks**
- Middle-aged woman with itch, raised ALP and anti-mitochondrial antibodies.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** First-line UDCA dosing, response criteria and diagnostic criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `primary_care_mental` (topics 1411) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** In primary care, PHQ-9 is widely validated for depression screening in a two-stage process, but detection is very low in low- and middle-income countries. Psychotherapy matches pharmacotherapy, combined treatment may be best, and CBT is recommended for mild to moderate depression.

**Teaching points**
- PHQ-9 has been widely validated for depression screening in primary care and is recommended in a two-stage screening process, though longitudinal studies of screening effectiveness are needed. [PMID 33126078]
- A meta-analysis found extremely low depression detection by primary care clinicians in low- and middle-income countries, posing a serious threat to scaling up mental healthcare. [PMID 35123556]
- A network meta-analysis found psychotherapy likely effective for depression in primary care versus care as usual or waitlist, with effects comparable to pharmacotherapy and combined treatment possibly better than either alone. [PMID 34180847]
- A meta-analysis supports offering CBT to primary care patients with mild to moderate depression, regardless of whether delivered individually, in groups or as therapist-guided self-help. [PMID 30688184]
- E-health interventions for depression in primary care have a small effect and appear cost-effective, with no evidence for effectiveness in anxiety. [PMID 30447572]
- A review found treatment effects for CBT and problem-solving therapy in primary care depression and anxiety, and in-person and technology-assisted treatments were both effective. [PMID 30699860]
- An individual participant data meta-analysis found the collaborative care component most associated with reduced depressive symptoms was a therapeutic treatment strategy such as manual-based psychotherapy or family integration. [PMID 40136273]

**Uncertainties**
- Anxiety interventions in primary care have thin evidence.

**MCQ angles**
- PHQ-9 two-stage screening.
- Collaborative care components.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with low mood and sleep disturbance in a GP clinic.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Antidepressant choice, stepped-care thresholds, suicide risk assessment and anxiety, somatisation and substance use are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30447572: The effectiveness and cost-effectiveness of e-health interventions for depression and anxiety in primary care: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 30688184: Cognitive behaviour therapy for depression in primary care: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 30699860: The effectiveness of four empirically supported psychotherapies for primary care depression and anxiety: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 33126078: Screening for depression in primary care with Patient Health Questionnaire-9 (PHQ-9): A systematic review (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 34180847: Psychologic Treatment of Depression Compared With Pharmacotherapy and Combined Treatment in Primary Care: A Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35123556: Under detection of depression in primary care settings in low and middle-income countries: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 40136273: Effective Components of Collaborative Care for Depression in Primary Care: An Individual Participant Data Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `pcnsl` (topics 1412) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Primary CNS lymphoma care includes rituximab-containing treatment, with preoperative corticosteroids reducing biopsy diagnostic yield. Ibrutinib and PD-1 inhibitors show activity in relapsed disease.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis suggests rituximab may have a positive impact on the prognosis of patients with PCNSL. [PMID 37453867]
- Preoperative corticosteroids reduce the diagnostic yield of stereotactic biopsies in PCNSL, with no evidence that tapering steroids before biopsy improves diagnostic rates, and CSF analysis has poor sensitivity. [PMID 38865324]
- In selected patients, surgical resection of PCNSL is associated with significantly better overall and progression-free survival than biopsy alone. [PMID 36449256]
- A meta-analysis of ibrutinib in CNS lymphoma calls for trials comparing it with standard therapies and for long-term safety data. [PMID 39689737]
- PD-1 inhibitors showed responses in relapsed PCNSL and primary testicular lymphoma, with mostly mild skin adverse effects. [PMID 40299829]
- Consensus recommendations define ideal and minimum-standard MRI and PET protocols at 3T and 1.5T for PCNSL imaging. [PMID 33560416]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal induction regimen is not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Avoid steroids before biopsy when lymphoma is suspected.
- Imaging protocols for PCNSL.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with periventricular enhancing lesion and suspected lymphoma awaiting biopsy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** High-dose methotrexate induction and consolidation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33560416: Consensus recommendations for MRI and PET imaging of primary central nervous system lymphoma: guideline statement from the International Primary CNS Lymphoma Collaborative Group (IPCG) (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 36449256: Surgical resection versus biopsy in the treatment of primary central nervous system lymphoma: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37453867: Role of Rituximab in Treatment of Patients With Primary Central Nervous System Lymphoma (PCNSL): A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `hyperparathyroidism` (topics 1414, 1415) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** A French and Italian guideline set covers diagnosis, indications for treatment, genetics, syndromic disease and recurrent disease in primary hyperparathyroidism.

**Teaching points**
- Guidelines outline investigation to identify classical and non-classical features of primary hyperparathyroidism and the methodology to locate abnormal tissue, with surgical and conservative management. [PMID 39162944]
- The diagnosis chapter notes the oral calcium loading test and the Pro-FHH score contribute to diagnosis in atypical forms. [PMID 39818287]
- Genetic assessment should guide diagnosis of hereditary primary hyperparathyroidism, with genetic counselling for patient and family. [PMID 39818301]
- In syndromic disease, surgical indications should be discussed in an expert centre, and recurrence risk, particularly high in MEN1, requires long-term monitoring. [PMID 39818298]
- For recurrent or persistent disease, revision surgery should follow laryngoscopy in an expert centre, ideally with intraoperative PTH measurement and recurrent nerve neuromonitoring. [PMID 39818302]
- Treatment options are surgery, local destruction and medical management, with indications addressed in a separate chapter. [PMID 39818297, 39818285]

**Uncertainties**
- Management of asymptomatic patients is only partly covered.

**MCQ angles**
- MEN1 and high recurrence risk.
- Revision surgery precautions.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with hypercalcaemia and raised PTH deciding between surgery and observation.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Numeric surgical criteria for asymptomatic patients are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39162944: Primary hyperparathyroidism: from guidelines to outpatient clinic (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `membranous` (topics 1418) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Anti-PLA2R levels of at least 20 RU/mL with thorough secondary screening may make kidney biopsy unnecessary. Rituximab is comparable to cyclophosphamide and cyclosporine for idiopathic membranous nephropathy.

**Teaching points**
- At EUROIMMUN ELISA PLA2R antibody levels of at least 20 RU/mL with thorough secondary screening a kidney biopsy may be unnecessary, but lower or negative levels still warrant biopsy. [PMID 39820570]
- A meta-analysis found rituximab comparable in efficacy to cyclophosphamide and cyclosporine in idiopathic membranous nephropathy, with no significant difference in remission rates or severe adverse events, and treatment should be individualised. [PMID 41351015]
- Low-dose rituximab appears promising for primary membranous nephropathy as main-line and rescue therapy, but randomised trials are needed. [PMID 39508703]
- Glucocorticoid with cyclophosphamide was reviewed in membranous nephropathy, with limited data on comparisons against other drugs alone and on secondary disease. [PMID 39664513]
- Recurrent membranous nephropathy after kidney transplant has risk factors including living donor selection, pre-transplant anti-PLA2R levels, dialysis, induction immunosuppression and tacrolimus use. [PMID 40576095]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal rituximab dose.

**MCQ angles**
- PLA2R antibody to avoid biopsy.
- Secondary causes must be screened.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with nephrotic syndrome and positive anti-PLA2R antibodies.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Supportive care, KDIGO risk-based treatment thresholds and remission definitions are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39508703: Low-Dose Rituximab in the Treatment of Primary Membranous Nephropathy - A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `primary_mr` (topics 1419) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** For degenerative mitral regurgitation, repair is associated with lower mortality than replacement, and waiting for Class I triggers is linked to postoperative survival loss, supporting early surgery. Guideline-adherent surveillance every 12 months or less improves outcomes in asymptomatic severe primary MR.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found mitral valve repair beneficial compared with replacement for degenerative MR across ages in all-cause mortality, with higher early mortality and reoperation in the replacement group. [PMID 30471709]
- A second meta-analysis found mitral replacement possibly associated with higher long-term mortality than repair in primary MR, though replacement is often a bailout option in more complex anatomy. [PMID 32648008]
- A registry analysis of 2,833 surgical DMR patients found that waiting for Class I or isolated Class IIa triggers before operating is associated with postoperative survival loss, encouraging an early surgical strategy. [PMID 38996050]
- In asymptomatic severe primary MR, guideline adherence with evaluations every 12 months or less was associated with earlier therapy and better long-term survival free from death or heart failure hospitalisation. [PMID 34325105]
- Baseline global longitudinal strain independently predicts postoperative outcomes in primary MR, with impaired values linked to higher mortality, though a preoperative threshold needs further analysis. [PMID 33783012]
- Percutaneous edge-to-edge repair appears efficacious and safe in both functional and degenerative MR in a meta-analysis, with randomised studies awaited. [PMID 28663365]

**Uncertainties**
- Exact GLS threshold for intervention.

**MCQ angles**
- Repair preferred over replacement.
- Early surgery in asymptomatic severe MR.

**Case hooks**
- Asymptomatic patient with severe MR from mitral valve prolapse and preserved LVEF.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Class I trigger thresholds (LVEF, LVESD), flail leaflet and AF triggers, and TEER role in primary MR are not stated beyond the excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28663365: Outcome after percutaneous edge-to-edge mitral repair for functional and degenerative mitral regurgitation: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 30471709: Meta-Analysis Comparing Mitral Valve Repair Versus Replacement for Degenerative Mitral Regurgitation Across All Ages (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 32648008: Comparison of Mitral Valve Replacement and Repair for Degenerative Mitral Regurgitation: a Meta-analysis and Implications for Transcatheter Mitral Procedures (meta-analysis, 2020)

## Cluster `myelofibrosis` (topics 1420) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** German ONKOPEDIA and NCCN guidance were updated in 2025-2026 for primary myelofibrosis. Ruxolitinib-based combinations may enhance efficacy, with response influenced by prior JAK inhibitor exposure.

**Teaching points**
- The German ONKOPEDIA guideline 2025 for primary myelofibrosis notes that combination therapies and novel agents show efficacy and point to future developments but are not yet available for routine practice. [PMID 42324204]
- The ONKOPEDIA myelofibrosis recommendations were updated by an MPN expert panel, extending the earlier guideline in light of recent developments. [PMID 42324205]
- A meta-analysis of ruxolitinib-based combination therapy in myelofibrosis found added drugs may further enhance efficacy, with personalised selection essential because efficacy is influenced by prior JAK inhibitor exposure. [PMID 41761659]
- NCCN Guidelines Insights (version 2.2026) focus on recent updates for myelofibrosis within myeloproliferative neoplasms. [PMID 42425157]
- EBMT recommendations address allogeneic transplantation in non-classical MPNs and MDS/MPNs, citing a lack of robust evidence. [PMID 40106773]

**Uncertainties**
- Transplant timing and sequencing of newer agents.

**MCQ angles**
- JAK inhibitors in myelofibrosis.
- Role of allogeneic transplant.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with splenomegaly, constitutional symptoms and leukoerythroblastic film.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Prognostic scores, JAK2/CALR/MPL diagnosis and fedratinib or pacritinib use are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `icd_primary` (topics 1421) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ICD therapy for primary prevention is associated with lower all-cause mortality, sudden cardiac death and arrhythmic death in ischaemic and non-ischaemic cardiomyopathy. Patient selection by LVEF alone remains imperfect.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found ICD therapy was associated with reduced all-cause mortality, sudden cardiac death and arrhythmic death in both ischaemic and non-ischaemic cardiomyopathy. [PMID 37952790]
- Pooled cohort data after myocardial infarction could not identify low-risk individuals with severely reduced LVEF or high-risk individuals with preserved LVEF, using LVEF or other predictors. [PMID 39378245]
- In non-ischaemic cardiomyopathy, patients without a guideline ICD indication had comparable rates of appropriate ICD therapy, suggesting current indications leave out a significant group. [PMID 40354022]
- Non-sustained VT was modestly associated with appropriate ICD therapy (HR 1.82) but not with VF or mortality in a primary prevention cohort, suggesting its clinical role be reconsidered. [PMID 41967966]
- Subcutaneous ICDs remain effective in obese patients, but technical challenges and higher complication rates warrant attention. [PMID 39019386]
- A study found a gap between guideline indications and real-world prescription of wearable cardioverter-defibrillators after acute myocardial infarction. [PMID 41655635]

**Uncertainties**
- Reassessing ICD need after LVEF recovery on quadruple therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- LVEF threshold as imperfect risk marker.
- Subcutaneous ICD considerations.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with LVEF 30% after MI on optimal therapy considered for ICD.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Specific LVEF thresholds, timing after MI and DANISH-type trial results are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37952790: Implantable cardioverter defibrillators for primary prevention in patients with ischemic and non-ischemic cardiomyopathy: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39019386: Efficacy and safety of the subcutaneous implantable cardioverter-defibrillator in patients with and without obesity: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39378245: Sudden cardiac death after myocardial infarction: individual participant data from pooled cohorts (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40354022: Primary prevention ICD in non-ischaemic cardiomyopathy: an ongoing search for improvement of current indications : A retrospective study analysing the impact of the new Dutch guideline on the use of ICDs (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41655635: Gap between guideline-based indications and real-world implementation of wearable cardioverter-defibrillators after acute myocardial infarction (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41967966: Contribution of Non-Sustained Ventricular Tachycardia to the Primary Prevention of Sudden Cardiac Death　- Validation of Current Japanese Circulation Society / Japanese Heart Rhythm Society Guideline Recommendations in the Nippon Storm Study (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `psc` (topics 1422, 1423) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** PSC is rare with variable global incidence, a high risk of hepatobiliary malignancy and uneven guideline adherence. MR imaging consensus and paediatric PSC-IBD position papers guide care.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of population-based studies reports incidence and prevalence of PSC and calls for further epidemiology in Asia, Africa and Latin America. [PMID 38052097]
- A meta-analysis of hepatobiliary malignancy in PSC found patients with PSC-IBD may be at higher risk for HCC, and suggests data may guide surveillance strategies. [PMID 39709139]
- An ESGAR consensus statement defines MR imaging in PSC with a structured reporting template. [PMID 40285815]
- An ESPGHAN position paper gives guidance on diagnostic criteria, follow-up and therapeutic strategies for children with PSC and IBD. [PMID 39741383]
- A five-year North American analysis found gaps in guideline adherence in PSC, notably in baseline fibrosis assessment and hepatobiliary cancer imaging. [PMID 41964270]

- Effective medical therapy.

- Cholangiocarcinoma surveillance.

## Cluster `radiotherapy` (topics 1424) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Modern radiotherapy relies on site-specific guidelines. Stereotactic body radiotherapy is preferred for stage I NSCLC when surgery is unsuitable and is efficacious with low adverse events for bone metastases.

**Teaching points**
- The ACCP guideline states stereotactic body radiotherapy is the preferred approach for stage I NSCLC patients who are not appropriate surgical candidates. [PMID 40562304]
- ISRS-endorsed consensus on SBRT for non-spine bone metastases found it efficacious and associated with low adverse event rates. [PMID 39862925]
- An ESTRO guideline addresses patient selection for SBRT of spine metastases and safe implementation, and values prospective trial enrolment. [PMID 37925107]
- An ASTRO-ASCO-SSO guideline addresses postmastectomy radiation, including bolus use. [PMID 40955654]
- An ASTRO guideline addresses anal squamous cell carcinoma radiation dose, immunotherapy, adaptive RT and minimising long-term toxicity. [PMID 40023252]
- An international consensus defines clinical target volumes at different dose levels for nasopharyngeal carcinoma to improve coverage and standardisation. [PMID 40419028]

**Uncertainties**
- Dose-fractionation choices vary by site.

**MCQ angles**
- SBRT for inoperable early NSCLC.
- SBRT for oligometastatic bone disease.

**Case hooks**
- Frail patient with 2 cm peripheral stage I lung cancer.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** General physics principles, radiosensitivity and toxicity grading are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `procalcitonin` (topics 1425, 1426, 1427) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Procalcitonin has moderate accuracy for sepsis diagnosis and only slightly outperforms CRP. Guided discontinuation of antimicrobials in critically ill adults with sepsis is supported where antimicrobials would otherwise exceed seven days.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found procalcitonin offers moderate accuracy in diagnosing sepsis in adults admitted to the emergency department and distinguishes viral from bacterial inflammation rapidly. [PMID 38254218]
- Procalcitonin appears to slightly outperform CRP for sepsis diagnosis, but its discriminatory power remains limited. [PMID 40214293]
- A meta-analysis of PCT- or CRP-guided antimicrobial discontinuation in critically ill adults with sepsis supported guidance in Sepsis-3 where antimicrobials are used for more than 7 days, and called for studies of monitoring protocols and recurrence. [PMID 38949476]
- A GRADE-based guideline provides guidance on procalcitonin use in emergency departments. [PMID 39705853]
- In cirrhosis, PCT and presepsin have high sensitivity and specificity for bacterial infection, and PCT has good rule-in value for spontaneous bacterial peritonitis. [PMID 39958447]
- A diagnostic test accuracy meta-analysis evaluated procalcitonin and CRP for invasive and serious bacterial infection in young febrile infants. [PMID 38499017]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal stopping thresholds.

**MCQ angles**
- PCT-guided antibiotic discontinuation.
- PCT as rule-in test for SBP in cirrhosis.

**Case hooks**
- ICU patient with sepsis on day 5 antibiotics and falling procalcitonin.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Numeric PCT cut-offs and the effect on mortality are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38254218: Accuracy of procalcitonin for diagnosing sepsis in adult patients admitted to the emergency department: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38499017: Diagnostic test accuracy of procalcitonin and C-reactive protein for predicting invasive and serious bacterial infections in young febrile infants: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38949476: Benefits and Harms of Procalcitonin- or C-Reactive Protein-Guided Antimicrobial Discontinuation in Critically Ill Adults With Sepsis: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39705853: GRADE-based procalcitonin guideline for emergency departments (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `procedural_sedation` (topics 1428) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Remimazolam is a propofol alternative for procedural sedation with less hypotension and injection pain; elderly patients show more stable haemodynamics. ED sedation complications are usually respiratory rather than cardiovascular.

**Teaching points**
- Meta-analyses found remimazolam could be used as an alternative to propofol for procedural sedation and general anaesthesia, with no difference in sedation success, recovery or discharge, but more data are needed for general anaesthesia. [PMID 37334113, 36326772]
- Remimazolam lowered the incidence of hypotension and injection pain compared with propofol across procedural sedation and general anaesthesia. [PMID 40503766]
- In elderly patients, remimazolam gave more stable haemodynamics after induction and may be safer than propofol. [PMID 39991057]
- Remimazolam did not increase postoperative delirium risk and maintained cognitive function versus propofol. [PMID 39748753]
- In emergency department and ICU sedation, recovery is shorter with propofol versus midazolam-opioids, and respiratory adverse events are less common with ketamine. [PMID 38185564]
- In adult emergency department procedural sedation, respiratory complications occur more often than hypotension, while laryngospasm, aspiration and intubation are rare but life-threatening. [PMID 39102153]

**Uncertainties**
- Haemodynamic benefit of remimazolam in induction is partly inconclusive.

**MCQ angles**
- Respiratory depression is the main risk.
- Ketamine causes fewer respiratory events.

**Case hooks**
- Elderly patient needing cardioversion under procedural sedation.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Fasting rules, monitoring standards and reversal agents are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36326772: Remimazolam versus propofol for procedural sedation and anesthesia: a systemic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37334113: Remimazolam <i>versus</i> propofol for procedural sedation: a meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38185564: Pharmacological agents for procedural sedation and analgesia in the emergency department and intensive care unit: a systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomised trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39102153: Safety of procedural sedation in emergency department settings among the adult population: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39748753: Effect of remimazolam on postoperative delirium and cognitive function in adults undergoing general anesthesia or procedural sedation: a meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39991057: Comparison of the safety of remimazolam and propofol during general anesthesia in elderly patients: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40503766: Anesthetic efficacy with remimazolam compared with propofol: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `as_prognosis` (topics 1429) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Aortic stenosis prognosis worsens with heart failure and cardiac amyloidosis; TAVR is safe in amyloidosis and improves survival over medical therapy or SAVR. Cerebral embolic protection is not supported routinely.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found cardiac amyloidosis is associated with increased mortality in severe aortic stenosis, and TAVR is safe and improves survival more than medical therapy or SAVR. [PMID 39955158]
- An HFA consensus statement covers management of aortic stenosis with chronic heart failure, focusing on pre- and post-procedural care in patients undergoing AVR. [PMID 40948025]
- In aortic stenosis with coronary disease, PCI+TAVR was associated with higher mortality or adverse events than CABG+SAVR, but lower perioperative complications. [PMID 41067128]
- In low and intermediate-risk patients, TAVR versus SAVR shows short-term benefits, but higher permanent pacemaker, paravalvular leak and reintervention rates raise concerns for younger, lower-risk patients. [PMID 40425422]
- Evidence does not support routine cerebral embolic protection during TAVR given no significant benefit in key outcomes. [PMID 41729166, 40910674]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term durability of TAVR in younger patients.

**MCQ angles**
- Amyloidosis in severe AS.
- TAVR versus SAVR in low-risk patients.

**Case hooks**
- Octogenarian with severe AS, heart failure and suspected amyloid.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Natural history after symptom onset and the timing of intervention in asymptomatic patients are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39955158: Outcomes of aortic stenosis in patients with cardiac amyloidosis: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40425422: TAVR vs. SAVR for severe aortic stenosis in the low and intermediate surgical risk population: An updated meta-analysis, meta-regression, and trial sequential analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40910674: Efficacy of Cerebral Embolic Protection Device in Transcatheter Aortic Valve Replacement: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

- PMID 41067128: Transcatheter versus surgical treatment in aortic stenosis with coronary artery disease: A meta-analysis of time-to-event data on 162,305 patients (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `ipf_prognosis` (topics 1430) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** IPF carries high mortality: pooled 3-year survival is about 60-67%, and untreated mean overall survival is around 4 years. Antifibrotic therapy reduces all-cause mortality and acute exacerbation risk, and DLCO decline predicts death better than FVC in one registry.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found pooled 3-year cumulative survival of 59.9% before 2010 and 66.2% in the 2010s, noting no improving trend before 2010 and probable multifactorial improvement afterwards. [PMID 35295232]
- A systematic review of untreated IPF found pooled mortality of 12% at 1-2 years, 38% at 2-5 years and 69% at 5 years or more, with mean overall survival about 4 years. [PMID 32759374]
- A meta-analysis found antifibrotic treatment reduces all-cause mortality and the risk of acute exacerbation (pooled RR 0.63), with the acute exacerbation effect consistent for nintedanib but not pirfenidone. [PMID 34217681]
- Antifibrotic therapy protects against FVC decline in progressive pulmonary fibrosis, with similar efficacy for nintedanib and pirfenidone in IPF and non-IPF disease. [PMID 34895203]
- In an IPF registry, DLCO changes were a better predictor of mortality than FVC changes, and age 70 or above, high HRCT interstitial scores and DLCO fall of 15% or more at 12 months were adverse factors. [PMID 28862397]
- Real-world mortality and adverse events with pirfenidone and nintedanib were higher than in previous trials, with pirfenidone patients showing higher mortality. [PMID 38963453]

**Uncertainties**
- Real-world safety differences between antifibrotics.

**MCQ angles**
- Antifibrotics slow FVC decline and reduce exacerbations.
- DLCO as a prognostic marker.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with IPF and 10% FVC decline over 6 months.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** GAP index, transplant referral criteria and diagnostic criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28862397: EMPIRE Registry, Czech Part: Impact of demographics, pulmonary function and HRCT on survival and clinical course in idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 32759374: Prognosis of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis without anti-fibrotic therapy: a systematic review (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 34217681: Impact of Antifibrotic Therapy on Mortality and Acute Exacerbation in Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34895203: Efficacy of antifibrotic drugs, nintedanib and pirfenidone, in treatment of progressive pulmonary fibrosis in both idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (IPF) and non-IPF: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35295232: Mortality and survival in idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 38963453: Real-world safety and effectiveness of pirfenidone and nintedanib in the treatment of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `psp` (topics 1431) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Evidence retrieved for PSP is diagnostic biomarker work: neurofilament light, neutrophil-lymphocyte ratio, transcranial sonography and susceptibility mapping. Dysphagia is common. Clinical diagnostic criteria are not covered.

**Teaching points**
- Dysphagia prevalence in PSP was pooled in a systematic review and meta-analysis. [PMID 38523230]
- Neurofilament light levels differ between certain atypical parkinsonian syndromes, with MSA showing the highest levels among Parkinson's disease and related disorders. [PMID 40180649]
- Transcranial sonography may be an important supplementary biomarker for diagnosing PSP, but its value for discriminating PSP from other atypical parkinsonism and between phenotypes needs more exploration. [PMID 37819487]
- In PSP the altered peripheral immune profile is mainly driven by neutrophils, which may inform targeted therapies. [PMID 39162120]
- Quantitative susceptibility mapping was assessed for differentiating Parkinson's disease from atypical parkinsonism, with standardised acquisition recommended. [PMID 42035898]

**Uncertainties**
- No retrieved evidence on treatment.

**MCQ angles**
- Dysphagia is a common PSP feature.

**Case hooks**
- Older patient with early falls, vertical gaze palsy and poor levodopa response.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Hummingbird sign, vertical gaze findings and diagnostic criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin and needs a clinical source.

## Cluster `prone_positioning` (topics 1433) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Awake proning in COVID-19 hypoxaemic respiratory failure is associated with better survival without intubation, particularly with longer daily duration. In ARDS, prone positioning improved survival on VV-ECMO in a meta-analysis.

**Teaching points**
- An individual-participant-data meta-analysis found awake prone positioning in COVID-19 was associated with increased survival without intubation and reduced intubation and mortality, with 10 or more hours per day linked to better outcomes. [PMID 40063016]
- A meta-analysis supports prone positioning in conscious ICU patients with COVID-19 pneumonia to reduce mortality and intubation. [PMID 38245784]
- A rapid practice guideline recommends a trial of awake prone positioning in acute hypoxaemic respiratory failure due to COVID-19, with monitoring by staff able to detect deterioration quickly. [PMID 36691710]
- In ARDS patients receiving VV-ECMO, a meta-analysis found survival improved when prone positioning was used. [PMID 35037993]
- A meta-analysis found prone positioning is most beneficial in ARDS with higher BMI and longer durations. [PMID 35672215]

**Uncertainties**
- ECMO plus proning needs randomised data.

**MCQ angles**
- Duration of prone sessions matters.
- Awake proning versus ventilated proning.

**Case hooks**
- Hypoxaemic COVID-19 patient on high-flow nasal oxygen.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Standard criteria (PaO2/FiO2 threshold and 16-hour sessions) for ventilated severe ARDS are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35037993: Effect of prone positioning on survival in adult patients receiving venovenous extracorporeal membrane oxygenation for acute respiratory distress syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35672215: Effectiveness of prone position in acute respiratory distress syndrome and moderating factors of obesity class and treatment durations for COVID-19 patients: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36691710: Awake proning in patients with COVID-19-related hypoxemic acute respiratory failure: A rapid practice guideline (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38245784: Prone positioning effect on tracheal intubation rate, mortality and oxygenation parameters in awake non-intubated severe COVID-19-induced respiratory failure: a review of reviews (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40063016: Awake Prone Positioning in Adults With COVID-19: An Individual Participant Data Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `prostate_cancer` (topics 1434) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Current guidelines from NCCN, EAU and AUA/ASTRO emphasise shared decision-making across the disease course. In mCRPC, ASCO recommends radium-223 for symptomatic bone-only disease, and sequencing evidence is lacking.

**Teaching points**
- NCCN Prostate Cancer version 3.2026 emphasises shared decision-making in all disease settings based on preferences, prior treatment, biomarkers, extent and location of metastases, symptoms and side effects. [PMID 41213253]
- The ASCO mCRPC guideline recommends radium-223 for patients with symptomatic bone-only disease, and notes evidence for optimal sequencing of mCRPC regimens is lacking. [PMID 40315400]
- The AUA/SUO early detection guideline update (2026) revises recommendations on biomarkers and atypical small acinar proliferation. [PMID 41744286]
- The AUA/ASTRO guideline amendment for clinically localised prostate cancer adds shared decision-making emphasis and new direction on radiation and disease management. [PMID 41988960]
- EAU guidelines 2026 note that diagnosis, staging and treatment of localised prostate cancer evolve rapidly, with a second part covering relapsing and metastatic disease. [PMID 42457454, 42457456]
- The French AFU update guides management of recurrent or metastatic prostate cancer according to patient and disease characteristics, prior therapies, genomic status and molecular imaging. [PMID 39581665]

**Uncertainties**
- Sequencing of systemic therapy in mCRPC.

**MCQ angles**
- Radium-223 for symptomatic bone-only mCRPC.
- Shared decision-making in screening and treatment.

**Case hooks**
- Man with rising PSA after radical prostatectomy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** PSA screening thresholds, risk groups and ADT regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `pji` (topics 1435, 1436, 1437, 1438) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence centres on PJI prevention and diagnosis: AAOS guidance, morbid obesity as a risk factor, topical vancomycin powder, and histology and sequencing tests. Retention and revision strategy is not covered.

**Teaching points**
- An AAOS clinical practice guideline summary addresses prevention of periprosthetic joint infection in total hip and knee arthroplasty, highlighting gaps in the literature. [PMID 40743492]
- Morbidly obese patients have markedly increased risk of PJI and other complications after primary THA, which should be discussed while preserving access to care. [PMID 40198881]
- Topical vancomycin powder appears to reduce PJI risk in total joint arthroplasty, but increased wound complications call for caution. [PMID 40971665]
- Patients with multiple prosthetic joints are at higher risk for metachronous PJI after a single-joint infection. [PMID 40102953]
- Histology differentiating septic from aseptic cases advocates a threshold of at least five PMNs per high-power field in at least five fields. [PMID 41173031]
- Metagenomic and targeted next-generation sequencing show comparable diagnostic accuracy for PJI and are valuable for confirming infection. [PMID 41270974]
- Infection is a leading cause of failure of revision total knee arthroplasty, alongside instability and aseptic loosening. [PMID 40669519]

**Uncertainties**
- High-quality evidence on prevention remains limited.

**MCQ angles**
- Histology PMN threshold.
- Obesity as a PJI risk factor.

**Case hooks**
- Painful knee replacement 6 months after surgery with raised CRP.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** DAIR versus one-stage versus two-stage revision, antibiotic regimens and MSIS criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40102953: Risk factors for metachronous periprosthetic joint infection in patients with multiple prosthetic joints: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40198881: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Periprosthetic Joint Infection Rates in Morbidly Obese Patients Undergoing Total Hip Arthroplasty (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40669519: Etiology of Failure in Revision Total Knee Arthroplasty: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 40743492: AAOS Clinical Practice Guideline Summary Prevention of Total Hip and Knee Arthroplasty Periprosthetic Joint Infection in Patients Undergoing Dental Procedures (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40971665: Clinical efficacy of topical vancomycin powder in the prevention of surgical site infection in total joint arthroplasty: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41173031: Histological analysis in the diagnosis of periprosthetic joint infection of the hip and knee : a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41270974: Comparative diagnostic accuracy of metagenomic next-generation sequencing and targeted next-generation sequencing for periprosthetic joint infection: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `valve_thrombosis` (topics 1439) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Mechanical valve thrombosis management has changed little for decades and guidelines differ. Evidence retrieved on bioprosthetic and subclinical valve thrombosis after TAVI shows stroke risk and uncertain antithrombotic strategy.

**Teaching points**
- A review of mechanical valve thrombosis describes current management and differences between guidelines, noting no significant recent changes in management. [PMID 37499958]
- An ACC/AHA versus ESC comparison highlights differences in prosthetic heart valve management in areas of controversy. [PMID 30947924]
- Clinical bioprosthetic valve thrombosis after TAVI is associated with significantly increased stroke risk. [PMID 33065085]
- A meta-analysis of subclinical valve thrombosis after TAVI suggests anticoagulation or antiplatelet therapy may prevent it, but optimal antithrombotic regimen needs further study. [PMID 32217021]

**Uncertainties**
- Thrombolysis versus surgery is not covered in the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Stroke risk with bioprosthetic valve thrombosis.

**Case hooks**
- Mechanical mitral valve patient with subtherapeutic INR and new dyspnoea.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Thrombolysis versus surgery decisions, obstructive versus non-obstructive thresholds and INR targets are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Needs a source before use.

**Sources**

## Cluster `ple` (topics 1440) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Protein-losing enteropathy retrieved evidence is mostly Fontan-associated: budesonide can raise albumin in Fontan PLE, and paediatric nutrition guidance exists. Other causes of PLE are not covered.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review and meta-analysis found budesonide can increase albumin in patients with protein-losing enteropathy after Fontan operation, though outcome impact needs study. [PMID 31835979]
- Consensus severity-graded definitions of Fontan-associated complications were developed to characterise Fontan circulatory failure. [PMID 38452996]
- A review of long-term outcomes of Fontan patients with an extracardiac conduit found several adverse outcomes needing consensus-based definitions as the population ages. [PMID 39952466]
- A paediatric guideline outlines nutrition management of enteropathy, including dietary education, counselling and monitoring. [PMID 38849288]

**Uncertainties**
- Non-Fontan causes of PLE.

**MCQ angles**
- PLE as a Fontan complication.
- Budesonide raises albumin.

**Case hooks**
- Fontan patient with oedema, low albumin and diarrhoea.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnosis by faecal alpha-1 antitrypsin and other causes (Crohn's, lymphangiectasia, SLE) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `proteinuria` (topics 1441) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence concerns testing and handling rather than interpretation: albuminuria testing adherence is poor, KDIGO categories track comorbidity burden, and standardised urine collection and handling reduce variability.

**Teaching points**
- A review found uACR testing rates in people with diabetes were suboptimal (1.5% to 76.6%, generally below 50%), highlighting poor guideline adherence. [PMID 41087290]
- A systematic review found guideline-recommended albuminuria testing allows timely identification, referral and treatment, whereas late referral led to higher costs and worse outcomes including higher mortality. [PMID 37644841]
- A systematic review by KDIGO categories found comorbidities become more prevalent with increasing albuminuria severity, with the highest burden in high-risk and very-high-risk groups. [PMID 33231861]
- A consensus on pre-analytical urine handling stresses standardised collection and handling to minimise variability, covering timing of collection, repeated measurements and conditions causing false-positive results. [PMID 42029072]
- Stage 1 and stage 2 hypertension (2017 ACC/AHA definitions) were associated with higher incidence of proteinuria than normal blood pressure. [PMID 35063272]
- Exercise training did not aggravate proteinuria in adult CKD patients without renal replacement therapy. [PMID 32393200]
- Elevated liver stiffness in NAFLD is associated with increased odds of kidney outcomes. [PMID 35053253]

**Uncertainties**
- Interpretation thresholds and aetiology are not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- uACR for screening in diabetes and hypertension.
- Albuminuria category and CKD risk.

**Case hooks**
- Diabetic patient with persistent albuminuria on repeat uACR.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** uACR versus uPCR thresholds, nephrotic-range definition, orthostatic proteinuria, causes and referral criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

**Sources**
- PMID 32393200: Effects of exercise training on proteinuria in adult patients with chronic kidney disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 33231861: Burden of Chronic Kidney Disease by KDIGO Categories of Glomerular Filtration Rate and Albuminuria: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 35053253: Liver Stiffness, Albuminuria and Chronic Kidney Disease in Patients with NAFLD: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35063272: Risk for Proteinuria in Newly Defined Hypertensive People Based on the 2017 American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Blood Pressure Guideline (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37644841: The drivers of non-adherence to albuminuria testing guidelines and the clinical and economic impact of not identifying chronic kidney disease (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 41087290: A review of albuminuria testing rates in people with diabetes mellitus: poor guideline adherence (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42029072: Recommendations for pre-analytical urine sample handling to allow optimal assessment of albuminuria and proteinuria (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `factor_v_prothrombin` (topics 1442) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Thrombophilia testing is variable in practice. Women with prothrombin G20210A mutation should generally not receive thrombosis prophylaxis in pregnancy based on thrombophilia and family history alone, and mild thrombophilia adds only modest risk with combined oral contraceptives.

**Teaching points**
- A Bayesian meta-analysis found women with factor V Leiden or prothrombin G20210A mutation should generally not be prescribed thrombosis prophylaxis in pregnancy on the basis of thrombophilia and family history alone. [PMID 29074563]
- Mild thrombophilia confers only a modest additive VTE risk with combined oral contraceptives, which can be offered when no other risk factors exist and alternatives are not tolerated. [PMID 27121914]
- Inherited thrombophilia is associated with increased risk of arterial ischaemic stroke in adults, with management implications needing further study. [PMID 31549567]
- An ISTH survey found thrombophilia testing practice is mostly restricted to single-variant testing, with multigene panels used by a minority of laboratories, and recommendations on when to test are needed. [PMID 41238194]
- An ACMG technical standard (2025 revision) governs laboratory testing for factor V Leiden and factor II c.*97G>A. [PMID 40616445]

**Uncertainties**
- Who should be tested.

**MCQ angles**
- Pregnancy prophylaxis is not indicated by thrombophilia alone.
- COC use in mild thrombophilia.

**Case hooks**
- Woman with heterozygous prothrombin mutation and family history asking about pregnancy prophylaxis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Absolute VTE risk figures and homozygous or compound states are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27121914: Combined oral contraceptives, thrombophilia and the risk of venous thromboembolism: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 29074563: Pregnancy, thrombophilia, and the risk of a first venous thrombosis: systematic review and bayesian meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31549567: Inherited Thrombophilia and the Risk of Arterial Ischemic Stroke: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 40616445: Venous thromboembolism laboratory testing (factor V Leiden and factor II c.∗97G&gt;A), 2025 revision: A technical standard of the American College of Medical Genetics and Genomics (ACMG) (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41238194: Survey on current practice in thrombophilia testing: from phenotype to genotype. Communication from the SSC of the ISTH (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `septic_shock_protocol` (topics 1443) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Surviving Sepsis Campaign guidelines are the main evidence framework: the 2016 adult guideline issued 32 strong, 39 weak and 18 best-practice statements. Shorter delays to lactate, cultures, fluids and antibiotics are associated with better outcomes.

**Teaching points**
- The 2016 Surviving Sepsis Campaign guideline gave 32 strong recommendations, 39 weak recommendations and 18 best-practice statements, with substantial expert agreement. [PMID 28098591]
- The 2021 adult Surviving Sepsis guideline serves as a bedside decision aid for adults with suspected or confirmed septic shock and sepsis-associated organ dysfunction. [PMID 35170043]
- A study of delays within the 3-hour bundle found shorter delays are associated with better outcomes, with significant delay thresholds of about 20 minutes for lactate, 50 minutes for blood cultures, 100 minutes for crystalloids and 125 minutes for antibiotics, and no evidence that 3 hours is safe. [PMID 29298189]
- A review aligned with Surviving Sepsis 2021 suggests preserving glycocalyx integrity through fluid volume changes, minimising catecholamine use, controlling hyperglycaemia and possibly corticosteroids and anticoagulants. [PMID 38658435]
- The 2026 paediatric Surviving Sepsis guidelines include 20 new recommendations and note only three are based on high or moderate certainty evidence. [PMID 41869844]

**Uncertainties**
- Protocolised versus usual care trials are not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Time to antibiotics matters.
- Surviving Sepsis bundle elements.

**Case hooks**
- Septic shock patient with lactate 5 mmol/L after 30 mL/kg fluids.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** ProCESS, ARISE and ProMISe trial results (protocolised versus goal-directed resuscitation) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28098591: Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock: 2016 (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 29298189: Delay Within the 3-Hour Surviving Sepsis Campaign Guideline on Mortality for Patients With Severe Sepsis and Septic Shock (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 35170043: Surviving sepsis campaign: International guidelines for management of sepsis and septic shock in adults 2021 - endorsement by the Scandinavian society of anaesthesiology and intensive care medicine (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 38658435: Managing sepsis and septic shock in an endothelial glycocalyx-friendly way: from the viewpoint of surviving sepsis campaign guidelines (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 41869844: Surviving Sepsis Campaign International Guidelines for the Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock in Children 2026 (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `pseudohypoparathyroidism` (topics 1444) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The first international consensus on pseudohypoparathyroidism recommends coordinated multidisciplinary care from infancy through adulthood with a transition programme. Genotype-phenotype work links PHP1a variants to round faces and subcutaneous ossifications.

**Teaching points**
- The first international consensus statement on pseudohypoparathyroidism and related disorders recommends a coordinated multidisciplinary approach from infancy through adulthood, including a transition programme. [PMID 29959430]
- An updated practical tool for physicians restates that long-term coordinated multidisciplinary care is recommended, starting as early as possible in infancy. [PMID 32756064]
- The paediatric implications of the consensus include insulin resistance, impaired glucose tolerance or type 2 diabetes, hypertension, ectopic ossification and impaired neurocognitive development. [PMID 30591400]
- A systematic review of PHP1a genotype-phenotype found associations between specific variants and round faces and subcutaneous ossifications. [PMID 37837607]
- A systematic review of dental findings in non-surgical hypoparathyroidism and pseudohypoparathyroidism found associations with enamel hypoplasia need larger studies. [PMID 29971010]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term outcomes with treatment.

**MCQ angles**
- PTH resistance with high PTH and low calcium.
- Albright hereditary osteodystrophy features.

**Case hooks**
- Child with round face, short stature and hypocalcaemia with raised PTH.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic biochemistry and GNAS imprinting classification are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29959430: Diagnosis and management of pseudohypoparathyroidism and related disorders: first international Consensus Statement (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 29971010: Dental Findings in Patients With Non-surgical Hypoparathyroidism and Pseudohypoparathyroidism: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2018)

## Cluster `bronchiectasis_pseudomonas` (topics 1445) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Eradication therapy for Pseudomonas in bronchiectasis clears sputum in about 40% at 12 months, with higher rates when systemic and inhaled antibiotics are combined. Long-term inhaled antibiotics reduce bacterial density and exacerbation risk but raise wheeze and bronchospasm.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found eradication treatment for P. aeruginosa in bronchiectasis results in eradication from sputum in about 40% at 12 months, with higher rates when combined systemic and inhaled antibiotics are used. [PMID 38296344]
- Long-term inhaled antibiotics in non-CF bronchiectasis reduce sputum bacterial density, increase P. aeruginosa eradication and reduce exacerbation risk, but cause more wheeze and bronchospasm. [PMID 25620629]
- Inhaled tobramycin for chronic Pseudomonas infection in non-CF bronchiectasis raised tolerability concerns, and a network meta-analysis was suggested to compare inhaled antibiotics. [PMID 33307314]
- The ERS clinical practice guideline provides an evidence-based framework for managing adult bronchiectasis. [PMID 41016738]
- A Chinese expert consensus recommends single active antimicrobial therapy with adequate pulmonary concentration for P. aeruginosa lower respiratory infection in patients without significant underlying disease. [PMID 35927044]

**Uncertainties**
- Which inhaled antibiotic is best.

**MCQ angles**
- Pseudomonas eradication after first isolation.
- Bronchospasm with inhaled antibiotics.

**Case hooks**
- Bronchiectasis patient with new Pseudomonas isolate in sputum.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Specific eradication regimens and long-term macrolide use are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 25620629: Efficacy and safety of long-term inhaled antibiotic for patients with noncystic fibrosis bronchiectasis: a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 33307314: Inhaled tobramycin for chronic infection with pseudomonas aeruginosa in non-cystic fibrosis bronchiectasis: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35927044: [Chinese expert consensus on the management of lower respiratory tract infections of <i>Pseudomonas aeruginosa</i> in adults(2022)] (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 38296344: Eradication treatment for <i>Pseudomonas aeruginosa</i> infection in adults with bronchiectasis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 41016738: European Respiratory Society clinical practice guideline for the management of adult bronchiectasis (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `pseudomyxoma` (topics 1446) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Multisociety GRADE consensus supports cytoreductive surgery with HIPEC for pseudomyxoma peritonei, and PSOGI endorses HIPEC after CRS using CDDP/MMC or high-dose MMC regimens.

**Teaching points**
- A multisocietal GRADE consensus makes recommendations on cytoreductive surgery and HIPEC for pseudomyxoma peritonei, intended to raise awareness of the benefits in this rare disease. [PMID 40817850]
- The 2022 PSOGI consensus recommends HIPEC following CRS for pseudomyxoma peritonei and endorses CDDP/MMC and high-dose MMC regimens. [PMID 39008204]
- A review of national guidelines found concordance for CRS and HIPEC in pseudomyxoma peritonei and mesothelioma, but none for gastric cancer, and called for high-level evidence. [PMID 40413333]
- The PSOGI-ESGO-ISSPP Lyon consensus aims to standardise cytoreductive surgery techniques in peritoneal malignancy. [PMID 41926971]
- A meta-analysis of CRS plus HIPEC in advanced epithelial ovarian cancer after neoadjuvant chemotherapy found improved progression-free survival, with acute kidney failure in 10.6% receiving HIPEC. [PMID 39904852]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal HIPEC regimen.

**MCQ angles**
- CRS plus HIPEC as treatment of pseudomyxoma peritonei.
- Renal toxicity with cisplatin HIPEC.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with jelly belly and appendiceal mucinous neoplasm.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Peritoneal Cancer Index, completeness of cytoreduction and survival figures are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

**Mentor intro.** EULAR 2023 recommendations integrate current drugs in a progressive approach to PsA, and EULAR and GRAPPA now define difficult-to-manage and treatment-refractory PsA. IL-17, IL-12/23 and IL-23 inhibitors show favourable efficacy and safety.

**Teaching points**
- The 2023 EULAR recommendations integrate all currently available drugs in a practical and progressive approach to pharmacological management of PsA, addressing remission. [PMID 38499325]
- EULAR and GRAPPA propose consensus definitions for difficult-to-manage and treatment-refractory PsA, which need prospective validation. [PMID 41168056, 41310208]
- A network meta-analysis of IL-17, IL-12/23 and IL-23 inhibitors for PsA found a favourable balance of relatively high efficacy and low risk. [PMID 41050680]
- The APLAR recommendations for PsA were developed from available evidence and region-specific considerations by rheumatologists, dermatologists and patients. [PMID 40725984]

- Sequencing of biologics.

**MCQ angles**
- Definition of difficult-to-manage PsA.
- IL-17 and IL-23 inhibitors.

## Cluster `pnes` (topics 1453) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The AAN functional seizures guideline covers management. Psychological treatment improves non-seizure outcomes beyond seizure frequency, and psychiatric comorbidities are common.

**Teaching points**
- The AAN guideline executive summary on management of functional seizures identifies evidence gaps and avenues for future research, including medications in people without another indication. [PMID 41370742]
- A meta-analysis found psychological treatment of functional seizures is associated with clinical improvements across a broad array of non-seizure outcomes beyond seizure frequency. [PMID 37157188]
- A systematic review linked early trauma, abuse and neglect to a combination of psychiatric and functional conditions including PTSD, BPD, FND and PNES. [PMID 39619491]
- Cognitive performance in functional seizures is slightly less severely impaired than in many patients with epilepsy but still argues for clinical assessment. [PMID 38879275]
- A systematic review suggests PNES is more common than previously thought. [PMID 34126844]
- Paediatric PNES recommendations advise screening for mental health and neurodevelopmental difficulties. [PMID 37804168]

**Uncertainties**
- Best psychological modality.

**MCQ angles**
- Psychotherapy as the mainstay of PNES treatment.
- PTSD in epilepsy and PNES.

**Case hooks**

## Cluster `psychosis` (topics 1454, 1455) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** A trial of clozapine is supported after two other antipsychotics fail. Cognitive deficits are common in first-episode psychosis, and sex-sensitive prescribing guidance exists for females.

**Teaching points**
- Analysis of sequential antipsychotic trials found response falls at later stages and supports a clozapine trial after two other antipsychotics have been ineffective. [PMID 38493023]
- ACKR1/Duffy-null genotype testing for clozapine was proposed as cost-effective for facilitating clozapine access in treatment-resistant schizophrenia. [PMID 42125771]
- Antipsychotic-naive first-episode psychosis shows cognitive deficits with high variability, suggesting a role for cognitive remediation in more severely affected individuals. [PMID 38416480]
- A clinical practice guideline addresses choice of first antipsychotic for females with first-episode psychosis, supporting safer, sex-sensitive prescribing. [PMID 41793755]
- Psychiatrists' treatment preferences for schizophrenia diverge from guidelines in places, highlighting the need to integrate evidence and clinical consensus. [PMID 40745910]

**Uncertainties**
- Acute behavioural disturbance management is not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Clozapine for treatment-resistant schizophrenia.
- Benign ethnic neutropenia and clozapine.

**Case hooks**
- Patient failed two antipsychotics at adequate dose.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Rapid tranquillisation, risk assessment and first-line drug choice for acute psychosis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38416480: Cognitive Function and Variability in Antipsychotic Drug-Naive Patients With First-Episode Psychosis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `ptsd` (topics 1456, 1457) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Trauma-focused psychotherapy is the core PTSD treatment: EMDR performs comparably to TF-CBT. Guideline evidence for complex presentations is of low certainty, and NICE-derived advice favours psychotherapy when insomnia is present.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found EMDR was significantly better than waitlist or usual care, with no significant difference in treatment effect between EMDR and TF-CBT. [PMID 40616777]
- A guideline with five meta-analyses on psychotherapies for adults with complex PTSD rated the certainty of evidence as low, including for targeting dissociation, and called for high-quality trials. [PMID 40234083]
- A review of NICE-derived interventions advises psychotherapy when insomnia symptoms are present, with psychotherapy particularly recommended for females and pharmacotherapy emphasised for males. [PMID 40665297]
- The updated German S3 guideline sets trauma-informed care as a standard approach in health institutions and covers exercise and physical activity. [PMID 42593701]
- A network meta-analysis suggests yoga with meditation and mindfulness may help adolescents with PTSD, but evidence is limited. [PMID 40001042]
- An Australian guideline development process is addressing MDMA-assisted psychotherapy for PTSD. [PMID 40683535]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of pharmacotherapy versus psychotherapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Trauma-focused CBT and EMDR as first-line.
- Complex PTSD and dissociation.

**Case hooks**
- Combat veteran with nightmares and avoidance.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** SSRI/venlafaxine evidence and diagnostic criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40001042: Mind-body intervention for post-traumatic stress disorder in adolescents: a systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40234083: Psychotherapies for adults with complex presentations of PTSD: a clinical guideline and five systematic reviews with meta-analyses (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40616777: Clinical and cost-effectiveness of eye movement desensitization and reprocessing for treatment and prevention of post-traumatic stress disorder in adults: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `pah` (topics 1459, 1460, 1470) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Sotatercept improves haemodynamics and function in PAH and lowers all-cause mortality and right ventricular failure in one meta-analysis, with mild thrombocytopenia. Guidance exists for intermediate-risk patients and portopulmonary hypertension.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials found sotatercept improves pulmonary haemodynamics in PAH while reducing all-cause mortality and right ventricular failure, with a mild increase in thrombocytopenia risk. [PMID 40668414]
- A second meta-analysis found sotatercept improves functional and haemodynamic outcomes with a favourable safety profile, though mortality benefit remains uncertain. [PMID 40602626]
- A Belgian consensus places subcutaneous sotatercept in the future PAH treatment strategy and notes effects on platelet counts and haemoglobin. [PMID 39377139]
- An expert consensus identifies evidence gaps for intermediate-risk prevalent PAH patients, including switching within drug classes, adding a prostacyclin-pathway drug, and switching PDE5i to riociguat. [PMID 39265796]
- The International Liver Transplantation Society updated its practice guideline on portopulmonary hypertension from expert opinion and available evidence. [PMID 40094355]
- A thoracic society statement addresses interventional strategies for children with progressive pulmonary hypertension despite optimal therapy. [PMID 39531626]

**Uncertainties**
- Sequencing of sotatercept with combination therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Sotatercept targets activin signalling (not in abstracts).
- Thrombocytopenia as adverse effect.

**Case hooks**
- PAH patient at intermediate risk on dual oral therapy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** WHO classification, haemodynamic definitions, risk scoring and CTD-PAH specifics are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `pa_catheter` (topics 1462) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** In cardiogenic shock, pulmonary artery catheter use is associated with lower mortality in observational pooled analyses, but randomised data are lacking. Echocardiography and thermodilution cardiac output measurements are broadly comparable.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of PAC monitoring in cardiogenic shock found a lower incidence of short-term mortality in adjusted observational studies, with prospective studies needed to confirm. [PMID 35344825]
- A further meta-analysis found PAC use in cardiogenic shock may be associated with decreased mortality and supports a randomised trial. [PMID 37100652]
- Right ventricular injury in ARDS was associated with increased overall and short-term mortality. [PMID 34020703]
- Echocardiographic and thermodilution cardiac output measurement showed no significant overall difference, and the techniques may be interchangeable under certain conditions. [PMID 31581196]
- A meta-analysis of cardiac output monitors in septic shock noted that real-time decision parameters were rarely assessed. [PMID 40652247]
- Passive leg raising predicts fluid responsiveness with good specificity but poor sensitivity when assessed by changes in pulse pressure. [PMID 26825952]

**Uncertainties**
- Randomised benefit of PAC.

**MCQ angles**
- PAC in cardiogenic shock.
- Fluid responsiveness tests.

**Case hooks**
- Patient in cardiogenic shock with uncertain filling pressures on inotropes.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Historical RCTs (ESCAPE, PAC-Man) and waveform interpretation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 26825952: Passive leg raising for predicting fluid responsiveness: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 31581196: Cardiac output measurements via echocardiography versus thermodilution: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 34020703: The impact of right ventricular injury on the mortality in patients with acute respiratory distress syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35344825: Prognostic implications of pulmonary artery catheter monitoring in patients with cardiogenic shock: A systematic review and meta-analysis of observational studies (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37100652: Clinical impact of pulmonary artery catheter in patients with cardiogenic shock: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 40652247: Cardiac output monitors in septic shock: do they deliver what matters? A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
