**Teaching points**
- The 2026 AHA/ACC multisociety guideline addresses diagnosis and management of acute PE including pharmacological therapy, advanced interventional therapies and in-hospital support. [PMID 41712677]
- Troponin is a strong and consistent predictor of short-term mortality and clinical deterioration in acute PE. [PMID 41121686]
- An ISTH review of risk stratification found substantial evidence gaps persist, using a PE severity index combined with clinical assessment of stability. [PMID 41354154]
- A meta-analysis of catheter-directed thrombectomy without thrombolysis in acute PE supports lowering the threshold to consider it, with randomised trials ongoing in intermediate and high-risk PE. [PMID 39515614]
- ESVM 2025 guidelines give recommendations on indications and management of catheter-based therapy in acute VTE. [PMID 40587333]
- A Spanish position statement on catheter-directed interventions in PE describes periprocedural support and a multidisciplinary approach, noting uncertainty from insufficient evidence. [PMID 39566820]

**Uncertainties**
- Which intermediate-risk patients benefit from catheter therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Troponin in risk stratification.
- Catheter-directed therapy in intermediate-high-risk PE.

**Case hooks**
- Normotensive PE with RV strain and raised troponin.

## Cluster `ph_crisis` (topics 1469) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence addresses inhaled nitric oxide in neonates and cardiac surgery and the framework for acute cor pulmonale. Evidence in adult ICU crisis is limited.

**Teaching points**
- A framework for management of right ventricular failure secondary to acutely increased afterload (acute cor pulmonale) is needed across disciplines, as it has a major impact on prognosis. [PMID 38135288]
- A network meta-analysis of persistent pulmonary hypertension of the newborn found the most effective treatment was MNO + OSID, with OSID + milrinone preferred if iNO is not available. [PMID 38363176]
- In preterm infants receiving rescue iNO, immediate improvement in oxygenation was associated with reduced odds of death before discharge. [PMID 37542786]
- A Bayesian network meta-analysis of pulmonary vasodilators and inodilators in cardiac surgery found no clear mortality improvement, limited by few events and imprecision. [PMID 37652847]
- A meta-analysis of inhaled nitric oxide with cardiopulmonary bypass calls for large multicentre trials of dose and duration. [PMID 38556145]
- A consensus statement describes echocardiography as the gold standard for noninvasive haemodynamic assessment in ICU patients. [PMID 40463753]

**Uncertainties**
- Adult PH crisis management.

**MCQ angles**
- Echocardiography in RV failure.
- iNO in PPHN.

**Case hooks**
- Post-cardiac-surgery patient with acute RV failure and rising PA pressures.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Triggers, inhaled vasodilator choice and RV support in adult ICU crisis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

**Teaching points**

- A review of PLCH advises referring progressive disease to specialist centres and considering pharmacotherapy trials or transplant evaluation. [PMID 28689173]

- A review notes smoking-related interstitial lung disease is increasingly found incidentally on thoracic CT. [PMID 29222007]
- Paired inspiratory and expiratory CT may help differentiate cystic lung diseases. [PMID 39625935]

## Cluster `sarcoidosis` (topics 1475) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Sarcoidosis relapse is common and defined inconsistently. Biologics and targeted therapies have been reviewed, FDG-PET/CT reductions track functional improvement, and steroid-sparing agents are needed for hypercalcaemia.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of FDG-PET/CT in pulmonary sarcoidosis found reduction in SUVmax correlated with improved forced vital capacity, DLCO and subjective health scores. [PMID 39044038]
- A meta-analysis of biologic and targeted synthetic therapies in sarcoidosis reports efficacy signals (tofacitinib in cutaneous disease, efzofitimod in pulmonary disease), limited by small sample sizes and heterogeneity. [PMID 40393718]
- A meta-analysis of relapse in pulmonary sarcoidosis highlights inconsistent definitions and observational designs, with clinical assessment appearing comparable to complex definitions. [PMID 42144146]
- In sarcoidosis-related hypercalcaemia, response to corticosteroids and alternatives was examined, and the most effective steroid-sparing therapy remains to be determined. [PMID 40699493]
- An expert consensus on systemic autoimmune disease-associated ILD includes sarcoidosis among the diseases covered. [PMID 40582403]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal steroid dose and duration.

**MCQ angles**

- Hypercalcaemia in sarcoidosis.

- Patient with bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy and persisting cough on steroids.

## Cluster `tb` (topics 1476) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra detects pulmonary TB with high sensitivity and specificity and rifampicin resistance. Truenat MTB Plus performs well, and survivors of pulmonary TB have mixed obstructive-restrictive lung function loss.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis reports Xpert Ultra for pulmonary TB with summary sensitivity 84.8% and specificity 86.2% in one analysis of 9 studies, and a second review shows it effectively diagnoses TB and rifampicin resistance. [PMID 40728034, 39754613]
- For Truenat MTB Plus, summary sensitivity was 90.6% with high-certainty evidence for pulmonary TB and rifampicin resistance in adults and adolescents. [PMID 40122135]
- Post-pulmonary TB patients have significantly decreased lung function compared with controls, with FEV1 more affected than FVC, a mixed obstructive and restrictive picture with predominant airflow obstruction. [PMID 40412395]
- A review synthesises advances in TB treatment from 2020 to 2026, including novel regimens, new drugs and host-directed therapy. [PMID 42526491]
- A clinical practice guideline for tuberculous meningitis gives evidence-based recommendations for treatment and diagnosis, exposing substantial knowledge gaps. [PMID 40840485]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal short regimens.

**MCQ angles**
- Xpert Ultra detects rifampicin resistance.
- Post-TB lung disease.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with cough, weight loss and a positive Xpert Ultra trace.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Standard four-drug regimen, duration, drug-induced liver injury management and contact tracing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `pvod` (topics 1478) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** PVOD is a rare PAH variant. Vasodilators may help some patients but trial evidence is lacking, and referral for lung transplant assessment matters. Right ventricular dilatation and elevated pressures portend poorer prognosis.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review of vasodilators for PVOD and pulmonary capillary haemangiomatosis found drawing firm conclusions difficult because of the lack of randomised controlled trials. [PMID 30473253]
- A systematic review of targeted therapy in PVOD suggests cautious use could improve or stabilise haemodynamics and exercise capacity in some patients, and patients may live longer than expected. [PMID 31856792]
- A 2025 systematic review of PVOD risk factors, presentation, diagnosis and treatment stresses early diagnosis and referral for transplant consideration, with RV dilatation and elevated pulmonary pressures indicating poorer prognosis. [PMID 39661909]
- The updated clinical classification of pulmonary hypertension places PVOD/PCH features within PAH because evidence suggests a continuum between arterial, capillary and venous involvement. [PMID 30545968]
- A systematic review catalogues drugs associated with pulmonary arterial hypertension. [PMID 29508628]

**Uncertainties**
- Safety of PAH drugs in PVOD.

**MCQ angles**
- PVOD risk of pulmonary oedema with vasodilators (not in abstracts).
- Transplant referral.

**Case hooks**
- Young patient with PAH, low DLCO and septal lines on CT.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** CT findings, genetic causes (EIF2AK4) and pulmonary oedema risk with PAH therapy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `prca` (topics 1479) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cyclosporine A, with or without corticosteroids, appears the single most effective immunosuppressive agent for acquired PRCA. Evidence is low quality, and thymoma-associated disease has distinct recurrence patterns.

**Teaching points**
- A review of pure red cell aplasia states cyclosporine A, with or without corticosteroids, appears the single most effective immunosuppressive agent, with specific pathogenic subtypes linked to specific therapies. [PMID 27881371]
- A systematic review and meta-analysis found substantial uncertainty remains about the best treatment of acquired PRCA absent high-quality evidence. [PMID 37624775]
- A meta-analysis of immunosuppressive therapy for PRCA found efficacy influenced by aetiology, drug combinations and mutations such as STAT3, supporting personalised strategies. [PMID 40105948]
- In thymoma-associated autoimmune PRCA, immunosuppressants, particularly cyclosporine, are effective, but infectious complications must be considered. [PMID 34049719]
- A review of thymoma-related paraneoplastic syndromes found new syndromes after surgery associate with recurrence of the first, and resolution associates with improved overall survival. [PMID 31982129]
- In thymoma-related aplastic anaemia the one-year mortality rate is 29.8%, and allo-HSCT remains preferred for young patients with severe disease and suitable donors. [PMID 41622196]

**Uncertainties**
- Best treatment sequence.

**MCQ angles**
- Thymoma and PRCA.
- Cyclosporine as first-line immunosuppression.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with severe anaemia, reticulocytopenia and an anterior mediastinal mass.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Parvovirus B19, drug causes, bone marrow criteria and thymectomy response are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

- PMID 31982129: A systematic review of paraneoplastic syndromes associated with thymoma: Treatment modalities, recurrence, and outcomes in resected cases (systematic review, 2020)

## Cluster `pyelonephritis` (topics 1480, 1481) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Short-course antibiotic therapy is at least as effective as longer courses for uncomplicated acute pyelonephritis. Emphysematous pyelonephritis needs risk-stratified, often minimally invasive treatment, and male UTI guidance lacks international consensus.

**Teaching points**
- A comparison of systematic reviews and guidelines found short-course antibiotic treatment at least as effective as longer courses for microbiological and clinical success in acute uncomplicated pyelonephritis. [PMID 32566969]
- A systematic review of male UTI guidelines found a lack of international consensus, with fluoroquinolone-sparing and short treatment for afebrile male UTI debated. [PMID 35833228]
- In emphysematous pyelonephritis, risk factors predict patients needing aggressive treatment, while minimally invasive modalities carry lower mortality risk. [PMID 35103928]
- In uncomplicated UTI in adult women, non-antibiotic strategies reduce overall antibiotic use but give poorer clinical outcomes, and tests for erythrocytes and bacteria could target prescribing. [PMID 35788049]
- ACR Appropriateness Criteria for acute pyelonephritis (2022) guide imaging in specific clinical scenarios. [PMID 36436954]
- The ACP best practice advice supports short-course antibiotics in common infections. [PMID 33819054]

**Uncertainties**
- Oral versus intravenous step-down choices are not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Short courses suffice for uncomplicated pyelonephritis.
- Emphysematous pyelonephritis in diabetics.

**Case hooks**
- Woman with fever, flank pain and positive urine culture.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Antibiotic choice by local resistance, admission criteria and imaging triggers are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `pyoderma` (topics 1482) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pyoderma gangrenosum has limited trial evidence: systemic therapy meta-analyses are constrained by heterogeneity, outcome measures beyond complete healing are needed, and registries and trial eligibility criteria are being standardised. Tyrosine kinase inhibitors can cause it.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review and single-arm meta-analysis of systemic therapies for pyoderma gangrenosum found heterogeneous methods and low numbers hamper research, with unmet need for better outcome measures than complete healing. [PMID 40740034]
- Japanese clinical practice guidelines for pyoderma gangrenosum (2022) are available in English. [PMID 37311717]
- A Delphi consensus (UPGRADE) standardised eligibility criteria for active ulcerative pyoderma gangrenosum in clinical trials. [PMID 38110114]
- A multidisciplinary consensus defined a minimum dataset for treatment effectiveness in pyoderma gangrenosum for an international registry. [PMID 41784109]
- A systematic review of case reports describes tyrosine kinase inhibitor-associated pyoderma gangrenosum, urging clinicians to consider drug-induced disease in oncology patients. [PMID 34282745]
- Meta-analyses of inflammatory bowel disease quantify extraintestinal manifestations and vedolizumab effects on them, with some skin-related manifestations exacerbated during vedolizumab. [PMID 37042969, 38740543]

**Uncertainties**
- Best first-line systemic therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Association with IBD.
- Drug-induced pyoderma gangrenosum.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with ulcerative colitis and a rapidly enlarging painful ulcer with violaceous border.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, pathergy and specific steroid or biologic regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `icu_quality` (topics 1483) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ABCDE and ABCDEF care bundles may reduce ICU stay, ventilation time, delirium and mortality, though more rigorous studies are needed. Handover tools lack mortality evidence, and nurses report weakness in error reporting and staffing.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review and meta-analysis found implementation of ABCDE and ABCDEF care bundles may reduce ICU stay, mechanical ventilation time, delirium and ICU and hospital mortality and promote early mobilisation, though more rigorous studies are needed. [PMID 35758388]
- CAM-ICU is recommended as the first-choice tool for delirium assessment in critically ill patients, followed by ICDSC. [PMID 38538305]
- A review of ICU-to-ward handover found there is no evidence that handover tools improve mortality or readmission, though supplementary written or verbal status reports and a liaison nurse may improve safety. [PMID 28452913]
- A meta-analysis of ICU nurses' perceptions found teamwork positive but weaknesses in response to error, event reporting frequency and staffing adequacy. [PMID 40785646]
- An antibiotic care bundle (ABC-Bundle) for the ICU was developed, with low daily adherence of 2% to 37%, showing its potential role in stewardship. [PMID 28705675]
- In situ simulation is proposed as an effective strategy to promote safety culture and teamwork in ICU after COVID-19. [PMID 36673631]

**Uncertainties**
- Which bundle elements drive benefit.

**MCQ angles**
- ABCDEF bundle components.
- CAM-ICU for delirium.

**Case hooks**
- ICU team implementing a daily checklist and delirium screening.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Central line and ventilator-associated pneumonia checklists, and rounding tools, are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `mi_quality` (topics 1484) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Secondary prevention after MI: colchicine lowers cardiovascular events by about 12% in prior coronary disease or stroke, and anti-inflammatory therapy reduces MACE with more gastrointestinal effects. Beta-blockers beyond one event-free year may not add benefit when ejection fraction is preserved.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found colchicine reduced the composite of cardiovascular death, MI or stroke by 12% in patients with prior coronary disease or stroke. [PMID 40314334]
- Long-term trials meta-analysis found colchicine reduced MACE, MI, ischaemic stroke and recurrent revascularisation compared with placebo. [PMID 40314333]
- A network meta-analysis of anti-inflammatory strategies after MI found significant MACE reduction without increased serious adverse events, but more gastrointestinal adverse events. [PMID 39756386]
- A meta-analysis found beta-blockers may not add mortality benefit beyond a 1-year event-free period post-MI without reduced EF, with possible detriment in preserved EF. [PMID 39298680]
- An individual-patient-data meta-analysis supports preferential use of clopidogrel over aspirin for secondary prevention in established coronary artery disease, based on lower risk of events and bleeding. [PMID 40902613]
- The Australian ACS guideline supports tailored LDL-C targets and PCSK9 inhibitor use in secondary prevention. [PMID 41693087]

**Uncertainties**
- Colchicine sub-groups and tolerability.

**MCQ angles**
- Colchicine for secondary prevention.
- Cardiac rehabilitation as a pathway element.

**Case hooks**
- Patient 1 month after STEMI planning long-term secondary prevention.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Door-to-balloon targets, performance measures and dual antiplatelet duration are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39298680: Beta-blockers for secondary prevention following myocardial infarction in patients without reduced ejection fraction or heart failure: an updated meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39756386: A pairwise and network meta-analysis of anti-inflammatory strategies after myocardial infarction: the TITIAN study (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `ra_cvd` (topics 1485) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cardiovascular risk is increased across inflammatory joint disease: RA carries a relative risk of about 1.55 for cardiovascular events. EULAR recommends CV risk management, and drug choice influences risk, with JAK inhibitors needing post-marketing data.

**Teaching points**
- The EULAR 2017 update on cardiovascular risk management in RA and other inflammatory joint disorders defines ten recommendations, underscoring that CVD risk is increased across the whole spectrum of inflammatory joint disease. [PMID 27697765]
- A meta-analysis found the relative risk of cardiovascular events in RA was 1.55 (95% CI 1.18-2.02). [PMID 34454117]
- A comparative meta-analysis suggested tocilizumab may be associated with reduced MACE risk versus TNF inhibitors, while csDMARDs may be associated with increased MACE and stroke risk. [PMID 30875456]
- Randomised trials showed no significant short-term change in cardiovascular risk with JAK inhibitors in RA, but postmarketing data are needed, especially for higher doses with thromboembolism risk for tofacitinib and baricitinib. [PMID 31088790]
- Hydroxychloroquine may benefit the metabolic profile in RA, with lower diabetes incidence among ever-users (HR 0.59) and possibly fewer cardiovascular events. [PMID 28970215]
- Italian recommendations advise more stringent CV risk assessment in RA patients needing NSAIDs or with hypertension, dyslipidaemia or diabetes, intima-media thickness measurement for moderate risk and cardiological evaluation for high risk. [PMID 36912349]
- A collaboration between rheumatology and general practice raised CVD risk screening in RA patients to 88% after reminder letters. [PMID 29167154]

**Uncertainties**
- Best risk calculator in RA.

**MCQ angles**
- Increased CV risk in RA.
- JAK inhibitor cardiovascular safety.

**Case hooks**
- Woman with long-standing RA and hypertension asking about cardiovascular risk.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** EULAR risk multiplier (1.5) and statin thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27697765: EULAR recommendations for cardiovascular disease risk management in patients with rheumatoid arthritis and other forms of inflammatory joint disorders: 2015/2016 update (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 28970215: Metabolic and cardiovascular benefits of hydroxychloroquine in patients with rheumatoid arthritis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)

## Cluster `rabies` (topics 1486) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Timely post-exposure prophylaxis is crucial: rabies despite prophylaxis is rare. Monoclonal antibodies can replace immunoglobulin in some settings, and older individuals mount lower antibody responses.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review of fatal breakthrough infections concluded prompt PEP administration is crucial, and that rabies despite adherence to core practices, even in high-risk or immunosuppressed people, remains exceedingly rare. [PMID 36535276]
- Current US guidelines address rabies pre-exposure prophylaxis and post-exposure prophylaxis regimens. [PMID 32752569]
- The Indian Academy of Pediatrics endorses WHO 2018 post-exposure prophylaxis, and monoclonal rabies antibody can be given as an alternative to rabies immunoglobulin. [PMID 30745480]
- An expert consensus standardises clinical use of ormutivimab for rabies post-exposure prophylaxis in China. [PMID 37427985]
- A dose-response meta-analysis of rabies vaccination suggests schedule choice should consider patient needs, cost and ability to return, and that older individuals develop lower antibody responses. [PMID 33478786]
- A Southeast Asia review identifies rural unavailability of vaccine and misinformation about seeking treatment after dog bites as drivers of rabies mortality. [PMID 37164462]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal abbreviated schedules.

**MCQ angles**
- PEP after animal exposure includes wound washing, vaccine and immunoglobulin.
- Immunosuppression reduces response.

**Case hooks**
- Traveller bitten by a stray dog abroad.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Wound care steps, exact vaccine schedules and category of exposure are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30745480: Indian Academy of Pediatrics (IAP) Advisory Committee on Vaccines and Immunization Practices (ACVIP) Recommended Immunization Schedule (2018-19) and Update on Immunization for Children Aged 0 Through 18 Years (guideline, 2018)

## Cluster `radial_access` (topics 1487) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Radial access reduces bleeding, vascular complications and mortality compared with femoral access in coronary interventions, with comparable stroke and MI. Distal radial and ulnar sites are viable secondary options.

**Teaching points**
- An updated meta-analysis of randomised trials found radial access associated with significant reductions in bleeding, vascular complications and mortality versus femoral access, with comparable stroke and MI. [PMID 33507598]
- A network meta-analysis of access sites for coronary angiography supports distal radial and ulnar access as a default secondary access site before femoral access. [PMID 39027936]
- A SCAI consensus update covers best practices for transradial intervention, including ulnar access, collateral flow testing, strategies to prevent radial artery occlusion and radial access for primary PCI. [PMID 31880380]
- A review of distal radial access gives practical recommendations on technique, pitfalls and complications. [PMID 40656779]
- An AHA scientific statement sees promise in radial access for peripheral vascular interventions as technology advances. [PMID 39629587]
- In NSTEMI PCI, bivalirudin reduced serious bleeding versus heparin from access and non-access sites without different rates of mortality or ischaemic events. [PMID 37746717]

**Uncertainties**
- Role in unstable shock patients.

**MCQ angles**
- Radial access lowers bleeding and mortality.
- Radial artery occlusion prevention.

- Patient with STEMI on anticoagulation: choice of access site.

## Cluster `rihd` (topics 1488) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Radiation-induced heart disease arises from endothelial injury, inflammation and oxidative stress, producing vascular and cardiac disease. Evidence for imaging, screening and prevention is mostly review-level.

**Teaching points**
- A review classifies radiation-induced heart disease and links it to endothelial injury, inflammatory response and oxidative stress, urging prevention. [PMID 31592122]
- A practical review provides clinical and imaging manifestations, multimodality screening recommendations and management options for radiation-associated cardiac disease. [PMID 30092970]
- A state-of-the-art review focuses on radiation-induced vascular disease, covering mechanisms, manifestations, prevention, screening, diagnosis and management. [PMID 33860001]
- A systematic review of nuclear imaging for early radiation-induced cardiotoxicity calls for larger populations, longer follow-up and heart dosimetric data. [PMID 37876964]
- A review of oxidative stress in radiotherapy cardiotoxicity summarises prevention and treatment methods. [PMID 32190171]
- A bibliometric analysis stresses prevention, consistent monitoring, prompt diagnosis and timely treatment to decrease RIHD in cancer survivors. [PMID 38655134]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal screening interval.

**MCQ angles**
- Radiation causes coronary, valvular, pericardial and myocardial disease.
- Latency of RIHD.

**Case hooks**
- Survivor of mediastinal radiotherapy with new exertional angina.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Dose thresholds, latency figures and surveillance intervals are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30092970: Radiation-Associated Cardiac Disease: A Practical Approach to Diagnosis and Management (review, 2018)
- PMID 31592122: Radiation-induced heart disease: a review of classification, mechanism and prevention (review, 2019)
- PMID 32190171: Oxidative Stress in Radiation-Induced Cardiotoxicity (review, 2020)
- PMID 33860001: Radiation-Induced Vascular Disease-A State-of-the-Art Review (review, 2021)
- PMID 37876964: Nuclear medicine imaging methods of early radiation-induced cardiotoxicity: a ten-year systematic review (systematic review, 2023)

## Cluster `radionecrosis` (topics 1489) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Amino acid PET, FDG-PET, SPECT and MRS help distinguish radiation necrosis from recurrence and are considered more useful than standard MRI. Treatment includes observation, steroids, bevacizumab, LITT and surgery, with low-quality evidence.

**Teaching points**
- A diagnostic meta-analysis ranked SPECT and MRS as more useful than standard MRI for distinguishing radiation necrosis from tumour recurrence. [PMID 36853489]
- A meta-analysis found amino acid PET and FDG-PET had good diagnostic accuracy in differentiating brain metastasis recurrence from radionecrosis, though 60% of studies were retrospective. [PMID 29242363]
- A systematic review of radiological diagnosis of brain radiation necrosis found combining imaging studies improves accuracy, but risk of bias prevents firm conclusions on the best technique. [PMID 30728041]
- A systematic review informing management of symptomatic radionecrosis after stereotactic radiosurgery found low-quality evidence across treatment modalities and needs head-to-head comparisons. [PMID 37482137]
- The DEGRO guidelines propose a multistep diagnostic approach and detailed treatment strategies including observation, surgery, steroids and bevacizumab, discussed in an interdisciplinary setting. [PMID 36038669, 36038670]
- Meta-analyses of bevacizumab found radiographic response and clinical improvement without serious adverse events, and outcomes mostly comparable to laser interstitial thermal therapy, with LITT possibly better for survival in selected patients. [PMID 33593308, 34218396]

**Uncertainties**
- Which patients benefit most from bevacizumab versus LITT.

**MCQ angles**
- Perfusion MRI and PET to differentiate necrosis from progression.
- Bevacizumab for symptomatic radionecrosis.

**Case hooks**
- Patient 8 months after radiosurgery with an enlarging enhancing lesion and oedema.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Perfusion MRI metrics and steroid dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29242363: Diagnostic Accuracy of Amino Acid and FDG-PET in Differentiating Brain Metastasis Recurrence from Radionecrosis after Radiotherapy: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 30728041: Radiological diagnosis of brain radiation necrosis after cranial irradiation for brain tumor: a systematic review (systematic review, 2019)
- PMID 33593308: Bevacizumab for radiation necrosis following radiotherapy of brain metastatic disease: a systematic review & meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34218396: Bevacizumab vs laser interstitial thermal therapy in cerebral radiation necrosis from brain metastases: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 36038669: DEGRO practical guideline for central nervous system radiation necrosis part 1: classification and a multistep approach for diagnosis (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36038670: DEGRO practical guideline for central nervous system radiation necrosis part 2: treatment (guideline, 2022)

## Cluster `rad_pneumonitis` (topics 1490) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Radiation pneumonitis is a diagnosis of exclusion, with ESTRO and Delphi consensus guidance on diagnosis and management. Combining radiotherapy with immune checkpoint inhibitors, and pre-existing interstitial lung abnormalities, raise risk.

**Teaching points**
- The ESTRO guideline states radiation pneumonitis remains a diagnosis of exclusion, proposes a diagnostic algorithm and outlines practical treatment approaches. [PMID 40185160]
- An international Delphi consensus gives practical guidance on diagnosis and management of radiation pneumonitis, emphasising risk stratification, especially in interstitial lung disease. [PMID 38788551]
- A meta-analysis examined pneumonitis incidence with radiotherapy combined with immune checkpoint inhibitors in lung cancer, with significant heterogeneity requiring prospective validation. [PMID 40313247]
- Interstitial lung abnormalities are associated with increased mortality, lung cancer risk and cancer treatment-related complications. [PMID 38538680]
- A radiomics-based hybrid model for predicting radiation pneumonitis may identify higher-risk patients. [PMID 38906047]
- A meta-analysis of functional lung imaging in radiotherapy for lung cancer supports its use in planning and delivery, with heterogeneity in approaches. [PMID 30082143]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal steroid regimens.

**MCQ angles**
- Radiation pneumonitis as exclusion diagnosis.
- Immunotherapy increases risk.

**Case hooks**
- Patient 8 weeks after chest radiotherapy with dry cough and dyspnoea.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Time course, grading and steroid dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30082143: Functional lung imaging in radiation therapy for lung cancer: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 38538680: Clinical outcomes of interstitial lung abnormalities: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38788551: Optimal management of radiation pneumonitis: Findings of an international Delphi consensus study (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38906047: Radiomics-based hybrid model for predicting radiation pneumonitis: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40185160: Diagnosis and treatment of radiation induced pneumonitis in patients with lung cancer: An ESTRO clinical practice guideline (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40313247: Incidences of pneumonitis associated with the combination of radiotherapy and immune checkpoint inhibitors in lung cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `radiculopathy` (topics 1491) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Epidural steroid injections give short- to medium-term relief of lumbar radiculopathy and sciatica; platelet-rich plasma showed comparable benefit in one meta-analysis. Guidelines advise clinical assessment with restricted imaging.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found epidural steroid injection relieves sciatica from lumbar disc herniation in the short to medium term and is a viable option. [PMID 38841695]
- A comparative systematic review of placebo-controlled and active-controlled trials rated epidural injections for lumbar radiculopathy as Level I evidence at 1 and 3 months and Level II at 6 and 12 months. [PMID 36288577]
- A meta-analysis found epidural platelet-rich plasma offers benefit comparable to epidural steroid with a similar safety profile for radiculopathy due to lumbar disc disease. [PMID 39968415]
- A Saudi guideline advises that assessment of low back pain and sciatica be guided by clinical assessment, with restricted imaging and careful selection of pharmacological, psychological, multidisciplinary and procedural interventions. [PMID 41598467]
- A Chinese medicine guideline for cervical radiculopathy gave strong recommendations for acupuncture and manual therapy. [PMID 41376234]
- An image-guided epidural steroid injection practice parameter addresses indications, contraindications and technique to reduce serious neurologic complications. [PMID 42235968]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term benefit beyond 12 months.

**MCQ angles**
- Epidural steroid for short-term sciatica relief.
- Imaging only when red flags exist.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with L5 radiculopathy and disc herniation after 6 weeks of conservative care.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Red-flag criteria, indications for surgery and cervical radiculopathy specifics are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36288577: Comparative Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Cochrane Review of Epidural Injections for Lumbar Radiculopathy or Sciatica (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 38841695: Efficacy of epidural steroid injection in the treatment of sciatica secondary to lumbar disc herniation: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39968415: Is platelet-rich plasma better than steroids as epidural drug of choice in lumbar disc disease with radiculopathy? Meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `rpgn` (topics 1493) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** RPGN is mainly ANCA vasculitis, anti-GBM disease or immune-complex disease. Plasma exchange lowers 12-month ESKD risk in ANCA vasculitis but raises serious infection risk, and cyclophosphamide or rituximab remain induction options.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of plasma exchange in ANCA-associated vasculitis found it reduces the 12-month risk of ESKD but increases the risk of serious infections. [PMID 35217545]
- The KDIGO update advises cyclophosphamide and rituximab or combinations to induce remission in patients with severe kidney involvement in ANCA vasculitis, with considerations for plasma exchange. [PMID 41151615]
- The EULAR 2022 update on ANCA-associated vasculitis management notes substantial data gaps remain and informed physician-patient decision-making matters. [PMID 36927642]
- A review of treatment advances in ANCA vasculitis notes the FDA proposed in April 2026 to withdraw approval for avacopan for lack of substantial evidence of effectiveness. [PMID 41817218]
- A systematic review of anti-GBM disease covers epidemiology, clinical features, risk factors and outcomes, with caution over observational heterogeneity. [PMID 38493958]
- A systematic review suggests plasma exchange may benefit some patients with Henoch-Schönlein purpura nephritis and IgA nephropathy presenting with RPGN. [PMID 36835388]

**Uncertainties**
- Place of avacopan.

**MCQ angles**
- Plasma exchange: ESKD reduction versus infection risk.
- Crescentic glomerulonephritis causes.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with haematuria, rapidly rising creatinine and positive ANCA.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Crescent biopsy findings, anti-GBM treatment and steroid regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35217545: The effects of plasma exchange in patients with ANCA-associated vasculitis: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36835388: Efficacy and Safety of Plasma Exchange as an Adjunctive Therapy for Rapidly Progressive IgA Nephropathy and Henoch-Schönlein Purpura Nephritis: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2023)

## Cluster `af_rate_rhythm` (topics 1494) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Recent guidelines favour early rhythm control and catheter ablation, especially for paroxysmal AF or heart failure, alongside anticoagulation and risk-factor modification. Guidance for older patients on rhythm control and ablation is limited.

**Teaching points**
- A clinical practice guideline states available evidence supports evaluating indications for oral anticoagulation, early rhythm control and catheter ablation, especially in paroxysmal AF or heart failure. [PMID 40526576]
- A new AF guideline commentary clarifies which patients should be considered for catheter ablation and delves into thrombosis risk calculations when deciding on anticoagulation. [PMID 40312119]
- An analysis of the 2024 ESC guideline in older patients found recommendations on rhythm control and catheter ablation lack specific advice for older people. [PMID 41212796]
- A meta-analysis found GLP-1 receptor agonist therapy was associated with reduced AF recurrence after ablation, with cautious interpretation needed. [PMID 42033283]
- A guideline on heart failure with AF discusses rate versus rhythm control separately for HFrEF and HFpEF and gives subtype-specific recommendations. [PMID 42170354]
- The 2026 ACC scientific statement covers direct oral anticoagulants in primary and secondary prevention of thrombotic events. [PMID 42377292]

**Uncertainties**
- Rhythm control in older and frail patients.

**MCQ angles**
- Early rhythm control and ablation.
- Anticoagulation by thrombosis risk.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with paroxysmal AF and heart failure considering ablation.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** EAST-AFNET 4, CASTLE-AF and rate-control targets are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `ra_t2t` (topics 1495) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Treat-to-target in RA pursues low disease activity with stepwise DMARD escalation. The best target remains uncertain, and JAK inhibitor safety (herpes zoster) needs monitoring.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review and meta-regression asking which target is best in RA treat-to-target found direct comprehensive comparisons of targets are still needed because of residual confounding and lack of safety and overtreatment data. [PMID 37116986]
- South African guidelines advise continuing DMARD therapy in a stepwise algorithm until low disease activity is achieved, with screening for comorbidities and vaccination. [PMID 39513243]
- Pan American League recommendations provide a practical, evidence-based framework for managing RA in Latin America. [PMID 41240967]
- A meta-analysis of upadacitinib in immune-mediated inflammatory diseases found herpes zoster needs caution, with thorough assessment, monitoring and individualised dosing vital. [PMID 40666511]
- Glucocorticoid therapy is a major risk factor for osteoporosis in RA, and higher BMI is protective. [PMID 41188646]

**Uncertainties**
- Which composite index to target.

**MCQ angles**
- Treat-to-target with escalation until low disease activity.
- JAK inhibitor and zoster risk.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with RA and moderate disease activity on methotrexate.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** DAS28, SDAI and CDAI remission cut-offs are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37116986: What is the best target in a treat-to-target strategy in rheumatoid arthritis? Results from a systematic review and meta-regression analysis (systematic review, 2023)

## Cluster `raynaud` (topics 1496, 1497) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Evidence for Raynaud's phenomenon and digital ulcers in systemic sclerosis is limited: non-pharmacological measures have modest effects, EULAR 2023 and Portuguese recommendations guide drug choice, and botulinum toxin appears a safe adjunct for refractory digital ischaemia.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review supporting Portuguese recommendations found non-pharmacological interventions have only modest effects and that high-quality evidence for the optimal treatment is limited. [PMID 38306796]
- Portuguese recommendations give comprehensive, personalised management of Raynaud's phenomenon and digital ulcers in systemic sclerosis and other connective tissue diseases, with a research agenda on non-pharmacologic interventions. [PMID 38956991]
- A meta-analysis found botulinum toxin injections appear a safe and effective adjunct for refractory digital ischaemia and ulcers in systemic sclerosis, needing prospective trials. [PMID 41369954]
- EULAR 2023 recommendations for systemic sclerosis include first-line combination therapy for newly diagnosed pulmonary arterial hypertension and therapies for fibrotic manifestations. [PMID 39874231]
- A meta-analysis suggests vascular endothelial growth factor as a potential biomarker in systemic sclerosis. [PMID 39669565]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal drug for digital ulcer prevention.

**MCQ angles**
- Primary versus secondary Raynaud.
- Botulinum toxin for refractory digital ischaemia.

**Case hooks**
- Woman with cold-induced colour changes and a new fingertip ulcer.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Calcium channel blockers, PDE5 inhibitors, bosentan and capillaroscopy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38306796: Systematic literature review to inform the Portuguese recommendations for the management of Raynaud's phenomenon and digital ulcers in systemic sclerosis and other connective tissue diseases (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `reactive_arthritis` (topics 1499, 1500) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Reactive arthritis follows genitourinary or enteric infection; only a small percentage of Campylobacter, Salmonella, Shigella or Yersinia infections lead to it. BASHH guidance covers sexually acquired reactive arthritis.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found the percentage of people developing reactive arthritis after Campylobacter, Salmonella, Shigella or Yersinia infection is relatively low (2.6%). [PMID 34255548]
- A 2024 meta-analysis found a significant proportion of Salmonella, Shigella and Yersinia cases resulted in reactive arthritis, with substantial heterogeneity between studies. [PMID 38929962]
- The BASHH national guideline gives management of sexually acquired reactive arthritis within UK sexual health services. [PMID 34014782]
- Updated estimates of sequelae after Campylobacter infection were provided, including for Crohn's disease and ulcerative colitis for the first time, with broad uncertainty. [PMID 40496789]
- A South African peripheral spondyloarthritis guideline advises a multidisciplinary team, treat-to-target escalation and screening for comorbidities. [PMID 39508216]
- A systematic review of intravesical BCG found autoimmune disorders, including articular and ocular groups, as complications. [PMID 37061015]

**Uncertainties**
- Efficacy of prolonged antibiotics is not covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Reactive arthritis after enteric or chlamydial infection.
- Management follows treat-to-target principles.

**Case hooks**
- Young man with urethritis, conjunctivitis and asymmetric oligoarthritis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** HLA-B27 role, triad definition, NSAID and DMARD treatment, and antibiotic evidence are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 34014782: British Association of Sexual Health and HIV national guideline on the management of sexually acquired reactive arthritis 2021 (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34255548: Enteric Pathogens and Reactive Arthritis: Systematic Review and Meta-Analyses of Pathogen-Associated Reactive Arthritis (meta-analysis, 2021)
